The Tangle Hypothesis: Dimension 1 Thanks: DA was supported by the National Science Foundation under awards 1812055 and 1945639. JF was supported by the National Science Foundation under award 1812057. This material is based upon work supported by the National Science Foundation under Grant No. DMS-1440140, while the authors were in residence at the Mathematical Sciences Research Institute in Berkeley, California, during the Spring 2020 semester.
David Ayala & John Francis

Abstract
. We introduce an ( ∞ , 1 ) (\oo,1) -category 𝖡𝗈𝗋𝖽 1 𝖿𝗋 ​ ( ℝ n ) {\sf Bord}_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}) , the morphisms in which are framed tangles in ℝ n × 𝔻 1 \mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1} . We prove that 𝖡𝗈𝗋𝖽 1 𝖿𝗋 ​ ( ℝ n ) {\sf Bord}_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}) has the universal mapping out property of the 1-dimensional Tangle Hypothesis of Baez–Dolan and Hopkins–Lurie: it is the rigid ℰ n \mathcal{E}_{n} -monoidal ( ∞ , 1 ) (\oo,1) -category freely generated by a single object. Applying this theorem to a dualizable object of a braided monoidal ( ∞ , 1 ) (\oo,1) -category gives link invariants, generalizing the Reshetikhin–Turaev invariants.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 57R56. Secondary 57R90, 18B30, 18D10.

Introduction

The construction of knot invariants from representations of braid groups dates to at least to the 1980s and the Jones polynomial: Vaughan Jones’s original work used braid group representations of Hecke algebra and von Neumann representations
[
Jon1
]
and
[
Jon2
]
to define polynomial link invariants. Building on these ideas, Turaev abstracted the role of Yang–Baxter equations
[
Tu
]
. Then, in 1990, Reshetikhin and Turaev
[
RT
]
constructed an invariant of framed links in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
given a finite-dimensional representation of a quantum group. This construction works even more generally, as in the work of Freyd–Yetter
[
FY
]
and Shum
[
Sh
]
, so as to construct from a dualizable object
V
in a braided monoidal category
C
a function

\textstyle{\Bigl\{{\rm framed\ links}\Bigr\}_{\big/{\rm istpy}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Z_{V}}
\textstyle{\Hom_{C}(\uno,\uno)}

from the set of framed isotopy classes of links to the set of endomorphisms of the unit of the monoidal structure of
C
. In the case where
C
is representations of the quantum group
U_{\hbar}({\sf sl}_{2}(\mathbb{C}))
, this invariant specializes to the 2-variable Jones–Conway polynomial, after reparametrization.

The 1-dimensional Tangle Hypothesis is a topological enhancement of this theorem of Shum, first put forward by Baez–Dolan
[
BaDo
]
, and given a rigorous
(\oo,1)
-categorically formulation by Hopkins–Lurie
[
Lu3
]
. In this formulation, the set of framed isotopy classes is the set of components of a topological moduli space of framed tangles
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, which has the homotopy type

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\simeq\coprod_{k}\Emb^{\fr}\Bigl(\underset{k}{\amalg}S^{1},\mathbb{R}^{3}\Bigr)_{/\Diff\bigl(\underset{k}{\amalg}S^{1}\bigr)}

as in Definition
1.15
. This space of framed tangles has a very interesting topology: See
[
Bu
]
,
[
Si1
]
,
[
Si2
]
,
[
Va
]
,
[
Hat2
]
, and
[
Ko
]
, among many other works. One can then ask for a refinement of Reshetikhin–Turaev’s function: Given a dualizable object
V
of a braided monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}
, one could ask for a map of spaces

\textstyle{\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Z_{V}}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathcal{R}}(\uno,\uno)~.}

For
\mathcal{R}
enriched in chain complexes, such an assignment is equivalent to a morphism of chain complexes

\textstyle{\mathsf{C}_{\ast}\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Z_{V}}
\textstyle{\underline{\End}_{\mathcal{R}}(\uno)}

where
\underline{\End}_{\mathcal{R}}(\uno)
is the chain complex of endomorphisms of the unit of
\mathcal{R}
. Dualizing would give a map

\textstyle{\underline{\End}_{\mathcal{R}}(\uno)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{C}^{\ast}\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\bigr)~.}

and thus a characteristic class
\sigma_{v}\in\mathsf{H}^{k}\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\bigr)
for tangles for each class in the homology of the dual,
v\in\mathsf{H}_{-k}\bigl(\underline{\End}_{\mathcal{R}}(\uno)^{\vee}\bigr)
.

The Tangle Hypothesis in ambient dimension 3 asserts that such a construction indeed exists and that, further, if one incorporates compatibility with boundary conditions on links, then this construction is essentially unique for each choice of
V\in\mathcal{R}
.

In ambient dimension
n>3
, any two links with the same number of connected components are isotopic.
However, the topology of each connected component of
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is extremely nontrivial: See
[
LTV
]
.
Through considerations similar to those above, the Tangle Hypothesis in ambient dimension
n
supplies a map

\mathsf{H}_{-k}\bigl(\underline{\End}_{\mathcal{R}}(\uno)^{\vee}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{k}\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\bigr)

for each object
V\in\mathcal{R}
in a rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category.

The following is the main result of this paper, the Tangle Hypothesis for 1-dimensional tangles in arbitrary ambient dimension. This result reduces to the identification of the free rigid braided monoidal
(1,1)
-category given by Freyd–Yetter
[
FY
]
and Shum
[
Sh
]
in the case where the target
\mathcal{R}
is a
(1,1)
-category.

Theorem 0.1

(Tangle Hypothesis)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, with
n\geq 2
. Let
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
be the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category of framed tangles of Definition
2.20
.
Evaluation at the object
\ast\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
defines an equivalence between spaces

\ev_{\ast}\colon\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Obj(\mathcal{R})~,\qquad Z\longmapsto Z(\ast)~.

between the space of objects of
\mathcal{R}
and the space of
\mathcal{R}
-valued
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal functors on
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
. Consequently, the univalent-completion

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}

is the rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category freely generated by a single object.

Taking the limit
n\mapsto\oo
gives the 1-dimensional Cobordism Hypothesis, proved by Hopkins–Lurie
[
Lu3
]
.

Corollary 0.2

(1-dimensional Cobordism Hypothesis)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a rigid symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, and let

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}:=\varinjlim\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})

be the 1-dimensional cobordism category.
Evaluation at the object
\ast\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
defines an equivalence between spaces

\ev_{\ast}\colon\Map_{\Alg_{\sf Com}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr},\mathcal{R}\bigr)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Obj(\mathcal{R})~,\qquad Z\longmapsto Z(\ast)~.

between the space of objects of
\mathcal{R}
and the space of
\mathcal{R}
-valued symmetric monoidal functors on
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
.

As a consequence of the Tangle Hypothesis, we calculate the classifying spaces of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
and
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
.

Corollary 0.3
.

For each
n\geq 2
, there is a canonical identification between
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-spaces:

\mathsf{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})~\simeq~\Omega^{\n 1}S^{\n 1}~.

In the limit
n\mapsto\oo
, there is a canonical identification between
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}
-spaces:

\mathsf{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}~\simeq~\Omega^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}S^{0}~.

Remark 0.4
.

The flagged
(\oo,1)
-categories
\Bord^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
exhibit special behavior in those dimensions in which unknots in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[1]
can be straightened up to a contractible choice. For
\Bord^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
, this is Proposition
2.24
. For
\Bord^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, this is a consequence of a theorem of Hatcher
[
Hat1
]
. In higher dimensions, however, the space of long knots has a complicated homotopy type: The space of objects of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is not equivalent to the space of isomorphisms (i.e., the space of framed h-cobordism tangles, or long unknots), and so the functor to the univalent-completion
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\rightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
is not an equivalence. (This is similar to, but distinct from, the phenomenon in which
\Bord_{k}^{\fr}
is not univalent-complete for
k>3
. This is due to the existence of nontrivial h-cobordisms.) As
n
tends to
\oo
, however, the space of long unknots becomes contractible, and for
n=\oo
then
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}\rightarrow(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
is an equivalence as in the cases of
n=2
and
n=3
.

The interesting topology of spaces of long knots in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
for
3<n<\oo
leads to the following question:

Question 0.5
.

Is there a direct construction of the
(\oo,1)
-categories
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
, for
3<n<\oo
? That is, can one construct
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
without first constructing some other flagged
(\oo,1)
-category (namely,
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
) and then taking its univalent-completion?

Outline of the proof

Let
\Free_{k}^{\sf rig}(\mathcal{K})
be the rigid
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category freely generated by an
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{K}
. The functor

\Free_{n}^{\sf rig}:\Cat_{(\oo,1)}\rightarrow\Alg_{n}^{\sf rig}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})

is equivalent to

\Free_{n}^{\sf rig}\simeq\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Free_{\n 1}^{\sf rig}

where
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}:\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\rightarrow\Alg_{n}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})
is the left adjoint to the functor defined by restriction along
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{n}
. The 1-dimensional Tangle Hypothesis is equivalent to the assertion
\Free^{\sf rig}_{n}(\ast)\simeq\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
. Our proof divides into two subsidiary assertions:

(1)

Base Case:
\Free^{\sf rig}_{1}(\ast)\simeq\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
; and

(2)

Inductive Case:
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\Bigr)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}\simeq\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
.

To establish both the Base Case and the Inductive Case, we use the following technique:

•

Let us say we want to evaluate a functor
\mathcal{F}
on an
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{C}
, where the output
\mathcal{F}
is again an
(\oo,1)
-category. Let
\mathcal{C}_{\bullet}
denote the underlying simplicial space of
\mathcal{C}
. There is a canonical functor

\Bigl(\Seg\bigl(\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{C}_{\bullet})\bigr)\Bigr)_{\sf unv}^{\wedge}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{C})

where

–

\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{C}_{\bullet})
is the simplicial object obtained by evaluating
\mathcal{F}
on each
\mathcal{C}_{p}
, regarded as an
\oo
-groupoid;

–

\Seg
is an endofunctor on simplicial objects, the output of which often satisfies the Segal condition;

–

(-)_{\sf unv}^{\wedge}
is univalent-completion, which converts a Segal space into a univalent-complete Segal space (i.e., an
(\oo,1)
-category).

We give a formula for this
\Seg
functor in Definition
3.25
, and prove in Lemma
3.32
that this canonical functor is an equivalence so long as the output indeed satisfies the Segal condition (which it will not in general).

To establish the base case, we start with a description of the free monoidal category generated by a single object with a right-dual, in Proposition
5.11
. Using our
\Seg
functor, we then analyze the pushouts which define the process of adding left-duals and right-duals, resulting in Lemma
5.17
.

To establish the inductive step, we give a topological model for induction from
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebras to
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebras, in Lemma
6.15
. We then apply the same strategy of Segaling and univalent-completing to analyze the induction functor from
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-categories to
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-categories via the composite of steps in the following diagram:

\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\scriptstyle{\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{n}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Step\ (1)}
\textstyle{\Alg_{n}(\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Step\ (2)}
\textstyle{\Alg_{n}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Step\ (3)}

The proof is then completed by composing these three steps:

(1)

In Corollary
7.6
, we show

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]\bigr)\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr,\pitchfork[p]}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])^{\sf s.pr.emb}~,

the induction on the space of framed tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is the space of slice-wise projection-embedding framed tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
, i.e., the tangles which can be sliced in such a way that they then project to embeddings in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
.

(2)

In Lemma
7.11
, we show

\Seg\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}\Bigr)[p]\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}^{\sf pr.imm}~,

the Segaling of the simplicial space of slice-wise projection-embedding framed tangles is the space of projection-immersion framed tangles, i.e., the tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
which project to immersions in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
.

(3)

In Lemma
7.15
, we show

\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}\Bigr)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}\simeq\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\Bigr)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}~,

the univalent-completion of the Segal space of projection-immersion framed tangles is the
(\oo,1)
-category of framed tangles.

Remark 0.6
.

These steps give a parametrized generalization of the familiar assertion that a framed knot or links admits a presentation in terms of braids. Combining these steps, there is an
(\oo,1)
-category which presents the space of framed links in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
: The classifying space of this
(\oo,1)
-category is equivalent to the space of framed links in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and an object of this
(\oo,1)
-category is a presentation of a framed link in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
as a stack of concatenated framed tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
. (Here, the stacking is the
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-monoidal structure of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and the concatenation is the composition of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.) Applying this in the case
n=3
, we find that the space of framed links in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
is a classifying space of an
(\oo,1)
-category whose objects are presentations and whose morphisms are relations.

Linear overview of the paper

We now give a sectional overview of the contents of this work.

Section 1
defines the moduli space of framed tangles.

Section 2
defines the flagged
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
, and shows that its space of morphisms are framed tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
. It defines the
(\oo,1)
-category
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
and shows that cosheaves on this category define rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories after restricting along a functor
\rho
and univalent-completion.

Section 3
constructs a functor
{\sf Seg}
on simplicial spaces. In opportune situations,
\Seg
agrees with the left adjoint of the inclusion of Segal spaces into simplicial spaces.

Section 4
shows that the twisted-arrow category of a poset of intervals localizes onto the twisted-arrow category of the simplex category.

Section 5
proves the 1-dimensional Tangle Hypothesis in ambient dimension 2:
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is the rigid monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category freely generated by a single object.

Section 6
establishes a topological model for induction from
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebras to
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebras.

Section 7
proves that
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is the
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category obtained by applying the induction functor
\Ind_{1}^{n}:\Alg(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\rightarrow\Alg_{n}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})
to
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
.

Section 8
establishes a relative h-principle for embedding-immersions, used to evaluate the univalent-completion in Section 7.

Section 9
proves the 1-dimensional Cobordism Hypothesis as the
n\mapsto\oo
limit of the Tangle Hypothesis.

Section 10
calculates the classifying spaces of the cobordism and tangle categories.

0.1.
Notation

•

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category whose objects are
n
-framed points in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
and whose morphisms are framed tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[1]
.

•

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})_{\sf unv}^{\wedge}
is the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category which is the univalent-completion of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
.

•

For
p\geq 1
,
\mathbb{D}[p]=(\{0,\ldots,p\}\subset[0,p])
is the 1-disk stratified with
p-1
points in the interior.

•

\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(M)
is the space of framed 1-tangles in
M
.
\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])
is the space of framed 1-tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
which are transverse to
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\{0,\ldots,p\}
. See §
1.2
.

•

\Strat
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of stratified spaces, whose morphisms are stratum-preserving maps. See
[
AFT1
]
.

•

\Bun
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of constructible bundles. See §
2.1
.

•

\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of solidly
n
-framed stratified spaces. See §
2.1
.

•

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
is a full
\oo
-subcategory of
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
of stratified spaces whose strata are diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
or
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. See §
2.1
.

•

\Disk^{\fr}_{k}
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of finite disjoint unions of framed
k
-dimensional Euclidean spaces, whose morphisms are framed embeddings. See §
6.1
.

•

The
(\oo,1)
-category of spaces, or
\infty
-groupoids, or
(\infty,0)
-categories, is

\Spaces~=:~{\sf Gpds}_{\infty}~=:~\Cat_{(\infty,0)}~.

•

\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of small
(\oo,1)
-categories.

•

\fCat_{(\oo,1)}
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of small flagged
(\oo,1)
-categories, which is equivalent to the
(\oo,1)
-category
\Fun^{\Seg}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces)
of Segal presheaves on
\mathbf{\Delta}
. See also
[
AF3
]
.

•

\TwAr(\mathcal{K})
is the twisted-arrow
(\oo,1)
-category of
\mathcal{K}
. It is the unstraightening of the functor
\Map:\mathcal{K}^{\op}\times\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\Spaces
. Limits indexed by
\TwAr(\mathcal{K})
are ends.

•

\TwAro(\mathcal{K})
is the opposite of the twisted-arrow
(\oo,1)
-category of
\mathcal{K}
. Colimits indexed by
\TwAro(\mathcal{K})
are coends.

•

\Seg
is an endofunctor on simplicial objects. See Definition
3.25
.

•

\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebras in a symmetric monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{V}
.

•

\Alg^{\sf rig}_{k}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of rigid
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories.

•

\mathbb{F}_{k}(V)
is the
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebra freely generated by
V
, an object of a symmetric monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{V}
.

•

\mathsf{B}
is the classifying space functor: for
\mathcal{C}
a flagged
(\infty,1)
-category,
\mathsf{B}\mathcal{C}
is its classifying space, which is the geometric realization of the simplicial space presenting
\mathcal{C}
.

•

\mathfrak{B}
is the deloop functor, from monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories to
(\oo,2)
-categories. See §
2.4
.

Convention 0.7
.

Unless otherwise specified, all stratified spaces in this work are assumed to be conically smooth in the sense of
[
AFT1
]
. All stratified maps are, likewise, also assumed to be conically smooth.

1.
Topological moduli spaces of framed tangles

1.1.
[\n 1,n]
-manifolds

The following class of stratified spaces will be used throughout this paper.

Definition 1.1
.

An
[\n 1,n]
-manifold
is a pair
(M^{(\n 1)}\subset M)
consisting of:

•

a smooth manifold
M
with boundary, such that each connected component is either a smooth
(\n 1)
-manifold without boundary or a smooth
n
-manifold with boundary;
and

•

a properly embedded smooth
(\n 1)
-dimensional submanifold
M^{(\n 1)}\subset M
that contains both the boundary
\partial M\subset M^{(\n 1)}
and each
(\n 1)
-dimensional component of
M
.

When there is no ambiguity, we denote an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold
(M^{(\n 1)}\subset M)
simply as the ambient manifold
M
.

Example 1.2
.

(1)

A smooth
(\n 1)
-manifold is an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
In particular,
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
is an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.

(2)

We regard a smooth
n
-manifold
M
with boundary as an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold, where
M^{(\n 1)}=\partial M
.
In particular, we regard
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
as an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.

(3)

More generally, for each
p\geq 0
,
we have

\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
as an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold, where
(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])^{(\n 1)}=\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\{0,\dots,p\}
.

Remark 1.3
.

An
[\n 1,n]
-manifold
M
is a conically smooth stratified space that satisfies the following condition.

Each stratum is either
(\n 1)
-dimensional or
n
-dimensional, and each
(\n 1)
-dimensional stratum is contained in the closure of at most two
n
-dimensional strata.

This condition is equivalent to requiring that for each
x\in M
, there is a basic neighborhood about
x
isomorphic with
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, or
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathsf{C}(\ast)
, or
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathsf{C}(S^{0})
.

Observation 1.4
.

Let
M
be an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
Its ambient smooth manifold admits a tangent vector bundle
\tau_{M}
, which is classified by map between spaces

\tau_{M}\colon M\longrightarrow\BO(\n 1)\coprod\BO(n)~,\qquad x\longmapsto\mathsf{T}_{x}M~,

in which the
(\n 1)
-dimensional components of
M
are carried to the first cofactor and the
n
-dimensional components of
M
are carried to the second.

Definition 1.5

(Remark 2.37 of
[
AFR1
]
)
.

Let
M
be an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
A
solid
n
-framing
on
M
is a homotopy-commutative lift

\textstyle{S^{\n 1}\coprod\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\BO(\n 1)\coprod\BO(n).}

Here, the downward map is the coproduct of the standard map
S^{\n 1}=\frac{\mathsf{O}(n)}{\mathsf{O}(\n 1)}\to\BO(\n 1)
with the basepoint
\ast=\frac{\mathsf{O}(n)}{\mathsf{O}(n)}\to\BO(n)
.

Remark 1.6
.

Note the identifications
S^{\n 1}\simeq{\sf Inj}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{n})_{/\mathsf{O}(n)}
and
\ast\simeq{\sf Inj}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{n})_{/\mathsf{O}(n)}
.
Consequently, a solid
n
-framing of an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold
M
is a vector bundle injection
\tau_{M}\hookrightarrow\epsilon^{n}_{M}
into a trivial rank-
n
vector bundle over
M
.
Indeed,
Such an injection restricts over each
n
-dimensional connected component of
M
as a trivialization of its tangent bundle.

1.2.
Topological moduli of tangles in
M

Definition 1.7

(Tangles)
.

Let
M=(M^{(\n 1)}\subset M)
be an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
A
1
-tangle in
M
is a compact smooth codimension-
(\n 1)
submanifold
W\subset M
with boundary that is transverse to
M^{(\n 1)}\subset M
and
\partial W=W\cap\partial M
.

Terminology 1.8
.

If the dimension is unspecified, a tangle
W\subset M
refers to a
1
-tangle.

Remark 1.9
.

If the
[\n 1,n]
-manifold
M
is simply a smooth
(\n 1)
-manifold, then a 1-tangle in
M
is a compact 0-dimensional submanifold of
M
.

Remark 1.10
.

Let
M
be a smooth
n
-manifold.
Let
\partial\subset M
be a codimension-1 properly embedded smooth submanifold that separates
M
.
Denote by
M^{\prime}
and
M^{\prime\prime}
the closures of each separation: Each is a smooth
n
-manifold with boundary
\partial
.
Regard
M=(\partial\subset M)
as an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
Then a tangle
W\subset M
is exactly a tangle in each of
W^{\prime}\subset M^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime\prime}\subset M^{\prime\prime}
which agree in
\partial
:
W^{\prime}\cap\partial=W^{\prime\prime}\cap\partial
.

Let
M=(M^{(\n 1)}\subset M)
be an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
Let
W
be a compact
[0,1]
-dimensional manifold.
There is a topological space

\Emb(W,M)

whose underlying set consists of smooth embeddings
W\xrightarrow{g}M
that are transverse to
M^{(\n 1)}\subset M
and that carry
0
-dimensional connected components of
W
to
(\n 1)
-dimensional connected components of
M
.
The topology on this set is the compact-open smooth topology (also known as the weak Whitney topology).
There is a natural function

\Emb(W,M)\longrightarrow\{W^{\prime}\subset M|\ W^{\prime}\text{ a tangle in }M\}

which assigns to an embedding
g:W\hookrightarrow M
the image
g(W)\subset M
.

Definition 1.11
.

Let
M
be an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
The topological space of tangles in
M
consists of the set
\Tang_{1}(M)=\{W\subset M|\ W\text{ a tangle in }M\}
of tangles, endowed with the finest topology for which the function

\Emb(W,M)\longrightarrow\Tang_{1}(M)

is continuous for every
[0,1]
-dimensional manifold
W
.
In the case that
M
is
(\n 1)
-dimensional, a tangle in
M
is a 0-dimensional submanifold therein, and we use the more suggestive notation

\Tang_{0}(M)

for the space of tangles in
M
.

Observation 1.12
.

For each
W
, there is a natural action of
\Aut(W)
on
\Emb(W,M)
. Letting
[W]
range over all isomorphism classes of
[0,1]
-dimensional manifolds
W
,
there is a homeomorphism

\coprod_{[W]}\Emb(W,M)_{/\Aut(W)}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\Tang_{1}(M)

between the disjoint union of the quotient of embeddings spaces, and the topological space of tangles.

1.3.
Topological spaces of framed tangles

In this section, we fix a space
B\to\Gr_{1}(n)=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{\n 1}
over the Grassmannian of 1-planes in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
We fix an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold
M
, together with a solid
n
-framing
\varphi
on
M
.

The solid
n
-framing
\varphi
on
M
determines, for each tangle
W\subset M
, a Gauss map

W\xrightarrow{~{\sf Gauss}_{W\subset(M,\varphi)}~}\Gr_{1}(n)~,\qquad w\longmapsto\begin{cases}\Bigl(\varphi\left(\mathsf{T}_{w}W\right)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\Bigr)~,&{\sf dim}_{w}(W)=1\\
\Bigl(\varphi(\mathsf{T}_{w}M)^{\perp}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\Bigr)~,&{\sf dim}_{w}(W)=0\end{cases}

whose value on
w\in W
depends on the dimension
{\sf dim}_{w}(W)
of the connected component of
W
containing
w
.

Definition 1.13
.

A
B
-framed tangle in
M
is a tangle
W\subset M
together with a lift of the Gauss map

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf Gauss}_{W\subset(M,\varphi)}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\Gr_{1}(n)~}

up to a specified homotopy.
For
W
a compact
[0,1]
-dimensional manifold,
the topological space of
B
-framed embeddings of
W
into
M
is the homotopy pullback:

\textstyle{\Emb^{B}(W,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map(W,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb(W,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map(W,\Gr_{1}(n)).}

This facilitates the following definition of the topological space of
B
-framed tangles.

Definition 1.14
.

The topological space
\Tang_{1}^{B}(M)
of
B
-framed 1-tangles in
M
is the quotient

\Tang_{1}^{B}(M)~:=~\coprod_{[W]}\Emb^{B}(W,M)_{/\Aut(W)}~,

indexed by the set of isomorphism classes of compact
[0,1]
-dimensional manifolds
W
.

In particular, a point of
\Tang_{1}^{B}(M)
is classified by a triple: a tangle
W\subset M
; a map
W\rightarrow B
; and a homotopy between
{\sf Gauss}_{W\subset(M,\varphi)}
and the composite
W\rightarrow B\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
.

We will be particularly concerned with the case of framed tangles.

Definition 1.15
.

The topological space of framed tangles
\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(M)
is the space
\Tang_{1}^{B}(M)
in which
B
is a point and the map
\ast\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
selects the line
\mathbb{R}^{\{n\}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
given by the last coordinate.

In particular, a point of
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(M)
is classified by a pair: a tangle
W\subset M
; and a nullhomotopy of the Gauss map
{\sf Gauss}_{W\subset(M,\varphi)}:W\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
.

2.
The
(\oo,1)
-category of tangles in terms of
\Bun

In this section, we define the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
. Heuristically, an object in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
consists of:

•

a finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
;

•

a nullhomotopy of the constant map
S\xrightarrow{{\sf const}_{\mathbb{R}^{\{n\}}}}\Gr_{1}(n)
.

Likewise, to define a morphism in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
, we endow
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
with its standard
n
-framing:
\mathsf{T}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\cong\epsilon^{\n 1}\oplus\epsilon^{1}\cong\epsilon^{n}
.
A morphism of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is then heuristically given by:

•

a 1-tangle
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
;

•

a nullhomotopy of the resulting Gauss map
W\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
.

This heuristic suggests that, whatever the technical definition of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
, its space of objects should have the homotopy type of
\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
and its space of morphisms should have the homotopy type
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})
.
In particular, for any pair of 0-tangles
S_{-},S_{+}\in\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
, there should result a homotopy equivalence between the space of morphisms of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})

\Map_{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})}(S_{-},S_{+})

and the fiber of the restriction map

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\longrightarrow\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})

over the point
S_{-}\sqcup S_{+}\in\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})
.

Remark 2.1
.

Observe that for a given
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
, the space of nullhomotopies of
S\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
is
\Omega(\Gr_{1}(n))^{S}
. Consequently, the set of equivalences classes of nullhomotopies is
\pi_{1}\Gr_{1}(n)
.
For
n=2
, we see that there are
\mathbb{Z}
-many objects of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
whose underlying tangle is a singleton
\ast\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
. For
n\geq 3
, we see that there are
\mathbb{Z}/2
-many isomorphism classes of objects of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
with underlying tangle a singleton
\ast\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
.

2.1.
The
(\oo,1)
-categories
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}

Recall the
(\infty,1)
-category
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
from
[
AFR1
]
.
An object is a conically smooth stratified space
M
equipped with a solid
n
-framing.
A morphism in
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
from
M
to
M^{\prime}
consists of:

•

a constructible bundle
X\xrightarrow{\pi}\Delta^{1}
over the standardly stratified 1-simplex;

•

a solid
n
-framing on
\pi
, which is an injection
\mathsf{T}_{X|\Delta^{1}}\hookrightarrow\epsilon^{n}_{X}
of the vertical tangent constructible bundle into the trivial rank-
n
bundle;

•

isomorphisms

X_{|\Delta^{\{0\}}}\cong M~,{\rm\ and}\qquad X_{|\Delta^{\{1\}}}\cong M^{\prime}\

between
M
and the special fiber of
X\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
, and
M^{\prime}
and the generic fiber of
X\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
.

There is a natural forgetful functor
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}\rightarrow\Bun
to the
(\infty,1)
-category
\Bun
classifying constructible bundles, given by forgetting the solid
n
-framing. We think of
\Bun
as parametrizing the deformation theory of stratified spaces: A morphism in
\Bun
is a 1-parameter deformation of a stratified space, encoded as a constructible bundle over the 1-simplex. More generally, for
K
a stratified space,
a
K
-point of
\Bun
is
K
-parameter family of stratified spaces, encoded as constructible bundle over
K
. See
[
AFR2
]
.

We recall the following special classes of morphisms in
\Bun
and, thereafter, in
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
. See §6.5–6.6 of
[
AFR2
]
and §1.4 of
[
AFR1
]
.

•

Closed-creation morphisms: The reversed mapping cylinder defines a monomorphism

\Cylr:\Strat^{\pcbl}\hookrightarrow\Bun^{\op}~,

where
\Strat^{\pcbl}
is the
(\infty,1)
-category whose objects are stratified spaces and whose morphisms are proper constructible bundles.
The
\Cylr
functor assigns to a morphism
X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}
of
\Strat^{\pcbl}
the morphism of
\Bun
given by the constructible bundle

\Cylr(X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}):=X_{0}\underset{X_{1}\times\Delta^{\{0\}}}{\amalg}X_{1}\times\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow\Delta^{1}~.

The image of
\Cylr
consists of
closed-creation
morphisms,
\Bun^{\sf cls.crt}
. If the proper constructible bundle
X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}
is, furthermore, an injection (i.e.,
X_{1}\to X_{0}
is the inclusion of a closed union of strata of
X_{0}
), then the associated morphism of
\Bun
is
closed
, and the essential image of closed embeddings is
\Bun^{\cls}
.
If
X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}
is surjective, the associated morphism of
\Bun
is
creation
, and the essential image of creation morphisms is
\Bun^{\sf crt}
.

•

Refinement-embedding morphisms: The open mapping cylinder defines a monomorphism

\Cylo:\Strat^{\sf open}\hookrightarrow\Bun~,

where
\Strat^{\sf open}
is the
(\infty,1)
-category whose objects are stratified spaces and whose morphisms are stratified maps
X_{0}\hookrightarrow X_{1}
which are open embeddings of underlying topological spaces. The
\Cylo
functor assigns to a morphism
X_{0}\hookrightarrow X_{1}
of
\Strat^{\sf open}
the morphism of
\Bun
given by the constructible bundle

\Cylo(X_{0}\rightarrow X_{1}):=X_{0}\times\Delta^{1}\underset{X_{0}\times\Delta^{1}\smallsetminus\Delta^{\{0\}}}{\amalg}X_{1}\times\Delta^{1}\smallsetminus\Delta^{\{0\}}\longrightarrow\Delta^{1}~.

The image of
\Cylr
consists of
refinement-embedding
morphisms,
\Bun^{\sf ref.emb}
. There are further
\oo
-subcategories of refinements and embeddings:
\Bun^{\sf ref}
is the essential image of stratified maps
X_{0}\rightarrow X_{1}
which are isomorphisms of underlying topological spaces;
\Bun^{\sf emb}
is the essential image of stratified maps
X_{0}\rightarrow X_{1}
which are isomorphisms onto its image as stratified spaces.

•

The
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bun^{\sf act}
of
active
morphisms of
\Bun
is the smallest
\oo
-subcategory containing both the refinement-embedding morphisms and the creation morphisms,
\Bun^{\sf ref.emb}
and
\Bun^{\sf crt}
.

Every morphism in
\Bun
admits a factorization as a closed-creation morphism followed by a refinement-embedding morphism.
Also,
\Bun
admits a factorization system: Every morphism admits a unique factorization as a closed morphism followed by an active morphism.
A morphism of
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
is closed, creation, refinement, embedding, and active if the underlying morphism of
\Bun
is so.
This defines corresponding
\oo
-subcategories
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
, as well a factorization system:
every morphism of
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
can be uniquely factored as closed morphism followed by an active morphism.

To rigorously construct
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
as an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category, we will define two functors

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1]}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}\longrightarrow\Spaces~.

Definition 2.2
.

The
(\oo,1)
-category
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[k]}
is the full
\oo
-subcategory of
\Mfd_{k}^{\sfr}
whose objects are solidly
k
-framed finite disjoint unions of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
The
(\oo,1)
-category
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
is the full
\oo
-subcategory of
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
whose objects are solidly
n
-framed
[\n 1,n]
-manifolds with the following properties:

•

each stratum is diffeomorphic to either
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
or
\mathbb{R}^{n}
;

•

the closure of each stratum is diffeomorphic with an open subspace of a closed disk.

Example 2.3
.

Here we explain how objects in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
arise as Poincaré duals of graphs embedded in framed
n
-manifolds.
Let
M
be a framed
n
-manifold with boundary.
Let
\Gamma\subset M
be a compact embedded graph that meets the boundary transversely.
Assume
\Gamma
has no self-loops.
Let
D\subset M
be a regular open neighborhood of
\Gamma
– it is an
n
-manifold with boundary
\partial D=D\cap\partial M
.
Being a regular neighborhood, there is a deformation retraction
D\xrightarrow{r}\Gamma
.
For each edge
E\subset\Gamma
, choose a point
e\in E
in its interior.
Then
r^{-1}(e)\subset D
is a properly embedded smooth
(\n 1)
-submanifold which is diffeomorphic with
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
– informally,
r^{-1}(e)
slices
D
transverse to
E
.
The pair
(\partial D\sqcup\underset{e}{\bigsqcup}r^{-1}\{e\}\subset D)
is an
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
The framing of
M
determines the structure of a solid
n
-framing on this
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
In this way,
\left(\partial D\sqcup\underset{e}{\bigsqcup}r^{-1}\{e\}\subset D\right)
may be regarded as an object in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
.
See Figure
1
.

Remark 2.4
.

Every object in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
is a finite disjoint union of
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
, equipped with a solid
n
-framing, and objects constructed in Example
2.3
.

Figure 1
.

A Poincaré dual of an embedded graph in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Here (in blue) is a compact embedded graph in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with no self-loops.
Engulfing this graph is an (red) open regular neighborhood of it.
Transverse to each edge is an (orange) open 2-disk that is properly embedded in the open regular neighborhood.
The open regular neighborhood, equipped with the open 2-disks therein and equipped with the solid 3-framing inherited from the standard framing of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, is an object in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[2,3]}
.

Let
k\geq 0
.
Recall the topological operad
\mathcal{E}_{k}
of little
k
-disks, which we regard as an
\infty
-operad:
\mathcal{E}_{k}\rightarrow\Fin_{\ast}
.
Recall the notion of closed covers from §3.5 of
[
AFR1
]
, which are pullback diagrams in
\Bun
of the form

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\cap B,}

all of whose morphisms are closed.
Such a closed cover is equivalent to a pair
A,B\subset X
of closed subspaces, each of which is a union of strata, such that
A\cup B=X
.
In particular, disjoint unions are closed covers, and disjoint unions constitute all of the closed covers in the particularly simple case of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}
.

Notation 2.5
.

For
k\geq 0
and
\mathcal{V}
be a symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, then

\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})
is the
(\oo,1)
-category of
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebras in
\mathcal{V}
:

\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})~:=~\Fun^{\otimes}(\mathcal{E}_{k},\mathcal{V})~.

For
k=1
, we further abbreviate
\Alg(\mathcal{V}):=\Alg_{1}(\mathcal{V})
.

Remark 2.6
.

Informally, an
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebra is an object with a
(k\text{-}1)
-sphere family of binary multiplication rules.
Remarkably, Dunn–Lurie additivity
[
Lu2
]
states that an
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebra in
\mathcal{V}
is precisely an object
A\in\mathcal{V}
with
k
compatible associative algebra structures.

Observation 2.7
.

(1)

Each linear injection
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
determines a morphism between topological operads
\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
of little disks, and therefore maps of spaces

(1)

S^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}{\sf Hom}^{\sf lin.inj}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\longrightarrow\Hom_{{\sf Operads}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{\n 1})~.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category.
The map (
1
) corepresents a conservative functor between
(\infty,1)
-categories,

(2)

S^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times\Alg_{\n 1}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\Alg(\mathcal{V})~,\qquad(v,A)\mapsto(A,\overset{v}{\otimes})~,

which is evidently functorial in the symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{V}
.

Algebras over little
n
-disks operads appear in the theory of functors on
(\oo,1)
-categories such as
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
as a consequence of the following proposition.

Proposition 2.8
.

Let
k\geq 0
.
Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(\oo,1)
-category with finite products, regarded as a symmetric monoidal structure via its Cartesian monoidal structure.
There is a canonical equivalence between
(\oo,1)
-categories:

\cShv_{\mathcal{X}}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr})~\simeq~\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{X})~.

Proof.

Taking path-components defines a functor
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[k]}\xrightarrow{\pi_{0}}{\sf Corr}(\Fin)
to the category of spans among finite sets.
There is a standard monomorphism
\Fin_{\ast}\xrightarrow{I_{+}\mapsto I}{\sf Corr}(\Fin)
between categories, whose value on a morphism
(I_{+}\xrightarrow{f}J_{+})
is the span
(I\hookleftarrow f^{-1}(J)\xrightarrow{f_{|}}J)
.
Consider the
\infty
-subcategory
\Disk^{\fr}_{k,+}:=\Fin_{\ast}\underset{{\sf Corr}(\Fin)}{\times}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[k]}
.
This
(\infty,1)
-category
\Disk^{\fr}_{k,+}
agrees with that of Definition 2.1.5 of
[
AF6
]
, and admits the following explicit description:

•

an object
U_{+}
is a space with a disjoint basepoint and where
U\in\Disk_{k}^{{\fr}}
is a finite disjoint union of framed
k
-dimensional Euclidean spaces;

•

a morphism from
U_{+}
to
V_{+}
is a pointed map
g:U_{+}\rightarrow V_{+}
such that the restriction

g_{|}:U_{|V}\rightarrow V

is a framed embedding.

As Proposition 2.1.10 of
[
AF6
]
, we proved that for
\mathcal{X}
a Cartesian symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, there is a fully-faithful embedding

\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{X})~\hookrightarrow~\Fun(\Disk^{{\fr}}_{k,+},\mathcal{X})~;

the essential image consists of functors
F
satisfying the following
reduced Segal
condition:
F(\ast)\rightarrow\uno_{\mathcal{X}}
is an equivalence, and the canonical morphisms

F(U_{+})\longrightarrow\prod_{i\in\pi_{0}U}F(U^{i}_{+})

are equivalences for all
U_{+}
, where
U^{i}\subset U
is the connected component corresponding to the element
i\in\pi_{0}U
.

Restriction and right Kan extension give an adjunction

\iota^{\ast}:\Fun(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[k]},\mathcal{X})\leftrightarrows\Fun(\Disk_{k,+}^{\fr},\mathcal{X}):\iota_{\ast}~,

which we now show restricts as an equivalence between the full
\oo
-subcategory
\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{X})\subset\Fun(\Disk^{\fr}_{k,+},\mathcal{X})
and the full
\oo
-subcategory of functors
F:\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[k]}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
which preserve products.
Let
F:\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[k]}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a product-preserving functor.
We show that the unit
F\rightarrow\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}F
is an equivalence. The proof that the counit is an equivalence is identical. For
U\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}
, the value of
\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}F
on
U
is given by the limit of the composite functor

(\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}F)(U)=\limit\Bigl(\Disk_{k,+}^{\fr}\underset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}}{\times}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr})^{U/}\rightarrow\Disk_{k,+}^{\fr}\xrightarrow{\iota^{\ast}F}\mathcal{X}\Bigr)~.

By the closed-active factorization system
[
AFR1
]
on
\Bun
, and hence on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}
, there is a left adjoint to the inclusion

\Disk_{k,+}^{\fr}\underset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}}{\times}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr})^{U/^{\cls}}\hookrightarrow\Disk_{k,+}^{\fr}\underset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}}{\times}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr})^{U/}

of the full
\infty
-subcategory consisting of those
U\to V
that are closed morphisms in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}
.
The adjoint sends a morphism
U\rightarrow E
to
U\rightarrow E^{\prime}
, the first factor in the closed-active factorization
U\xrightarrow{\cls}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sf act}E
of the original morphism. Consequently, the inclusion is an initial functor, and therefore the right Kan extension can be computed after restricting to the
\infty
-undercategory.

Lastly, we observe that the closed
\infty
-undercategory
\Disk_{k,+}^{\fr}\underset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr}}{\times}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]}^{\sfr})^{U/^{\cls}}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{P}(\pi_{0}U)^{\op}
, the opposite of the poset of subsets of
\pi_{0}U
.
Since
F
preserves products, this gives that the value of the limit indexed by
\mathcal{P}(\pi_{0}U)^{\op}
is exactly the product of the values of
F
over the elements of
\pi_{0}U
, which is
F(U)
.
∎

Remark 2.9
.

Compare Proposition
2.8
with Proposition C.1 of
[
BH
]
, which is the
k\mapsto\oo
limit of this result.
The proofs of these two results are essentially identical.

2.2.
Rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal categories from copresheaves on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}

Recall from
[
AF3
]
the pair of
(\infty,1)
-categories
\Cat_{(\oo,n)}\subset\fCat_{(\infty,n)}
of
(\infty,2)
-categories and of flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories.
Recall the full
\infty
-subcategory
\bTheta_{n}\subset\Cat_{(\oo,n)}
from
[
Re2
]
, where
\bTheta_{1}=\mathbf{\Delta}
.
Each object in
\bTheta_{n}
is an
n
-category associated to a certain class of
n
-dimensional pasting diagram.
After the work
[
Re2
]
, it is proved in
[
AF3
]
that the full
\infty
-subcategory
\bTheta_{n}\subset\Cat_{(\oo,n)}
strongly generates. Specifically, the restricted Yoneda functor

\fCat_{(\infty,n)}\longrightarrow\PShv(\bTheta_{n})~,\qquad\mathcal{C}\longmapsto\bigl(T\mapsto\Hom_{\fCat_{(\infty,n)}}(T,\mathcal{C})\bigr)~,

is fully-faithful with essential image those presheaves on
\bTheta_{n}
that satisfy the Segal condition.

Definition 2.10
.

Let
n\geq 2
.
The respective
(\infty,1)
-categories of
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories
and of
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-categories
are

\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})~\subset~\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})~,

those of
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebras in
\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
and in
\fCat_{(\oo,1)}
.
A
monoidal (flagged)
(\infty,1)
-category
is an
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-algebra in (flagged)
(\infty,1)
-categories.

Definition 2.11
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{R}\in\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})
be a monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category.
An object
V\in\mathcal{R}
is
right-dualizable
if left-tensoring with
V

\mathcal{R}\xrightarrow{V\otimes-}\mathcal{R}

has a right adjoint

\mathcal{R}\xleftarrow{-\otimes V^{R}}\mathcal{R}

represented by right-tensoring with an object
V^{R}\in\mathcal{R}
.
The monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}
has
right-duals
if every object of
\mathcal{R}
has a right-dual.

(2)

An
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}\in\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})
is
rigid
if, for each
v\in S^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}
, the monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
(\mathcal{R},\overset{v}{\otimes})
(see the notation of Observation
2.7
(2)), has right-duals.
The
(\infty,1)
-category of
rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories
is the full
\infty
-subcategory

\Alg^{\sf rig}_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})~\subset~\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})

consisting of those
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories
\mathcal{R}
that are rigid.

Observation 2.12
.

A monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}
is rigid if and only if each object in
\mathcal{R}
has both a right-dual and a left-dual.

Observation 2.13
.

The condition that an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category is rigid can be detected on any of its underlying monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories.
More precisely,
for each
v\in S^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}
, the evident diagram among
(\infty,1)
-categories is a pullback:

\textstyle{\Alg^{\sf rig}_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm fully~faithful}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e25})_{v}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e25})_{v}}
\textstyle{\Alg^{\sf rig}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm fully~faithful}
\textstyle{\Alg(\Cat_{(\infty,1)}).}

Recall from
[
AFR1
]
the
cellular realization
functor,
\bTheta_{n}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\langle-\rangle}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{n}^{\sfr}
. In this paper, we only use this functor in the case that
n=1
. In that case, each value of the cellular realization functor is a 0-disk, or a stratified closed 1-disk.
We hereafter use the following suggestive notation.

Notation 2.14
.

\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{~\langle-\rangle~}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{\sfr}~,\qquad[p]\longmapsto\mathbb{D}[p]~.

Now, taking products of stratified spaces, and products of framings, defines a functor

(3)

\rho\colon\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1]}^{\sfr}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\id\times\langle-\rangle}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1]}^{\sfr}\times\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{1}^{\sfr}\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}~,\qquad\bigl(U,[p]\bigr)\longmapsto U\times\mathbb{D}[p]~.

Observation 2.15
.

The functor
\rho
of (
3
) carries disjoint unions in the first variable to disjoint unions, and closed covers in the second variable to closed covers.
By Proposition
2.8
, restriction along (
3
) therefore defines a functor

(4)

\cShv(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\xrightarrow{~\rho^{\ast}~}\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})~.

Consider the reflexive localization between
(\oo,1)
-categories:

(5)

(-)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}\colon\fCat_{(\infty,1)}~\leftrightarrows~\Cat\colon{\rm inclusion}~,

in which the left adjoint is the forgetful functor, given by taking univalent-completion.
This left adjoint preserves finite products.
Therefore, the reflexive localization (
5
) lifts to a reflexive localization between
(\oo,1)
-categories:

(6)

(-)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}\colon\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})~\rightleftarrows~\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})\colon{\rm inclusion}~.

We state the main result of this subsection here, and postpone its proof to the end of this subsection.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
n\geq 2
.
The univalent-completion of each value of the functor
\rho^{\ast}
is an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category that is rigid. That is, there is a unique dashed functor making the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\cShv(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}^{\sf rig}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm fully~faithful}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)}).}

We prove Lemma
2.16
by reducing to the case in which
n=2
, then invoking the following diagrammatic criteria for rigidity.

Lemma 2.17
.

The univalent-completion of a monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}
is rigid provided the existence of the following data:

(1)

maps between spaces

\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L}
\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})}
and
\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})~;}

(2)

fillers in diagrams among spaces

\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\eta_{L}}
\scriptstyle{!}
\scriptstyle{(\id,L)}
\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\times\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\times\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}}

\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})}
,

\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})}

and

\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\uno\rangle}
\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{!}

\textstyle{\Obj(\mathcal{R})\times\Obj(\mathcal{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\otimes}

\scriptstyle{\otimes}

,

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

Proof.

Denote the unit morphism of the adjunction (
6
):

\overline{(-)}\colon\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}~,\qquad V\longmapsto\overline{V}~,

which is a morphism between monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-categories.

Let
\overline{V}\in\Obj(\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv})
.
Using that the map
\Obj(\mathcal{R})\xrightarrow{\Obj\bigl(\overline{(-)}\bigr)}\Obj(\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv})
is surjective, choose a lift
V\in\Obj(\mathcal{R})
of
\overline{V}\in\Obj(\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv})
.
Consider the four 1-cells in
\mathcal{R}
:

\uno\xrightarrow{~\eta_{L}~}V\otimes L(V)~,~L(V)\otimes V\xrightarrow{~\epsilon_{L}(V)~}\uno\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\uno\xrightarrow{~\eta_{R}(V)~}R(V)\otimes V~,~V\otimes R(V)\xrightarrow{~\epsilon_{R}~}\uno~.

Consider the invertible 2-cells in
\mathcal{R}
:

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{L}(V)\otimes\id_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\id_{V}}
\textstyle{\alpha_{L}(V)}
\textstyle{V}
\textstyle{L(V)\otimes V\otimes L(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{L}(V)\otimes\id_{L(V)}}
\textstyle{V\otimes L(V)\otimes V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id_{V}\otimes\epsilon_{L}(V)}
,
\textstyle{L(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id_{L(V)}\otimes\eta_{L}(V)}
\scriptstyle{\id_{L(V)}}
\textstyle{\beta_{L}(V)}
\textstyle{L(V)}

and

\textstyle{R(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{R}(V)\otimes\id_{R(V)}}
\scriptstyle{\id_{R(V)}}
\textstyle{\alpha_{R}(V)}
\textstyle{R(V)}
\textstyle{V\otimes R(V)\otimes V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{R}(V)\otimes\id_{V}}
\textstyle{R(V)\otimes V\otimes R(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id_{R(V)}\otimes\epsilon_{R}(V)}
,
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id_{V}\otimes\eta_{R}(V)}
\scriptstyle{\id_{V}}
\textstyle{\beta_{R}(V)}
\textstyle{V.}

Now, these diagrams in
\mathcal{R}
are carried by the morphism
\overline{(-)}
between monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-categories to corresponding diagrams in
\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
.
By definition of left- and right-duals in a monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, these data witness the values
\overline{L(V)}\in\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
and
\overline{R(V)}\in\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
as, respectively, left- and right-duals of
\overline{V}\in\mathcal{R}^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
.

∎

We prove Lemma
2.16
by constructing specific diagrams in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
which any closed sheaf
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\Spaces
carries to the diagrams of Observation
2.17
.
Constructing these diagrams in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
takes some explanation.
The hasty reader might simply inspect Figures
2
–
7
for depictions of such diagrams in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
.

Let
M
be a
[1,2]
-manifold.
Consider the space of solid 2-framings on
M
:

\sfr_{2}(M)~:=~\Map_{/\BO(1)\amalg\BO(2)}\left(M,S^{1}\amalg\ast\right)~.

Via Remark
1.6
, the standard action
\mathsf{O}(2)\lacts\mathbb{R}^{2}
determines an action

(7)

\mathsf{O}(2)~\lacts~\sfr_{2}(M)~.

Should
M\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be given as subspace of a Euclidean 2-space, there is a natural solid 2-framing:

\varphi_{\natural}^{M}~\in~\sfr_{2}(M)~.

In particular, there is a natural solid 2-framing on
\mathbb{R}^{1}
and on
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
and, more generally, on
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
for each
p\geq 0
(see Notation
2.14
).
Generally, for
(D,\varphi)\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
an object, if
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\varphi=\varphi_{\natural}^{D}
, we simply denote the object
D:=(D,\varphi)\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
.
For each
p\geq 0
, there is a canonical
\mathsf{O}(2)
-equivariant identification,

S^{1}\simeq\mathsf{O}(2)_{/\mathsf{O}(1)}~\simeq~\sfr_{2}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\qquad\text{ (if $p=0$) }\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathsf{O}(2)~\simeq~\sfr_{2}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\qquad\text{ (if $p>0$) }~,

that associates the identity element with the natural solid 2-framing:
\uno\mapsto\varphi^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]}_{\natural}
.
In particular, for each
p\geq 0
, the space of solid 2-framings on
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is a 1-type;
for
\varphi,\psi\in\sfr_{2}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)
,
there is a canonical
\mathbb{Z}
-torsor of identifications
\varphi\simeq\psi
.

Denote the object

\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\sf rev}~\in~\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}

which is
\mathbb{R}^{1}
with the solid 2-framing that is the action by
-\uno\in\mathsf{O}(2)
on the standard solid 2-framing on
\mathbb{R}^{1}
.
Consider the continuous map between topological spaces:

{\sf Rot}\colon\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathsf{O}(2)~,\qquad t\longmapsto\begin{bmatrix}\cos(\pi t)&-\sin(\pi t)\\
\sin(\pi t)&\cos(\pi t)\end{bmatrix}~.

Consider the composite continuous maps

(8)

\Delta^{2}\xrightarrow{(t_{0},t_{1},t_{2})\mapsto t_{1}}\mathbb{R}\xrightarrow{~\sf Rot~}\mathsf{O}(2)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\Delta^{2}\xrightarrow{(t_{0},t_{1},t_{2})\mapsto-t_{1}}\mathbb{R}\xrightarrow{~\sf Rot~}\mathsf{O}(2)~.

Once and for all, choose a framed diffeomorphism
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{\cong}(-\frac{1}{2},+\frac{1}{2})
to an open interval.
Consider the framed open embedding

\mu\colon\mathbb{R}^{1}\sqcup\mathbb{R}^{1}~=~\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\{\pm 1\}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}~,\qquad(v,r)\longmapsto f(v)+r~.

Consider the open embeddings

(9)

\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\xrightarrow{~\eta^{\prime}_{L}~}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}~,\qquad(v,r)\longmapsto\Bigl(~(1+f(v))\sin(-\frac{\pi}{2}r)~,~(1+f(v))\cos(-\frac{\pi}{2}r)-1~\Bigr)~,

and

(10)

\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\xrightarrow{~\epsilon^{\prime}_{L}~}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}~,\qquad(v,r)\longmapsto\Bigl(~(1-f(v))\sin(\frac{\pi}{2}r)~,~1-(1-f(v))\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}r)~\Bigr)~,

and

(11)

\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\xrightarrow{~\eta^{\prime}_{R}~}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}~,\qquad(v,r)\longmapsto\Bigl(~(1-f(v))\sin(\frac{\pi}{2}r)~,~(1-f(v))\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}r)-1~\Bigr)~,

and

(12)

\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\xrightarrow{~\epsilon^{\prime}_{R}~}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}~,\qquad(v,r)\longmapsto\Bigl(~(1+f(v))\sin(-\frac{\pi}{2}r)~,~1-(1+f(v))\cos(-\frac{\pi}{2}r)~\Bigr)~.

See Figures
2
–
5
.

Figure 2
.
The open embedding
\epsilon^{\prime}_{L}
.
It, together with the indicated framing, defines the morphism
\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{L}}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[1,2]}
.

Figure 3
.
The open embedding
\epsilon^{\prime}_{R}
.
It, together with the indicated framing, defines the morphism
\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{R}}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[1,2]}
.

Figure 4
.
The open embedding
\eta^{\prime}_{L}
.
It, together with the indicated framing, defines the morphism
\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{\eta_{L}}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[1,2]}
.

Figure 5
.
The open embedding
\eta^{\prime}_{R}
.
It, together with the indicated framing, defines the morphism
\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{\eta_{R}}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[1,2]}
.

Pulling back the standard solid 2-framing on
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
along each of these open embeddings determines solid 2-framings on each domain
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
:

(\eta^{\prime}_{L})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~,~(\epsilon^{\prime}_{L})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~,~(\eta^{\prime}_{R})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~,~(\epsilon^{\prime}_{R})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~.

Consider the two proper constructible embeddings:

\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{~\partial_{\pm}~}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}~,\qquad x\longmapsto(x,\pm 1)~.

These morphisms correspond to closed morphisms in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
:

\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\xrightarrow{~\partial_{\pm}~}\mathbb{R}^{1}~.

Note that each of these solid 2-framings restricts along
\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{\partial_{\pm}}\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
as, respectively, the standard solid 2-framing on
\mathbb{R}^{1}
and the reversed solid 2-framing on
\mathbb{R}^{1}
:

\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}}~=~\partial_{-}^{\ast}(\eta^{\prime}_{L})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~=~\partial_{-}^{\ast}(\epsilon^{\prime}_{L})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~=~\partial_{-}^{\ast}(\eta^{\prime}_{R})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~=~\partial_{-}^{\ast}(\epsilon^{\prime}_{R})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~;

and

(-\uno)\cdot\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}}~=~\partial_{+}^{\ast}(\eta^{\prime}_{L})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~=~\partial_{+}^{\ast}(\eta^{\prime}_{R})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~=~\partial_{+}^{\ast}(\epsilon^{\prime}_{L})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~=~\partial_{+}^{\ast}(\epsilon^{\prime}_{R})^{\ast}\varphi_{\natural}^{\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}}~.
