Proof of Lemma
2.16
.

Taking products with
\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}
defines downward functors in a commutative square among
(\oo,1)
-categories:

\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1]}^{\sfr}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)}
\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1]}^{\sfr}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}.}

Notice that the downward functors carry closed covers to closed covers.
By Observation
2.15
, all of the functors in this diagram carry closed covers to closed covers.
Therefore, applying
\Fun(-,\Spaces)
to this commutative square results in a commutative square on the left in this diagram among
(\oo,1)
-categories:

\textstyle{\cShv(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\cShv(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\Alg(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}}
\textstyle{\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)}).}

The universal property of univalent-completions supplies a 2-cell in the right square witnessing it as lax-commuting.
Because univalent-completion
\fCat_{(\infty,1)}\to\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
preserves products, this 2-cell is invertible, and therefore the right square canonically commutes.
Observation
2.13
implies the diagram among
(\oo,1)
-categories

\textstyle{\Alg^{\sf rig}_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}\times-)^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\Alg^{\sf rig}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})}

is a pullback.
Therefore, the statement of the lemma is true if and only if it is true for
n=2
.
We are therefore reduced to the case in which
n=2
.

Next, let
(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\Spaces)\in\cShv(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr})
.
Denote the monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}:=\rho^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
.
Observe that
\mathcal{F}
carries

(1)

the morphisms in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
,

\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{~L~}\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\sf rev}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbb{R}^{1}\xrightarrow{~R~}\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\sf rev}~,

to the maps between spaces

\Obj(\mathcal{R})\xrightarrow{~L~}\Obj(\mathcal{R})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\Obj(\mathcal{R})\xrightarrow{~R~}\Obj(\mathcal{R})~,

which are the data of Lemma
2.18
(1);

(2)

the diagrams in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
of Observation
2.18
to the diagrams of Lemma
2.17
(2);

(3)

the diagrams in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1,2]}^{\sfr}
of Observation
2.19
to the diagrams of Lemma
2.17
(3).

Therefore, by Lemma
2.17
, the univalent-completion of the monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}:=\rho^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
is rigid.

∎

2.3.
Tangle categories

We now define
\Bord^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
, the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category of principal interest in this paper.
We do this through Lemma
2.16
, by defining a functor

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1]}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Spaces

that carries closed covers to pullbacks.
In particular, its space of objects
\obj(\Bord^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}))
is the value of this functor on
(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},[0])
, and its space of morphisms
\mor(\Bord^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}))
is the value on
(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},[1])
.

Definition 2.20
.

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is the composite functor

\textstyle{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\colon\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1]}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Map_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},-)}
\textstyle{\Spaces~.}

We have the following corollary of Lemma
2.16
.

Corollary 2.21
.

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category. The univalent-completion
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}
is a rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category.

Proof.

Recall that closed covers are, in particular, limit diagrams in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
.
Therefore, the second functor in the definition of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is a closed sheaf on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]}
.
The result follows from Lemma
2.16
.

∎

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
X
be a solidly
n
-framed
[\n 1,n]
-manifold.
The space of morphisms in
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
from
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
to
X
is the moduli space of framed tangles in
X
.
In particular, for
X=\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
and
p\geq 1
, and for
X=\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
, we have equivalences

\Map_{\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])

and

\Map_{\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\bigr)\simeq\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})~.

Proof.

We denote by
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\subset\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
the full
(\oo,1)
-subcategory of
[\n 1,n]
-manifolds. Composition in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
defines a functor over
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\times\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
:

\textstyle{\Ar^{\sf cls.crt}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\sf emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}\times\ev_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\sf comp}
\textstyle{\Ar(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}\times\ev_{t}}
\textstyle{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\times\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}
.

where
\Ar^{\sf cls.crt}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})
is the full
\oo
-subcategory of
\Ar(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})
whose objects are the closed-creation morphisms, and
\Ar^{\sf emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})
is the full
\oo
-subcategory of whose objects are the embeddings.
For each object
M\in\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
, the base change of this diagram along

\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\xrightarrow{(\{M\},{\sf id})}\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\times\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}

gives a functor over
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
:

(18)

\textstyle{(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\sf emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\scriptstyle{{\sf comp}}
\textstyle{(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\textstyle{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}
.

The statement of the present lemma is implied by the following stronger assertion:

\dagger

The composition functor in (
18
) is an equivalence between
(\oo,1)
-categories if
M
is a smooth manifold equipped with a solid
n
-framing.
In particular, each morphism
M\to X
in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
admits a unique factorization

M\xrightarrow{~\sf cls.crt~}E\xrightarrow{~\emb~}X

as the reversed mapping cylinder on a proper constructible bundle followed by a stratified open embedding morphism.

We first show that this assertion applied to the case
M=\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
gives the desired result.
By intersecting tangles with closed unions of strata, the assignment
X\mapsto\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(X)
is functorial with respect to closed morphisms in the argument
X
.
Consequently, this assignment carries closed covers to limits.
Meanwhile, because closed covers are, in particular, limit diagrams in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
, the functor
X\mapsto\Map_{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},X)
also carries closed covers to limits.
Now, by definition of
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
, every object therein admits a closed cover by
n
-manifold with boundary and
(\n 1)
-manifolds without boundary.
We now prove the case where
X
is a solidly
n
-framed
n
-manifold with boundary; the case of
X
an
(\n 1)
-manifold is nearly identical, but easier, and we leave the details to the reader.

Consider first the case in which
X
is an
n
-dimensional manifold with boundary.
The assertion then gives an equivalence

\Map_{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},X)\simeq\obj\Bigl(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]/^{\sf emb}X}\Bigr)~,

the latter space being the fiber over
X
of the functor

(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\sf emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\xrightarrow{\ev_{t}}\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}~.

We can now observe that there is a forgetful map

(19)

\obj\Bigl((\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]/^{\sf emb}X}\Bigr)\longrightarrow\obj\Bigl(\Mfld_{1}^{\partial,\fr}\Bigr)

to the moduli space of framed 1-manifolds with boundary.
To see this, let
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\xrightarrow{\sf cls.crt}E\xrightarrow{\sf emb}X
be a closed-creation morphism followed by an embedding morphism.
The first morphism is, by definition of closed-creation morphisms in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
, the datum of a proper fiber bundle
E\to\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
that respects solid
n
-framings.
The second morphism is, by definition of embedding morphisms in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
, an open embedding between manifolds with boundary.
Denote by
W
the fiber over
0\in\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
of this proper fiber bundle – necessarily it is a compact manifold with boundary, and it has dimension-1 because
X
is
n
-dimensional.
Using that the fiber bundle
E\to\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
respects solid
n
-framings,
W
inherits the structure of a framing as a compact 1-manifold with boundary.
The map (
19
) assigns this compact framed 1-manifold
W
to
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\xrightarrow{\sf cls.crt}E\xrightarrow{\sf emb}X
.

Let
W
be a compact framed 1-manifold with boundary.
From the definition of (
19
), we identify its fiber over
W
as a space of framed embeddings:

\textstyle{\obj\Bigl((\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[\n 1,n]/^{\sf emb}X}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{reportW})}
\textstyle{\Emb^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times W,X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\obj\Bigl(\Mfld_{1}^{\partial,\fr}\Bigr)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\{W\}~.}

The associated action of the loop space
\Omega_{\{W\}}\bigl(\obj(\Mfld_{1}^{\partial,\fr})\bigl)
on the fiber is then the natural action of the framed diffeomorphisms of
W
on the space of embeddings:

\Omega_{\{W\}}\bigl(\obj(\Mfld_{1}^{\partial,\fr})\bigl)\simeq\Diff^{\fr}(W)\circlearrowright\Emb^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times W,X)~.

This implies the equivalence

\Map_{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},X)\simeq\coprod_{[W]}\Emb^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times W,X)_{/\Diff^{\fr}(W)}

where
[W]
ranges over isomorphism classes of framed 1-manifolds with boundary.

We now give a matching identification of
\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(X)
. There is likewise a forgetful map

\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(X)\longrightarrow\obj\bigl(\Mfld_{1}^{\partial,\fr}\bigr)~,\qquad(W\subset X)\longmapsto W~,

since a trivialization of the Gauss map associated to a tangle
W\subset X
defines a framing of the 1-manifold
W
. The fiber of this forgetful map is equivalent to the homotopy fiber of the (oriented lift of the) Gauss map

\Emb(W,X)\longrightarrow\Map(W,\mathsf{V}_{1}(n))~,\qquad(W\xrightarrow{f}X)\longmapsto(W\xrightarrow{{\sf Gauss}_{f}}\mathsf{V}_{1}(n))~,

which is equivalent to the space of framed embeddings

\Emb^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times W,X)\simeq{\sf fiber}_{\gamma}\Bigl(\Emb(W,X)\rightarrow\Map(W,V_{1}(n))\Bigr)

where
\gamma
is the framing of
W
. The loop space
\Omega_{\{W\}}\obj\bigl(\Mfld_{1}^{\partial,\fr}\bigr)\simeq\Diff^{\fr}(W)
again acts by the canonical action. This gives an identification

\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(X)\simeq\coprod_{[W]}\Emb^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times W,X)_{/\Diff^{\fr}(W)}

and thus proves the equivalence

\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(X)\simeq\Map_{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},X)

in the case of
X\in\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
an
n
-dimensional manifold with boundary.

The second case, of
X
an
(\n 1)
-manifold without boundary, follows mutatis mutandis, replacing the target of the forgetful maps with the moduli space of compact 0-dimensional manifolds.
We leave the details to the reader.

We now prove the assertion
(\dagger)
, which will then complete the proof of the present lemma.

The composition functor (
18
) is an equivalence if and only if, for each
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
the map between the fibers of the map,

\Map\left([p],(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\right)\longrightarrow\Map\left([p],(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}\right)~,

is a weak homotopy equivalence.
That is, it suffices to show that for each choice of basepoint in the domain of this map, and each
q\geq 0
,
the map between sets of connected components of based maps from the
q
-sphere,

\Map_{\ast}\Bigl(S^{q},\Map\bigl([p],(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Map_{\ast}\Bigl(S^{q},\Map\bigl([p],(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}\bigr)\Bigr)~,

is bijective on
\pi_{0}
.
We show below that the map between connected components of non-based maps

(20)

\Map\Bigl(S^{q},\Map\bigl([p],(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Map\Bigl(S^{q},\Map\bigl([p],(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}\bigr)\Bigr)~,

is bijective on
\pi_{0}
.
The case of based maps follows nearly identically, and we leave the modification to the reader.
By adjunction,
\pi_{0}
-bijectivity of (
20
) is implied by bijectivity of the map between sets of connected components of spaces of functors,

(21)

\Map\Bigl(\exit(K),(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Map\Bigl(\exit(K),(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}\Bigr)~,

for each compact stratified space of the form
K=\Delta^{p}\times S^{q}
.
Here
\Delta^{p}
is the standardly stratified
p
-simplex, and
S^{q}
is the unstratified
q
-sphere.
In this way,
\pi_{0}
-bijectivity of the map (
20
) is implied by
\pi_{0}
-bijectivity of the map (
21
) for each compact stratified space
K
.
This is to equivalent to showing that for each functor
\exit(K)\rightarrow(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}
, there exists a lift

(22)

\textstyle{(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/^{\sf cls.crt}}\underset{\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}{\times}\Ar^{\emb}(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\exit(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}}

that is unique up to homotopy.

By adjunction, a functor
\exit(K)\rightarrow(\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})^{M/}
is equivalent to a functor

\exit(K)^{\tl}~\simeq~\exit\bigl(\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)\bigr)\longrightarrow\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}

together with an identification of the value on the cone-point as
M
.
Here,
\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K):=\{0\}\underset{K\times\{0\}}{\amalg}K\times[0,1]
is the closed cone on
K
, regarded as a stratified space via the stratification
[0,1]\xrightarrow{t\mapsto\lceil t\rceil}[1]
.
The assumed compactness of
K
implies compactness of
\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
.
The latter classifies the following data:

•

a constructible bundle
X\xrightarrow{\pi}\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
;

•

an
\sfr_{n}
-structure on
\pi
, by which we mean a lift

\mathsf{T}_{\pi}\colon\exit(X)\to\Vect^{\sf inj}_{/\mathbb{R}^{n}}\to\Vect^{\sf inj}
of the fiberwise tangent classifier (see Remark 2.37 of
[
AFR1
]
);

•

an equivalence
M\simeq X_{0}
over
\Vect^{\sf inj}_{/\mathbb{R}^{n}}
between
M
and
X_{0}
, the fiber of
X
over
\{0\}\subset\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
.

So fix such data.
A lift in (
22
) is equivalent to the following data:

•

a constructible bundle
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{1})
over the closed cone;

•

an
\sfr_{n}
-structure on the constructible bundle
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{1})
;

•

an identification
over
\Vect^{\sf inj}_{/\mathbb{R}^{n}}
of its restriction:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{X}_{|\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{\{1\}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{\{1\}});}

and subject to the conditions that:

•

for each point
k\in K
, the pulled-back constructible bundle

\widetilde{X}_{|\overline{\mathsf{C}}(\{k\}\times\Delta^{\{0\}})}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathsf{C}}\bigl(\{k\}\times\Delta^{\{0\}}\bigr)=\overline{\mathsf{C}}\bigl(\{k\}\bigr)\cong\Delta^{1}

is classified by a closed-creation morphism in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
;

•

for each point
k\in K
, the pulled-back constructible bundle

\widetilde{X}_{|\{k\}\times\Delta^{1}}\longrightarrow\{k\}\times\Delta^{1}\times\{1\}=\Delta^{1}

is classified by an embedding morphism in
\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
.

We first construct such a lift (
22
).
Take the stratified space
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)
,
which is the link of the closed constructible subspace
X_{0}\subset X
. This link lies in a commutative diagram among stratified spaces:

\textstyle{M=X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf p.cbl}
\scriptstyle{\sf open}
\textstyle{X_{|K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{0\}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Link_{\{0\}}\bigl(\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K.}

From the definition of links, the fact that
X_{0}=M
is trivially stratified results in two further simplifications.

(1)

The proper constructible bundle
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\rightarrow X_{|K\times\Delta^{1}}
is, further, a
fiber bundle
of stratified spaces.
(Heuristically, the fibers vary continuously.)

(2)

The open map
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\rightarrow X_{|K\times\Delta^{1}}
is, further, an
open stratified embedding
.
(The refinement map onto its image is an isomorphism.)

This allows us to now make use of the reversed and open mapping cylinders, as in Theorem 6.6.15 of
[
AFR2
]
. The map
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\rightarrow M
is proper constructible fiberwise over
K
, and taking the reversed mapping cylinder fiberwise over
K
gives a constructible bundle

{\sf Cylr}^{\sf fib}_{K}\Bigl(M\leftarrow\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)~.

The map
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\rightarrow X_{|K}
is open fiberwise over
K
, and taking the open mapping cylinder fiberwise over
K
gives a constructible bundle

{\sf Cylo}^{\sf fib}_{K}\Bigl(\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\rightarrow X_{|K}\Bigr)\longrightarrow K\times\Delta^{1}~.

These constructible bundles base change over
K=K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}
identically.
Using that
\Bun
is an
(\oo,1)
-category, this pair of concatenating constructible bundles extends to a constructible bundle

\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{1})\supset\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)\underset{K}{\amalg}K\times\Delta^{1}~.

With point (2) above,
the construction of this constructible bundle supplies the sought data of a lift (
22
), sans
\sfr_{n}
-structures.
We now extend the given
\sfr_{n}
-structure on the constructible bundle
X\to\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
to one on
\widetilde{X}\to\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{1})
.

Since the
\sfr_{n}
tangential structure pulls back along open maps, point (2) just above grants that there is a unique
\sfr_{n}
-structure on the constructible bundle
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\to K
for which the open embedding of point (2) is one between
\sfr_{n}
-structured constructible bundles.
Furthermore, the
\sfr_{n}
-structure on
X\to\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
determines a
\sfr_{n}
-structure on
{\sf Cylr}\bigl(X_{0}\leftarrow\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\bigr)\to\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
that restricts to the
\sfr_{n}
-structure on
\Link_{X_{0}}(X)\to K
.
Composition in the
(\oo,1)
-category
\Mfd^{\sfr}_{n}
endows this extension
\widetilde{X}\to\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{1})
with a
\sfr_{n}
-structure extending the given one over
\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{\{1\}})
.
We have exhibited a sought lift (
22
).

We lastly show that every factorization is equivalent to that constructed above.
Let
F\rightarrow\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K\times\Delta^{1})
be a constructible bundle together a
\sfr_{n}
-structure on it and identifications over
\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
, defining another such lift (
22
).
This is classified by a diagram in
\Fun\bigl(\exit(K),\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\bigr)
,

\textstyle{F_{|K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\emb}
\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf cls.crt}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{X}}
\textstyle{X_{|K},}

in which
X_{0}
is understood
as the constant functor
\exit(K)\rightarrow\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}
valued at
X_{0}
.
By taking iterated links, we obtain a diagram in
\Fun\bigl(\exit(K),\Bun\bigr)
:

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Link_{X_{0}}(F_{|K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(3)}
\scriptstyle{\sf cls.crt}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\scriptstyle{\sf open}
\textstyle{F_{|K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\emb}
\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf cls.crt}
\scriptstyle{\sf cls.crt}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Link_{\Link_{X_{0}}(F_{|K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}})}\bigl(\Link_{X_{0}}(F_{|K\times\Delta^{1}})\bigr)}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\scriptstyle{\sf open}
\textstyle{\Link_{X_{0}}(X_{|K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\scriptstyle{\sf open}
\textstyle{X_{|K}.}

As argued above, this diagram admits a unique lift to one in
\Fun\bigl(\exit(K),\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\bigr)
, extending the given lift of
c_{1}\to\Fun\bigl(\exit(K),\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\bigr)
classifying
X\to\overline{\mathsf{C}}(K)
.
Also as argued above, using that each stratum of
M=X_{0}
is both open and closed, the construction of links gives that the horizontal open morphisms labeled as (1) must be embedding morphisms.
Furthermore, the assumption that the morphism
F_{|K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}}\to X_{|K}
in
\Fun\bigl(\exit(K),\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr})
is by embedding morphisms gives that the top horizontal morphism is in fact an equivalence.
Since the morphism (2) factors a closed-creation through a closed-creation, it too must be an equivalence.
This implies the closed-creation morphism (3) must be an equivalence.
We conclude an equivalence in
\Fun\bigl(\exit(K),\Mfd_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\bigr)
,

\Link_{X_{0}}(X_{|K})~\simeq~F_{|K\times\Delta^{\{0\}}}~,

under the constant functor at
X_{0}
and over the restriction of the given functor classifying
X_{|K}
.

∎

We now have the following explicit description of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
.
Recall the notation
\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(V)
for the free
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra on an object
V
.

Corollary 2.23
.

The object and morphism spaces of the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
are

\Obj\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\bigr)\simeq\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(\Omega\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{\n 1})

and

\Mor\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\bigr)\simeq\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])~.

Proof.

Combining Definition
2.20
and Lemma
2.22
, we have the equivalences

\Obj\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\bigr):=\Map_{\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\bigr)\simeq\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})

and

\Mor\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\bigr):=\Map_{\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])~.

It only remains to establish the equivalence

\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(\Omega\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{\n 1})~.

To see this, recall from Remark
2.1
that the space of
n
-framings on a finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
is

(\Omega\Gr_{1}(n))^{S}=(\Omega\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{\n 1})^{S}
.
Consequently, there is a canonical map
\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(\Omega\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{\n 1})\rightarrow\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
which is immediately seen to be an equivalence.
∎

2.4.
Tangles in 2-dimensional space, and the categorical delooping
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})

The case of the monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is of particular importance in our argument, because the Tangle Hypothesis in 2-dimensional ambient space forms the base case of the induction on the ambient dimension in §5.

Proposition 2.24
.

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is a monoidal category.
That is, the monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
has discrete mapping spaces, and the natural map from its space of objects to its space of isomorphisms

\obj\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\bigr)\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})^{\sim}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Given 2-framed configurations
S_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
and
S_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
, the space of morphisms
\Map_{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})}(S_{0},S_{1})
is the fiber over
S_{0}\amalg S_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}[1]
of the map

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])\rightarrow\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}[1])~.

To show that this fiber is discrete, it suffices to show that each of these spaces is discrete. This, in turn, follows from showing that both the spaces
\Emb^{\fr}(W,\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])_{/\Diff^{\fr}(W)}
and
\Emb^{\fr}(S,\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}[1])_{/\Diff^{\fr}(S)}
are discrete for all 2-framed 1-dimensional and 0-dimensional manifolds
W
and
S
, respectively. Note first that for
W\cong S^{1}
, the space
\Emb^{\fr}(S^{1},\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])
is empty: The Gauss map for any embedding
S^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1]
is not nullhomotopic (being twice the generator of
\pi_{1}\Gr_{1}(2)\cong\mathbb{Z}
), so there does not exist a trivialization of this Gauss map. For
W\cong\mathbb{D}^{1}
, it is a classical result
(implied by a theorem of Smale
[
Sm
]
)
that the space
\Emb(\mathbb{D}^{1},\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])
is discrete. The spaces of framings are likewise discrete, by the equivalence
\Omega\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}\simeq\mathbb{Z}
.

We lastly show univalent-completeness. The inclusion of identity morphisms into isomorphisms is given by the map

\Tang_{0}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\longrightarrow\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])

assigning a set
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
to the straight-line tangles
S\times\mathbb{D}[1]\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1]
. Again by discreteness of the space
\Emb(\mathbb{D}^{1},\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[1])
, it follows that this inclusion is an monomorphism and the essential image consists of untangles—which are the isomorphisms in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
.
∎

Recall that given a monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{C}
, there exists a pointed
(\oo,2)
-category
\ast\in\mathfrak{B}\mathcal{C}
, the categorical deloop of
\mathcal{C}
.
It is presented by the simplicial
(\infty,1)
-category

{\sf Bar}_{\bullet}\mathcal{C}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Cat_{(\infty,1)}~.

Delooping determines a functor between
(\infty,1)
-categories

(23)

\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\xrightarrow{~\mathfrak{B}~}\Cat_{(\infty,2)}^{\ast/}

from monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories to pointed
(\infty,2)
-categories. This functor is a fully-faithful with image consisting of those pointed
(\infty,2)
-categories
\ast\in\mathcal{C}
for which the morphism
\ast\to\mathcal{C}
is surjective on objects.
Furthermore, the deloop
\mathfrak{B}\mathcal{C}
is uniquely characterized by the following:

•

the datum of an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\Omega\mathfrak{B}\mathcal{C}
of monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories, where
\Omega\mathfrak{B}\mathcal{C}
is the limit of the diagram
\ast\rightarrow\mathfrak{B}\mathcal{C}\leftarrow\ast
with monoidal structure given by composition of loops;

•

the condition that the underlying
\oo
-groupoid category of
\mathfrak{B}\mathcal{C}
is connected.

We now give a convenient geometric description of
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
, which we identify with the categorical deloop in Lemma
2.28
.
In §3.8 of
[
AFR1
]
, we constructed a functor

\bTheta_{n}^{\op}\xrightarrow{~\langle-\rangle~}\Mfd^{\sfr}_{n}

whose value on
T
is the stratified space
\langle T\rangle
depicting the pasting diagram of
T
– it is a naturally subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, from which it inherits a solid n-framing. This allows for the following definition. Recall first that
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
is a pointed
(\oo,1)
-category, the zero-object of which is given by the empty set
\emptyset
.

Definition 2.25
.

\mathfrak{B}^{\n 1}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is the pointed presheaf on
\bTheta_{n}
given by the composite functor

\bTheta_{n}^{\op}\xrightarrow{~\langle-\rangle~}\Mfd^{\sfr}_{n}\xrightarrow{~\Map_{\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},-)~}\Spaces_{\ast}

whose distinguished point
\emptyset\in\mathfrak{B}^{\n 1}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})(T)
is given by the empty tangle, which is the composite
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\rightarrow\emptyset\rightarrow\langle T\rangle
in
\Mfd_{n}^{\sfr}
.

Observation 2.26
.

By Lemma
2.22
, for
T\in\bTheta_{n}
, the space

\mathfrak{B}^{\n 1}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})(T)~\simeq~\left\{~\left(~W\subset\langle T\rangle~,~\varphi~\right)~\right\}

is a moduli space of codimension-
(\n 1)
properly embedded tangles
W\subset\langle T\rangle
equipped with a trivialization
\varphi
of the Gauss map
W\xrightarrow{\tau_{W}}{\sf Gr}_{1}(n)
defined by the solid
n
-framing of
\langle T\rangle\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 2.27
.

\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is a pointed
(\oo,2)
-category.
That is, the
\bTheta_{2}
-presheaf
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
belongs to the full
\oo
-subcategory
\Cat_{(\infty,2)}^{\ast/}\subset\Fun(\bTheta_{2}^{\op},\Spaces_{\ast})

Proof.

The functor
\langle-\rangle
sends Segal covers to closed covers, and
\Map_{\Mfd_{2}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{1},-)
is a closed sheaf. Consequently, the composite functor satisfies the Segal condition and therefore presents an
(\oo,2)
-category.
∎

Lemma 2.28
.

The pointed
(\oo,2)
-category
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is the categorical deloop of the monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
.

Proof.

Consider the functor
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{1}\xrightarrow{~(-)^{\geq 1}~}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{[1]}
given by
D\mapsto D^{\geq 1}:=D\smallsetminus\sk_{0}(D)
, deletion of the 0-dimensional strata.
The open embedding
D^{\geq 1}\hookrightarrow D
for each object
D\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}^{\sfr}_{1}
determines a natural transformation
(-)^{\geq 1}\to\id
.
Consider the wreath functor
\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\wr}\bTheta_{2}
, which is given by
\left([p],[q]\right)\mapsto[p]\wr[q]:=[p]\left([q],\dots,[q]\right)
.

Observe that the natural transformation
(-)^{\geq 1}\to\id
above determines a lax-commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\langle-\rangle^{\geq 1}\times\id}
\textstyle{\bTheta^{\op}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle-\rangle}
\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1]}^{\sfr}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id\times\langle-\rangle}
\textstyle{\Mfd_{2}^{\sfr}.}

Now, the emptyset is the only 1-tangle in a 0-dimensional manifold.
Consequently, for
X
a solidly 2-framed stratified space, each tangle
W\subset X
necessarily avoids the 0-dimensional strata of
X
:

W~\subset~X^{\geq 1}~:=~X\smallsetminus\sk_{0}(X)~.

The open embedding
X^{\geq 1}\hookrightarrow X
thereby determines an equivalence between spaces of framed tangles:

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(X^{\geq 1})\simeq\Map_{\Mfd_{2}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{1},X^{\geq 1})\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Map_{\Mfd_{2}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{1},X)\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(X)~.

In particular, the canonical map

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})(\langle[p]\rangle^{\geq 1}\times\langle[q]\rangle)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})(\langle[p]\wr[q]\rangle)

is an equivalence.
In this way, we achieve commutativity of the outer diagram of space-valued functors on
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

\textstyle{\bTheta^{\op}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle-\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\langle-\rangle^{\geq 1}\times\id}
\textstyle{\Uparrow}
\textstyle{\Mfd_{2}^{\sfr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Map_{\Mfd_{2}^{\sfr}}(\mathbb{R}^{1},-)}
\textstyle{\Spaces}
\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[1]}^{\sfr}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\id\times\langle-\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})}

Finally, we note that the bottom composite

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\circ(\langle-\rangle^{\geq 1}\times\id)={\sf Bar}_{\bullet}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})

is the bar construction of the monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
. This identifies the top composite as the categorical deloop of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
.
∎

Recall from
[
BSP
]
that an
(\oo,2)
-category
\mathcal{C}
is gaunt if, for each
0\leq k\leq 2
, the space of
k
-morphisms
\mathcal{C}(c_{k})
is discrete.

Proposition 2.29
.

\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is a gaunt
(\oo,2)
-category.

Proof.

We prove that
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})(c_{k})
is a discrete space for
0\leq k\leq 2
.
By Observationn
2.26
, the space of
k
-morphisms of
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
can be expressed as a coproduct over diffeomorphism classes of
({\mathit{k}\text{-}1})
-manifolds with boundary:

\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})(c_{k})\simeq\coprod_{[W]}\Emb^{\fr}(W,\mathbb{D}^{k})_{/\Diff(W)}\simeq\coprod_{[W]}{\sf fiber}_{\ast}\Bigl(\Emb(W,\mathbb{D}^{k})\rightarrow\Map(W,\Gr_{1}(2))\Bigr)_{/\Diff(W)}~.

For the
[W]
-component to be nonempty, there must exist a trivialization of the Gauss map of an embedding
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{k}
, which disallows the case of
W\cong S^{1}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
. Consequently,
W
is diffeomorphic to a finite disjoint union of disks
\mathbb{D}^{{\mathit{k}\text{-}1}}
.
Since
k\leq 2
, the space
\Emb(W,\mathbb{D}^{k})
is discrete, and the space
\Map(W,\Gr_{1}(2))
is a 1-type. Consequently, the fiber is discrete. Again since
k\leq 2
, the diffeomorphism group
\Diff(W)
is equivalent to a symmetric group if
W
is 0-dimensional, or a product of the symmetric group on
\pi_{0}W
and
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{\pi_{0}W}
if
W
is 1-dimensional. In particular, it is discrete. Further, by inspection the diffeomorphism group
\Diff(W)\simeq\Sigma_{\pi_{0}W}\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{\pi_{0}W}
acts freely on the above fiber, which implies that
{\sf fiber}_{\ast}\bigl(\Emb(W,\mathbb{D}^{k})\rightarrow\Map(W,\Gr_{1}(2))\bigr)_{/\Diff(W)}
is again discrete. This implies that
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})(c_{k})
is discrete, completing the proof.
∎

3.
Segaling

In this section, we construct a functor
\Seg:\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
for
\mathcal{X}
a presentable
(\oo,1)
-category in which colimits are universal – see Definition
3.25
. The main result of this section is Lemma
3.32
, which states that if
\mathcal{C}\in\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
is such that
\Seg(\mathcal{C})
is Segal, then
\Seg(\mathcal{C})
is the Segal completion of
\mathcal{C}
.

In this section, we use the following.

Notation 3.1
.

For
a\in\mathcal{A}
an object in an
(\infty,1)
-category,

a^{\circ}\in\mathcal{A}^{\op}
is the corresponding object in the opposite
(\infty,1)
-category.

Definition 3.2
.

The category
\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
is the full subcategory of
\Ar(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
whose objects are closed morphisms
A^{\circ}\rightarrow C^{\circ}
, i.e., those morphisms such that
C\rightarrow A
is a convex inclusion.

We forthwith regard
\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
as a category over
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
via the source-evaluation functor
\ev_{s}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\xrightarrow{\ev_{s}}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
is a Cartesian fibration, with monodromy given by convex-hull of image. In particular,
\ev_{s}
is an exponentiable fibration.

Proof.

We define a functor
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}-}:\mathbf{\Delta}\rightarrow\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
. This sends
A\in\mathbf{\Delta}
to the category
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}
of closed morphisms
C\subset A
. To a map
f:A\rightarrow B
, we assign the covariant functor
{\sf Hull}(f):\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}B}
which sends
C\subset A
to

{\sf Hull}(f)(C\subset A):=\bigl({\sf Hull}(f(C))\subset B\bigr)

the convex hull of the image
f(C)\subset B
.
The unstraightening of this functor,
\Un(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}-})\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}
, is a coCartesian fibration, and its opposite
\Un(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}-})^{\op}\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
is a Cartesian fibration. We claim there is an equivalence
\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta})\simeq\Un(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}-})
, which will prove the result after further noting the equivalence
\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta})^{\op}\cong\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
. By the factorization system on morphisms in an unstraightening, a morphism in
\Un(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}-})
from
(C_{1}\subset A_{1})
to
(C_{0}\subset A_{0})
consists of a morphism
f:A_{1}\rightarrow A_{0}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
and a morphism
{\sf Hull}(f)(C_{1}\subset A_{1})\rightarrow C_{0}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}
. However, there exists such a morphism
{\sf Hull}(f)(C_{1}\subset A_{1})\rightarrow C_{0}
if and only if the image
f(C_{1})\subset A_{0}
is contained in the image
C_{0}\subset A_{0}
. Since
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}
is equivalent to a poset, this morphism
{\sf Hull}(f)(C_{1}\subset A_{1})\rightarrow C_{0}
is unique if it exists. Consequently, a morphism from
(C_{1}\subset A_{1})
to
(C_{0}\subset A_{0})
in
\Un(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}-})
is uniquely determined by a commutative diagram

\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cls}
\textstyle{C_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cls}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{0}}

in
\mathbf{\Delta}
. This is exactly a morphism in
\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta})
from
(C_{1}\subset A_{1})
to
(C_{0}\subset A_{0})
.
∎

For
\mathcal{K}
an
(\infty,1)
-category, recall from §
0.1

the
(\infty,1)
-category
\TwAro(\mathcal{K})
.
Source-evaluation and target-evaluation define functors

\mathcal{A}\xleftarrow{~\ev_{s}~}\TwAro(\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{~\ev_{t}~}\mathcal{A}^{\op}~.

Consider the fiber product

\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})~.

Note that an object of the fiber product consists of a pair
(A^{\circ}\rightarrow B^{\circ})\in\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
and
(C\subset A)^{\circ}\in\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
, where
C\subset A
is a convex inclusion (or equivalently,
A^{\circ}\rightarrow C^{\circ}
is a closed morphism). A morphism

\bigl(A_{0}^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{0}}B_{0}^{\circ},(C_{0}\subset A_{0})^{\circ}\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigl(A_{1}^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{1}}B_{1}^{\circ},(C_{1}\subset A_{1})^{\circ}\bigr)

consists of a morphism in
\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
given by a commutative diagram in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

\textstyle{A_{0}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\circ}}
\textstyle{A_{1}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{0}^{\circ}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B_{1}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{g^{\circ}}

and a morphism in
\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
given by a commutative diagram in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

\textstyle{A_{0}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cls}
\scriptstyle{f^{\circ}}
\textstyle{A_{1}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cls}
\textstyle{C_{0}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}^{\circ}}

which is the same as a morphism
f:A_{1}\rightarrow A_{0}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
for which the image
f(C_{1})
is contained in the image
C_{0}\subset A_{0}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

There exists a functor

\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\overset{S}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

uniquely determined by the following values:

•

On objects,
S
sends
\bigl(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi}B^{\circ},(C\subset A)^{\circ}\bigr)
to the convex subset of
A
:

C^{\varphi}:=\Bigl[{\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi(b)\big|\ \varphi(b)\leq{\sf min}(C)\bigr\},{\sf inf}\bigl\{\varphi(b)\big|\ {\sf max}(C)\leq\varphi(b)\bigr\}\Bigr]~.

Here we use the convention
{\sf sup}(\emptyset):={\sf min}(A)
and
{\sf inf}(\emptyset):={\sf max}(A)
.

•

On morphisms,
S
sends a morphism given by the opposite of

\textstyle{C_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cls}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cls}
\textstyle{A_{0}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}

to (the opposite of) the map in
\mathbf{\Delta}
given by the restriction of
f
:

\textstyle{C_{1}^{\varphi_{1}}:=\Bigl[{\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ \varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(C_{1})\bigr\},{\sf inf}\bigl\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ {\sf max}(C_{1})\leq\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\bigr\}\Bigr]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{|}}
\textstyle{C_{0}^{\varphi_{0}}:=\Bigl[{\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\big|\ \varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\bigr\},{\sf inf}\bigl\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\big|\ {\sf max}(C_{0})\leq\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\bigr\}\Bigr]}

Proof.

To show that
S
is well-defined, it suffices to verify the inequalities

f\Bigl({\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ \varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(C_{1})\bigr\}\Bigr)\geq{\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\big|\ \varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\bigr\}

and

f\Bigl({\sf inf}\bigl\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ {\sf max}(C_{1})\leq\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\bigr\}\Bigr)\leq{\sf inf}\bigl\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\big|\ {\sf max}(C_{0})\leq\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\bigr\}~.

We only verify the first inequality; the verification of the second is identical.
Note the inequalities

f\Bigl({\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ \varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(C_{1})\bigr\}\Bigr)\geq{\sf sup}\bigl\{f\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ f\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\bigr\}\geq{\sf sup}\bigl\{f\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ f\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\bigr\}

where the first follows from
f{\sf sup}\geq{\sf sup}f
, and the second follows from the inequality
{\sf min}(C_{0})\leq f({\sf min}(C_{1}))
.
We have the further inequality

{\sf sup}\bigl\{f\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\big|\ f\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\bigr\}\geq{\sf sup}\bigl\{f\varphi_{1}g(b_{0})\big|\ f\varphi_{1}g(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\bigr\}={\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\big|\ \varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\bigr\}

since
\varphi_{0}(b_{0})=f\varphi_{1}g(b_{0})
. Composing inequalities gives the result.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

Note that the construction of
S
determines, for each map
B\xrightarrow{\varphi}A
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, an endofunctor

\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}

sending
C\subset A
to
C^{\varphi}\subset A
. Note the natural containment
C\subset C^{\varphi}\subset A
, for all
C\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}
.

Observation 3.6
.

We give an explicit presentation of the values of the functor
\varphi
of Remark
3.5
. Given
\varphi^{\circ}:[a]^{\circ}\rightarrow[b]^{\circ}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
and
0<i\leq a
, we have

\{i-1<i\}^{\varphi}=\begin{cases}[0,\varphi(0)]&\text{if $i\leq\varphi(0)$}\\
[\varphi(b),a]&\text{if $\varphi(b)\leq i-1$}\\
[\varphi(j-1),\varphi(j)]&\text{if $\varphi(j-1)\leq i-1<i\leq\varphi(j)$}\end{cases}

and

\{i\}^{\varphi}=\begin{cases}[0,\varphi(0)]&\text{if $i<\varphi(0)$}\\
[\varphi(b),a]&\text{if $\varphi(b)<i$}\\
[\varphi(j-1),\varphi(j)]&\text{if $\varphi(j-1)<i<\varphi(j)$}\\
\{\varphi(j)\}&\text{if $i=\varphi(j)$}\end{cases}

Since
\ev_{s}:\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
is an exponentiable fibration by Lemma
3.3
, this guarantees existence of the relative functor
(\oo,1)
-category
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
.
See Definition 1.7 of
[
AF5
]
.
Recall that
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
is an
(\infty,1)
-category over
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
characterized as follows:
for each
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{K}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
over
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
, a lift

\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}

is the datum of a functor

\mathcal{K}\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}~.

Observation 3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathcal{B}
be an exponentiable fibration, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(\oo,1)
-category. An object
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{E},\underline{\mathcal{X}})
is a pair
(b\in\mathcal{B},\mathcal{E}_{|b}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{X})
. For
(b_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})
and
(b_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1})
objects of
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{E},\underline{\mathcal{X}})
, a morphism
(b_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})\rightarrow(b_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1})
consists of a morphism
f:b_{0}\rightarrow b_{1}
in
\mathcal{B}
together with a filler

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{|b_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{|f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{|b_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where
\mathcal{E}_{|f}=\mathcal{E}\underset{\mathcal{B}}{\times}[1]
is the pullback of
\mathcal{E}
along the functor
[1]\xrightarrow{\langle f\rangle}\mathcal{B}
classifying the morphism
f:b_{0}\rightarrow b_{1}
.

Observation 3.8
.

If
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a Cartesian fibration, then there is an equivalence over
[1]

\textstyle{\Cylr(\mathcal{E}_{|b_{0}}\xleftarrow{f^{\ast}}\mathcal{E}_{|b_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{|f}}
\textstyle{[1]}

between the pullback of
\mathcal{E}
along
[1]\xrightarrow{\langle f\rangle}\mathcal{B}
, and the right-cylinder of the Cartesian monodromy functor
f^{\ast}
. Consequently a morphism
(b_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})\rightarrow(b_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1})
is equivalent to a natural transformation
\Phi
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{|b_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{0}}
\textstyle{\Phi\Downarrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{|b_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{1}}

Observation 3.9
.

If
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a Cartesian fibration, then
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{E},\underline{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a coCartesian fibration. Furthermore, the coCartesian monodromy functor over
f:b_{0}\rightarrow b_{1}
is given by precomposition with the Cartesian monodromy functor
f^{\ast}
associated to
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
:

\Fun(\mathcal{E}_{|b_{0}},\mathcal{X})\xrightarrow{(-\circ f^{\ast})}\Fun(\mathcal{E}_{|b_{1}},\mathcal{X})~.

Definition 3.10
.

The functor

\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}

is adjoint to the functor

\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\xrightarrow{S}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

constructed in Lemma
3.4
.

Observation 3.11
.

On objects, the functor
S:\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\rightarrow\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})
sends
(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ})\in\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
to the pair
\bigl(A^{\circ},(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\bigr)
, where
\varphi
is the functor defined as the composite

\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}

sending
(C\subset A)^{\circ}
to
C^{\varphi}
as in Remark
3.5
. To a morphism
(f,g):(A_{0}^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{0}}B_{0}^{\circ})\rightarrow(A_{1}^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{1}}B_{1}^{\circ})
, defined by a commutative diagram in
\mathbf{\Delta}

\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{A_{0}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B_{0}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}

the functor
S
assigns the morphism in
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})
(see Observation
3.8
) defined by the natural transformation

\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{\Downarrow}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{1}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf Hull}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}

which to
C_{1}\subset A_{1}
assigns the morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
given by the opposite of the map

f_{|}:C_{1}^{\varphi_{1}}\rightarrow{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))^{\varphi_{0}}~.

This is well-defined, by Lemma
3.4
.

Definition 3.12
.

The functor

\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{\sf LIM}\mathcal{X}

assigns to a
\mathcal{K}
-point
\mathcal{F}
of
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
, given by

\mathcal{K}\xrightarrow{\bigl\langle\mathcal{K}\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{X}\bigr\rangle}\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})~,

the
\mathcal{K}
-point of
\mathcal{X}
defined by the composite functor

\mathcal{K}\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{X}

where
\mathcal{K}\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})

is the pullback along
\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
of the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\rightarrow\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
given by identity morphisms: i.e.,
A^{\circ}
is sent to identity morphism
A^{\circ}\rightarrow A^{\circ}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

The left Kan extension

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf LIM}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ev_{s})_{!}({\sf LIM}\circ S)}

is equivalent to the identity functor:
(\ev_{s})_{!}({\sf LIM}\circ S)\simeq{\sf id}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}
.
Furthermore, this diagram commutes.

Proof.

Let
\varphi^{\circ}:A^{\circ}\rightarrow B^{\circ}
be an object of
\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
. We claim that the value of
{\sf LIM}\circ S
on this object is

({\sf LIM}\circ S)(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ})=A^{\circ}~.

To see this, recall first that the value
S(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ})\in\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})
is given by the pair
\bigl(A^{\circ},(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\bigr)
. Consequently, the object
({\sf LIM}\circ S)(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ})
is given by the composite

\ast\longrightarrow(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

where
\ast\rightarrow(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}
selects the identity morphism
A^{\circ}\rightarrow A^{\circ}
. Consequently, we obtain

({\sf LIM}\circ S)(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ})=(A^{\varphi})^{\circ}

and (recall Lemma
3.4
) we have the further simplification:

A^{\varphi}=\Bigl[{\sf sup}\bigl\{\varphi(b)\big|\ \varphi(b)\leq{\sf min}(A)\bigr\},{\sf inf}\bigl\{\varphi(b)\big|\ {\sf max}(A)\leq\varphi(b)\bigr\}\Bigr]=[{\sf min}(A),{\sf max}(A)]=A~.

Consequently, the value
(\ev_{s})_{!}({\sf LIM}\circ S)(A^{\circ})
is the colimit of the constant functor

\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}_{/A^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

taking constant value
A^{\circ}
on every object
(A^{\circ}\rightarrow B^{\circ})
. Since
\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\underset{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}{\times}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}_{/A^{\circ}}
is a connected category and
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
is discrete, the value of this colimit is
A^{\circ}
.

To finish, inspection of the functor
{\sf LIM}
reveals that the diagram commutes.
∎

Corollary 3.14
.

For
\mathcal{C}:\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, there is a canonical commutative diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf LIM}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}

which witnesses
\mathcal{C}
as the left Kan extension
(\ev_{s})_{!}({\sf LIM}\circ\mathcal{C}\circ S)

Proof.

By Lemma
3.13
and the construction of
{\sf LIM}
in Definition
3.12
, the diagram commutes. Also,
\ev_{s}
is a Cartesian fibration whose fibers have contractible classifying spaces, therefore it is a localization (by Corollary 3.20 of
[
AF5
]
, using that Cartesian fibrations are right-initial). Lastly, we observe that a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C}

automatically witnesses
C
as the left Kan extension
G_{!}F
if
G
is a localization. The result follows.
∎

Definition 3.15
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(\oo,1)
-category. The Morita
(\oo,1)
-category of
\mathcal{X}

\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\subset\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})

is the full
\oo
-subcategory of the relative functors consisting of those objects
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A})^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{X}
which send Segal covers to limits.

Remark 3.16
.

If
\mathcal{X}
has finite limits, then the
(\oo,1)
-category
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
is the unstraightening of a functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{{\sf Morita}[\mathcal{X}]}\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
,
similar to the developments in
[
Hau1
]
and
[
Hau2
]
.

Observation 3.17
.

The functor
S
of Definition
3.10
factors through
\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]
:

\textstyle{\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}).}

Observation 3.18
.

Given a functor
\mathcal{C}:\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
such that the restriction
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\cls,\op}\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{X}
carry Segal covers to limits, there is a factorization

\textstyle{\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}}).}

Notation 3.19
.

For
A^{\circ}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
,

b_{A}\colon(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}A})^{\op}\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A})^{\op}

is the inclusion of the full subcategory consisting of those
A^{\circ}\rightarrow[p]^{\circ}
with
p\leq 1
.

Lemma 3.20
.

For
(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})\in\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
, then
(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})
belongs to the
\oo
-subcategory
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
if and only if the natural map

\mathcal{F}\rightarrow b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Consider a convex inclusion
[p]\subset A
defining an object of
\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}
. We prove that
\mathcal{F}[p]\rightarrow(b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F})[p]
is an equivalence. The right Kan extension
b_{A\ast}
is computed by the limit of the functor
b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}:(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}[p]})^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, which is exactly the iterated fiber product
\mathcal{F}(\{0<1\})\times_{\mathcal{F}(\{0\})}\ldots\times_{\mathcal{F}(\{p-1\})}\mathcal{F}(\{p-1<p\})
. We thus see that
\mathcal{F}
is Segal if and only if it computed by the right Kan extension from closed morphisms.
∎

Definition 3.21
.

The
\oo
-subcategory
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]\subset\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
consists of those morphisms
(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ},\eta)

\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}B}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf Hull}(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow\eta}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{B}}

such that the canonical morphism

b_{B}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(\varphi)^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{A}\longrightarrow b_{B}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(\varphi)^{\ast}b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{A}

is an equivalence.

Lemma 3.22
.

There is an inclusion
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\subset\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]
of
\oo
-subcategories of
\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.20
, an object
(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F}_{A})\in\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})
belongs to
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
if and only
\mathcal{F}_{A}\rightarrow b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{A}
is an equivalence, from which the result immediately follows.
∎

Lemma 3.23
.

For
\mathcal{C}:\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
a simplicial object in
\mathcal{X}
, the essential image of the composite functor
\mathcal{C}\circ S
lies in
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]
. If
\mathcal{C}
is Segal, then the essential image of
\mathcal{C}\circ S
lies in
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
. That is, there are unique factorizations:

\textstyle{\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{\exists~{\rm if}~\mathcal{C}\ {\rm Segal}}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\sf rel}_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}(\Ar^{\cls}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}),\underline{\mathcal{X}})}

Proof.

The factorization for
\mathcal{C}
Segal follows from Observation
3.17
and Observation
3.18
. It remains to prove the first factorization, that for any simplicial object
\mathcal{C}
the composite
\mathcal{C}\circ S
belongs to
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]
. That is, we show that for any morphism in
\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
, the image under
\mathcal{C}\circ S
satisfies the condition of Definition
3.21
. Let
f=(f,g)
be such a morphism, consisting of a commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{0}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\circ}}
\textstyle{A_{1}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}^{\circ}}
\textstyle{B_{0}^{\circ}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B_{1}^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{g^{\circ}}

in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
. The functor
\mathcal{C}\circ S
carries this morphism to the natural transformation
\Phi_{f}

\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\Phi_{f}\Downarrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{1}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\sf Hull}(f)}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{1}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}

where
\Phi_{f}
takes the value, for each
C_{1}\subset A_{1}
a convex subset, as the morphism in
\mathcal{X}
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\Bigl({\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))^{\varphi_{0}}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\Bigl(\bigl[{\sf sup}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq f({\sf min}(C_{1}))\},{\sf inf}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0}\geq f({\sf max}(C_{1}))\}\bigr]^{\circ}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}(f_{|}^{\circ})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(C_{1}^{\varphi_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\Bigl(\bigl[{\sf sup}\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})|\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\leq{\sf min}(C_{1})\},{\sf inf}\{\varphi_{1}(b_{1})|\varphi_{1}(b_{1})\geq{\sf max}(C_{1})\}\bigr]^{\circ}\Bigr)}

Denote by
\mathcal{F}
the top horizontal composite:
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{C}\circ{\rm forget}\circ\varphi_{0}
. Let
C_{1}\subset A_{1}
be an object of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}A_{1}}
. Then

b_{A_{1}}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(f)^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(C_{1}^{\circ})\simeq\mathcal{C}\Bigl(\bigl[{\sf sup}\{\varphi_{0}|\varphi_{1}(b_{0})\leq f(C_{1})\},{\sf inf}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\geq f(C_{1})\}\bigr]^{\circ}\Bigr)

while

b_{A_{1}}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(f)^{\ast}b_{A_{0}\ast}b_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(C_{1}^{\circ})\simeq b_{A_{0}\ast}b_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\bigl({\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))^{\circ}\bigr)

\simeq{\sf lim}\Bigl(\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}\bigr)^{\op}\hookrightarrow\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{0}}\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\rm forget}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{X}\Bigr)

is the limit of the composite functor which is given by

(C_{0}\subset{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1})))^{\circ}\mapsto\mathcal{C}\Bigl(\bigl[{\sf sup}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(C_{0})\},{\sf inf}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\geq{\sf max}(C_{0})\}\bigr]\Bigr)~.

We show that the canonical morphism in
\mathcal{X}
defined by the unit of the adjunction
(b_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\dashv b_{A_{0}\ast})

b_{A_{1}}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(f)^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(C_{1}^{\circ})\xrightarrow{\sf unit}b_{A_{1}}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(f)^{\ast}b_{A_{0}\ast}b_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(C_{1}^{\circ})

is an equivalence, again for
(C_{1}\subset A_{1})\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}A_{1}}
. To establish this, we claim that there is a localization functor

\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}\overset{Q}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}f(C_{1})}

together with a factorization

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q^{\op}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}f(C_{1})}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces~.}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}

Here, the functor
Q
is defined to take values on
I\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}

Q(I)=\begin{cases}\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{if $I=\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}$}\\
\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{if $I=\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}$}\\
f(C_{1})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}~.

Let
I\subset{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))
be an object of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}
. Observe the following containments of subsets of
A_{0}
:

\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})|{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))<\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(I)\}\overset{(1)}{\subset}\Bigl({\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))\smallsetminus\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}\Bigr)\cap\varphi_{0}(B_{0})

\overset{(2)}{\subset}\Bigl({\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))\smallsetminus\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}\Bigr)\cap f(A_{1})\overset{(3)}{\subset}\Bigl(f(C_{1}))\smallsetminus\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}\Bigr)\overset{(4)}{\subset}\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}

where containment (1) is immediate from the definition of the hull; (2) follows from the containment
\varphi_{0}(B_{0})\subset f(A_{1})
; (3) follows from the equality
{\sf Hull}(f(S))\cap f(A_{1})=f(S)
, for any subset
S\subset A_{1}
; and (4) follows from the assumption on the cardinality
1\leq|C_{1}|\leq 2
.

Therefore:

{\sf sup}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(I)\}=\begin{cases}{\sf max}(C_{1})&\text{${\sf max}(C_{1})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})$ and $I=\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}$ }\\
{\sf sup}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\leq{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and similarly

{\sf inf}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\geq{\sf max}(I)\}=\begin{cases}{\sf min}(C_{1})&\text{${\sf min}(C_{1})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})$ and $I=\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}$}\\
{\sf inf}\{\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})|\varphi_{0}(b_{0})\geq{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Therefore,

I^{\varphi_{0}}=\begin{cases}\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{if ${\sf max}(C_{1})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})$ and $I=\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}$ }\\
\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{if ${\sf min}(C_{1})\in\varphi_{0}(B_{0})$ and $I=\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}$ }\\
f(C_{1})^{\varphi_{0}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}~.

This shows the factorization through
Q:\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}\rightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}f(C_{1})}
. We now show that
Q
is a localization.
The source
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}
is equivalent to the category

\Bigl\{\{h_{0}\}\rightarrow\{h_{0}<h_{1}\}\leftarrow\{h_{1}\}\rightarrow\{h_{1}<h_{2}\}\leftarrow\ldots\rightarrow\{h_{q-1}<h_{q}\}\leftarrow\{h_{q}\}\Bigr\}

where
h_{0}={\sf min}(f(C_{1}))
and
h_{q}={\sf max}(f(C_{1}))
. The functor
Q
is then given by:

Q(\{h_{i-1}<h_{i}\})=f(C_{1})\\

Q(\{h_{i}\})=\begin{cases}\{{\sf min}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{if $k=0$}\\
\{{\sf max}(f(C_{1}))\}&\text{if $k=q$}\\
f(C_{1})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

And so
Q
witnesses a localization with respect to the set of morphisms whose source is not
\{h_{0}\}
or
\{h_{q}\}
. Since localizations are final and initial, we obtain in particular that the functor
Q^{\op}
is initial. We thus obtain that the canonical morphism

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(f(C_{1})^{\varphi_{0}})\simeq{\sf lim}\Bigl(\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}f(C_{1})}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{0}}\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{X}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\sf lim}\Bigl(\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\leq 1}_{/^{\cls}{\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{0}}\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A_{0}}\bigr)^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{X}\Bigr)\simeq b_{A_{0}\ast}b_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\bigl({\sf Hull}(f(C_{1}))^{\circ}\bigr)}

is an equivalence. That is, the unit morphism

\mathcal{C}(f(C_{1})^{\varphi_{0}})\simeq b_{A_{1}}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(f)^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(C_{1}^{\circ})\rightarrow b_{A_{1}}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(f)^{\ast}b_{A_{0}\ast}b_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}(C_{1}^{\circ})\simeq\mathcal{C}(f(C_{1})^{\varphi_{0}})

is an equivalence, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.24
.

If
\mathcal{X}
has finite limits, the inclusion
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\rightarrow\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]
admits a left adjoint
\mathsf{L}
, which commutes with projection to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
.

Proof.

Let
(A^{\circ},(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A})^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{X})
be an object of
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]
.
By Lemma 2.17 of
[
AF5
]
, it suffices to show
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]^{(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})/}
has an initial object,
\mathsf{L}(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})
.
Define
\mathsf{L}(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F}):=(A^{\circ},(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A})^{\op}\xrightarrow{b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{X})
. This object exists, since
\mathcal{X}
has finite limits. By the equivalence
b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\simeq{\sf id}_{(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}A})^{\op}}
, we have

b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\simeq b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}b_{A\ast}b_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}~.

Therefore
\mathsf{L}(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})
is an object of
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
. By construction, there is a canonical morphism
(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\mathsf{L}(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})
over
{\sf id}_{A^{\circ}}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
. Therefore, we have defined an object
\mathsf{L}(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})\in\Morita[\mathcal{X}]^{(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})/}
.

We now show that the object
\mathsf{L}(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})
is initial in
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]^{(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})/}
. So let

(A^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}B^{\circ},(\mathbf{\Delta}_{/^{\cls}B})^{\op}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{X},\Phi)

be an object of
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]^{(A^{\circ},\mathcal{F})/}
, where
\Phi
is a natural transformation

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.42926pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.33604pt\raise
