By definition of
\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
, the unit morphism
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow b_{B\ast}b_{B}^{\ast}\mathcal{G}
is an equivalence. By definition of
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]
, the top left morphism
b_{B\ast}b_{B}^{\ast}{\sf Hull}(\varphi)^{\ast}{\sf unit}
is an equivalence. The existence and uniqueness of the dashed filler then follows.
∎

Definition 3.25
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a presentable
(\oo,1)
-category in which colimits are universal. The endofunctor
\Seg:\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
sends a simplicial object
\mathcal{C}
to the left Kan extension

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{s}}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf LIM}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Seg(\mathcal{C})=(\ev_{s})_{!}({\sf LIM}\circ\mathsf{L}\circ\mathcal{C}\circ S)}

Observation 3.26
.

Given
\mathcal{C}:\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, the value

\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathcal{X}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf LIM}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}

is given by

•

(\mathcal{C}\circ S)([a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}[b]^{\circ}):(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}_{/^{\cls}[a]})^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is the functor sending
(C\subset[a])^{\circ}
to
\mathcal{C}((C^{\varphi})^{\circ})
.

•

(\mathsf{L}\circ\mathcal{C}\circ S)([a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}[b]^{\circ}):(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}_{/^{\cls}[a]})^{\op}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is the functor sending
(C\subset[a])^{\circ}
to

\mathcal{C}((\{c_{0}<c_{1}\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})\underset{\mathcal{C}((\{c_{1}\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})}{\times}\ldots\underset{\mathcal{C}((\{c_{q\text{-}1}\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})}{\times}\mathcal{C}((\{c_{q\text{-}1}<c_{q}\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})

where
C=\{c_{0}<\ldots<c_{q}\}
.

•

The composite
({\sf LIM}\circ\mathsf{L}\circ\mathcal{C}\circ S)([a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\circ}}[b]^{\circ})\in\mathcal{X}
is then the value

\mathcal{C}((\{0<1\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})\underset{\mathcal{C}((\{1\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})}{\times}\ldots\underset{\mathcal{C}((\{a\text{-}1\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})}{\times}\mathcal{C}((\{a\text{-}1<a\}^{\varphi})^{\circ})

\simeq\mathcal{C}([0,\varphi(0)]^{\circ})\underset{\simeq\mathcal{C}(\{\varphi(0)\}^{\circ})}{\times}\mathcal{C}([\varphi(0),\varphi(1)]^{\circ})\underset{\mathcal{C}(\{\varphi(1)\}^{\circ})}{\times}\ldots\underset{\mathcal{C}(\{\varphi(b\text{-}1)\}^{\circ})}{\times}\mathcal{C}([\varphi(b\text{-}1),\varphi(b)]^{\circ})\underset{\mathcal{C}(\{\varphi(b)\}^{\circ})}{\times}\mathcal{C}([\varphi(b),a]^{\circ})

by Observation
3.6
.

Observation 3.27
.

The unit of the adjunction
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{X}]\leftrightarrows\Morita[\mathcal{X}]
determines a canonical morphism
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\Seg(\mathcal{C})
for any
\mathcal{C}\in\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
.
Using that colimits in
\mathcal{X}
are universal, if
\mathcal{C}
is Segal, then
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivalence.

Definition 3.28
.

A monad
T
on an
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{V}
is idempotent if the structure morphisms

\textstyle{T\circ{\sf id}_{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf id}_{T}\circ{\sf unit}}
\textstyle{T\circ T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf mult}_{T}}
\textstyle{T}
\textstyle{{\sf id}_{\mathcal{V}}\circ T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf unit}\circ{\sf id}_{T}}

are equivalences. The
(\oo,1)
-category
{\sf Monad}^{\sf idemp}(\mathcal{V})
is the full
\oo
-subcategory of
{\sf Monad}(\mathcal{V}):=\Alg(\Fun(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}))
consisting of the idempotent monads.

Observation 3.29
.

Since the diagram

\textstyle{T\circ{\sf id}_{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf id}_{T}\circ{\sf unit}}
\scriptstyle{{\sf id}_{T}}
\textstyle{T\circ T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf mult}_{T}}
\textstyle{T}
\textstyle{{\sf id}_{\mathcal{V}}\circ T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf unit}\circ{\sf id}_{T}}
\scriptstyle{{\sf id}_{T}}

commutes,
T
is idempotent if any one of the three structure morphisms are equivalences.

Definition 3.30
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be an
(\oo,1)
-category. The full
\oo
-subcategory
{\sf RFull}(\mathcal{V})\subset\Cat_{(\infty,1)/\mathcal{V}}
has objects those functors
R:\mathcal{V}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
which are fully-faithful right-adjoints. That is,
R:\mathcal{V}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
belongs to
{\sf RFull}(\mathcal{V})
if and only if there exists a left adjoint
L:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{0}
for which the counit of the adjunction
LR\rightarrow{\sf id}_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}
is an equivalence.

Lemma 3.31
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be an
(\oo,1)
-category. There is a natural equivalence of spaces

\obj\Bigl({\sf RFull}(\mathcal{V})\Bigr)\simeq\obj\Bigl({\sf Monad}^{\sf idemp}(\mathcal{V})\Bigr)

between the space of fully-faithful right-adjoints to
\mathcal{V}
, and the space of idempotent monads on
\mathcal{V}
.

Proof.

Let
\Cat^{\mathsf{R}}
be the
(\oo,1)
-category of
(\oo,1)
-categories whose morphisms are right adjoints. There is a functor

f:\Cat^{\mathsf{R}}_{/\mathcal{V}}\longrightarrow{\sf Monad}(\mathcal{V})

sending a right adjoint
R:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
to the monad
T:=RL
, where
L
is the left adjoint of
R
. To a morphism in
\Cat^{\mathsf{R}}_{/\mathcal{V}}
, which is a right adjoint
R^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
over
\mathcal{V}
with left adjoint
L^{\prime}
, this functor assigns the map of monads given by the unit of
L^{\prime}\dashv R^{\prime}
:

T=RL\xrightarrow{{\sf unit}_{L^{\prime}\dashv R^{\prime}}}RR^{\prime}L^{\prime}L=T^{\prime}~.

Now assume the functor
R:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
is fully-faithful, and thus the counit map
LR\rightarrow{\sf id}_{\mathcal{U}}
is an equivalence. Then the multiplication morphism
T\circ T\rightarrow T
is an equivalence, since it given by the counit of
LR
:
T\circ T=R(LR)L\rightarrow LR\simeq RL=T
. Consequently, this restricts to a functor
{\sf RFull}(\mathcal{V})\rightarrow{\sf Monad}^{\sf idemp}(\mathcal{V})
.

We construct a map
g:\obj({\sf Monad}^{\sf idemp}(\mathcal{V}))\rightarrow\obj({\sf RFull}(\mathcal{V}))
. To a monad
T
, we assign the
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{V}_{T}
of
T
-local objects, the full
\oo
-subcategory of those objects
x
of
\mathcal{V}
on which the unit of
T
is an equivalence:

\mathcal{V}_{T}:=\{x\in\mathcal{V}|\ x\xrightarrow{\sim}Tx\}\subset\mathcal{V}~.

The inclusion
\mathcal{V}_{T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
has a left adjoint, which is
T
itself: For any morphism
x\rightarrow t
where
t
is
T
-local, there is a functorial commutative diagram

\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{t\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{Tx\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Tt}

and thus a canonical factorization of the morphism
x\rightarrow t
through the unit morphism
x\rightarrow Tx
. Consequently,
Tx
is initial in
\mathcal{V}_{T}^{x/}
, and the functor
T:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{T}
defines a left adjoint to the inclusion
\mathcal{V}_{T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
.
By this construction of
g
, observe an equivalence
{\sf id}\simeq f\circ g
, of self-maps of
\obj({\sf Monad}^{\sf idemp}(\mathcal{V}))
.

We lastly exhibit a natural equivalence
{\sf id}\simeq g\circ f
, of self-maps of
\obj({\sf RFull}(\mathcal{V}))
. To a fully-faithful right-adjoint
R:\mathcal{U}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
with left adjoint
L
, we claim there is a unique factorization

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}_{RL}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}}

where
\mathcal{V}_{RL}\subset\mathcal{V}
is the full
\oo
-subcategory of
x\in\mathcal{V}
for which
x\rightarrow RLx
is an equivalence. However,
x
belongs to
\mathcal{V}_{RL}
if and only if
x
is in the essential image of
R
: so a unique functor
\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{RL}
exists over
\mathcal{V}
, and is an equivalence.
∎

We now come to the culminating result of this section, which describes the Segal-completion functor in special cases.

Lemma 3.32
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a presentable
(\oo,1)
-category in which colimits are universal, and let
\mathcal{C}\in\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
be a simplicial object in
\mathcal{X}
. If
\Seg(\mathcal{C})
is Segal, then
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\Seg(\mathcal{C})
exhibits
\Seg(\mathcal{C})
as the universal Segal object under
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The fully-faithful inclusion of Segal functors
R:\Fun^{\Seg}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})\hookrightarrow\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
has a left adjoint
L
by the adjoint functor theorem, since the inclusion preserves limits and filtered colimits. Let
\mathcal{V}:=\Seg^{-1}\bigl(\Fun^{\Seg}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})\bigr)\subset\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
be the full
\oo
-subcategory of all
\mathcal{C}
such that
\Seg(\mathcal{C})
is Segal.

Observe that on
\mathcal{V}
the functor
\Seg
has the structure of an idempotent monad. The functor
RL
additionally has the structure of an idempotent monad on
\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
, which restricts to an idempotent monad on
\mathcal{V}
. Consequently, both
\Seg
and
RL
define idempotent monads on
\mathcal{V}
, both of which correspond to fully-faithful right adjoints
\mathcal{V}_{\Seg}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}_{RL}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
, defined by the
\Seg
-local and
RL
-local objects, respectively.
Observe that these
(\oo,1)
-categories are both equivalent to
\Fun^{\Seg}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{X})
, however. Consequently, by Lemma
3.31
, we have an equivalence
\Seg\simeq L
on
\mathcal{V}
. That is, for any
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{V}
, then
\Seg(\mathcal{C})
is the universal Segal object under
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

4.
Twisted-arrow categories of 1-disks

In this section, we prove Lemma
4.6
, that the twisted arrow category of a poset of disks localizes onto the twisted arrow category of
\mathbf{\Delta}
.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
I
be a 1-manifold with boundary. The category
\disk_{I}
is the full sub-poset of
\Opens(I)
consisting of all
U\subset I
such that:

•

each component of
U
is diffeomorphic to either
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
;

•

U
contains the boundary
\partial I
;

•

U
has finitely many components.

Recall the following result from, e.g.,
[
AF1
]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
I
be an oriented closed interval. There is functor

\disk_{I}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}

sending an open
U\subset I
to the finite set
\pi U:=\pi_{0}(I\smallsetminus U)
, ordered according to the orientation of
I
. This functor is a localization with respect to the set of morphisms
U\rightarrow V
which are isotopy equivalences or, equivalently, the morphisms which induce bijections
\pi U\simeq\pi V
.

Our goal in this section, Lemma
4.6
, is to prove the analogous localization after taking categories of twisted arrows. This will rely on the following two preparatory results, Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.5
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
I
be a 1-manifold with boundary.
Let
\mathcal{J}\subset\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})
be the subcategory consisting of all objects
U\hookrightarrow V
, and whose morphisms
(U\hookrightarrow V)\rightarrow(U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V^{\prime})
are those commutative diagrams

\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}}

for which both
U\leftarrow U^{\prime}
and
V\rightarrow V^{\prime}
are isotopy equivalences.
Let
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{J}
be the full subcategory consisting of those objects
U\hookrightarrow V
for which
V
contains the closure of
U
:
\overline{U}\subset V
.
Under these conditions, the inclusion

\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{J}

is a final functor.

Proof.

By Quillen’s Theorem A, it suffices to check that for any object
U\hookrightarrow V
, the undercategory

\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\underset{\mathcal{J}}{\times}\mathcal{J}^{U\rightarrow V/}

has contractible classifying space. There is a full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\underset{\mathcal{J}}{\times}\mathcal{J}^{U\rightarrow V/}
consisting of those
U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime}
under
U\rightarrow V
for which
V\rightarrow V^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. The inclusion
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\underset{\mathcal{J}}{\times}\mathcal{J}^{U\rightarrow V/}
admits a right adjoint, sending
U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime}
to
U^{\prime}\rightarrow V
: the unit of the adjunction is given by

\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}}

for each
U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime}
under
U\rightarrow V
. Hence the classifying space of
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\underset{\mathcal{J}}{\times}\mathcal{J}^{U\rightarrow V/}
is equivalent to that of
\mathcal{C}
. The category
\mathcal{C}
is clearly seen to be equivalent to
\mathsf{D}_{U}^{\op}
, via evaluation at source. The category
\mathsf{D}_{U}
has an initial object, given by the empty inclusion
\emptyset\rightarrow U
, hence its classifying space is contractible.
∎

The next result uses the following.

Notation 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an
(\infty,1)
-category; let
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-subcategory of an
(\infty,1)
-category.
We denote the
\infty
-subcategory

\Fun^{\mathcal{W}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{C})~\hookrightarrow~\Fun(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{C})

which is characterized by the following property.

•

A natural transformation
F\xrightarrow{\eta}G
belongs to this
\infty
-subcategory if and only if, for each
x\in\mathcal{K}
, the morphism
F(x)\xrightarrow{\eta(x)}G(x)
in
\mathcal{C}
belongs to
\mathcal{W}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
I
be a closed oriented interval, and let
U\rightarrow V
be a morphism of
\mathsf{D}_{I}
for which
V
contains the closure of
U
: i.e.,
U\rightarrow V
is an object of
\mathcal{J}\subset\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})
. For all
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
, the natural functor

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Hom\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)\Bigr)

induces an equivalence between the classifying space of
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)
and the set of
p
-simplices of
\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)
.

Proof.

We prove this result by induction on
[p]
. First, note the equivalence
({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\cong\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}
.

Base Case
: We show that

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([0],\TwAr\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\obj\Bigl(\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)\Bigr)

induces an equivalence between the classifying space of the source and the set of objects of the target. We analyze the following commutative diagram, defined by evaluating twisted arrows at their target:

\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigl([0],\TwAr\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm final}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([0],\TwAr\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\obj\Bigl(\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm final}
\textstyle{\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}}

Here,
\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}\subset\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}
is the subcategory consisting of those morphisms
V^{\prime}\subset V^{\prime\prime}
that are isotopy equivalences;

\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\subset\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}
is the full subcategory consisting of those objects
V^{\prime}
such that there is containment of the closure:
\overline{U}\subset V^{\prime}
.
We claim that the inclusion

\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}

is final, as indicated in the diagram. By Quillen’s Theorem A, it suffices to show that for every
V^{\prime}\in\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}
, the poset

\Bigl(\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigr)^{V^{\prime}/}

has contractible classifying space.
Explicitly, this poset consists of those
D\subset V
such that
D
contains both
V^{\prime}
and the closure of
U
, and the inclusion
V^{\prime}\rightarrow D
is an isotopy equivalence.
Using that the ambient space
I
is a closed interval, this poset has finite nonempty colimits: Given any finite nonempty diagram

G:\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\Bigl(\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigr)^{V^{\prime}/}

the union
D:=\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}G(k)
is again an element of
\Bigl(\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigr)^{V^{\prime}/}
, and is the colimit of the diagram. This implies that the classifying space of
\Bigl(\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigr)^{V^{\prime}/}
is contractible, as claimed.

We have thus established the finality of the functor
\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}
. Since finality is preserved under pullbacks along coCartesian fibrations (e.g., Corollary 5.15 of
[
AF5
]
), to establish the base case, we have reduced to showing that

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigl([0],\TwAr\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\obj\Bigl(\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)\Bigr)

induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.
To do so, it suffices to show first that the map on the bases of the
\ev_{t}
coCartesian fibrations

\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}}\longrightarrow({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}

is an equivalence on classifying spaces, and second that for every
\ev_{t}
-fibers over any object
(\overline{U}\subset V^{\prime}\subset V)\in\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}
, the map

\ev_{t}^{-1}\{\overline{U}\subset V^{\prime}\subset V\}\longrightarrow\ev_{t}^{-1}\{\pi U\subset\pi V^{\prime}\subset\pi V\}

is an equivalence on classifying spaces. For the first statement, this follows from the proofs of Proposition 2.19 and Lemma 3.11 from
[
AF1
]
: The only alteration is that we now have an undercategory, and the proofs carry over mutatis mutandis. For the second statement, we can identify the fibers:

\ev_{t}^{-1}\{\overline{U}\subset V^{\prime}\subset V\}\cong\bigl(\mathsf{D}_{V^{\prime}}^{U/}\bigr)^{\mathcal{J},\op}

and

\ev_{t}^{-1}\{\pi U\subset\pi V^{\prime}\subset\pi V\}\cong({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V^{\prime}})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V^{\prime}/,\op}~.

We now have the equivalence on classifying spaces, since this statement is the opposite of that just established by Proposition 2.19 and Lemma 3.11 from
[
AF1
]
, and taking opposites does not change the classifying space.

Inductive Step
: We assume the equivalence on
[k]
-points for
k<p
. For each choice of an object
(U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V^{\prime})\in\TwAr\bigl((\mathsf{D}_{I/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)
, we have a pullback square:

\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p-1],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V^{\prime}})^{U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime}/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime}\rangle}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl(\{p\},\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)}

where
\ev_{p}
is a coCartesian fibration. Note further the equivalences

\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})_{/U\rightarrow V}\cong\TwAr\bigl(((\mathsf{D}_{I})_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\cong\TwAr\bigl((\mathsf{D}_{V})^{U/}\bigr)

and thus the equivalence

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p-1],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})_{/U\rightarrow V})\cong\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p-1],\TwAr((\mathsf{D}_{V})^{U/}))~.

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\rm final}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Hom\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)\Bigr)}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCard}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Bigl(\{p\},\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Lemma~\ref{lemma.I.final}}
\scriptstyle{\rm final}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl(\{p\},\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\obj(\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}))}

where the left square is defined as a pullback.
Finality of the lower left horizontal functor follows from Lemma
4.3
: Using Quillen’s Theorem A, finality of the fully-faithful inclusion
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{J}
(Lemma
4.3
) implies finality of the functor between any undercategories for any object in
\mathcal{J}
.
Again using that the pullback of a final functor along a coCartesian fibration is again final, it suffices to show that

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V})^{U\rightarrow V/}\bigr)\Bigr)^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Hom\Bigl([p],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}_{/\pi V})^{\pi U\rightarrow\pi V/}\bigr)\Bigr)

induces an equivalence on the classifying space.

Since each
\ev_{p}
is a coCartesian fibration and the
the base case ensures the functor between base categories induces an equivalence between classifying spaces,
we reduce to the statement on the fibers, to showing that for
U\rightarrow V
belonging to
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, then

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p-1],\TwAr\bigl(({\mathsf{D}_{I}}_{/V^{\prime}})^{U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime}/}\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Map([p-1],\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})_{/\pi V^{\prime}}^{\pi V^{\prime}\rightarrow\pi U^{\prime}/})

induces an equivalence on the classifying space. This equivalence is exactly given by the inductive hypothesis.
∎

The following is the main result of this section.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
I
be a closed oriented interval. The functor

\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})

is a localization.

Proof.

We apply the following sufficiency condition (Theorem 3.8 of
[
MG
]
)
for establishing when a functor
\pi:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a localization:

•

Let
\mathcal{J}=\pi^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\sim}
be the
\oo
-subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
which maps to isomorphisms in
\mathcal{D}
, and let
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{C})\subset\Fun(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{C})
be the
\oo
-subcategory of functors in which the morphisms are natural transformations contained in
\mathcal{J}
. If for each
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
, the natural map

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\Map([p],\mathcal{D})

exhibits the space of
p
-simplices of
\mathcal{D}
as the classifying space of
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\mathcal{C})
, then
\pi:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a localization.

We apply this condition to the functor
\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})\longrightarrow\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
, and prove by induction on
p
that the functor

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))\longrightarrow\Map([p],\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}))

induces an equivalence from the classifying space of
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))
.

Consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\Map([p],\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\rm coCart}
\textstyle{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm final}
\textstyle{\mathcal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\obj\bigl(\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\bigr)}

where
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))^{\prime}
is the pullback of
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))
along
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{J}
. By Lemma
4.3
, the inclusion
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{J}
is final. Since
\ev_{p}
is a coCartesian fibration, and finality is preserved by pullbacks along coCartesian fibrations, then
\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))^{\prime}\rightarrow\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}([p],\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I}))
is again final, and the classifying spaces of the two categories are equivalent.

Consequently, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{J}\rightarrow\obj\bigl(\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\bigr)
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces, and that for every object
(U\rightarrow V)\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, the functor on the
\ev_{p}
-fibers

\Fun^{\mathcal{J}}\Bigl([p-1],\TwAr\bigl((\mathsf{D}_{I})_{/U\rightarrow V}\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Hom\Bigl([p-1],\TwAr\bigl((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})_{/\pi U\rightarrow\pi V}\bigr)\Bigr)

induces an equivalence on classifying spaces. This second assertion is given by Lemma
4.5
. To show that
\mathcal{J}\rightarrow\obj\bigl(\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\bigr)
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces, we can use the coCartesian fibrations defined by
\ev_{t}
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{J}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\textstyle{\obj\bigl(\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\textstyle{(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\sf istpy}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\obj(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})}

where
(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\sf istpy}\subset\mathsf{D}_{I}
is the sub-poset with morphisms those inclusions which are isotopy equivalence.
By Proposition 2.19 and Lemma 3.11 from
[
AF1
]
, we have that the functor on the base,
(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\sf istpy}\rightarrow\obj(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})
, induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.
Over
V\in(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\sf istpy}
, the functor between fibers is identified as
(\mathsf{D}_{V})^{\sf istpy,\op}\rightarrow\obj\bigl((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}_{/\pi V})^{\op}\bigr)
, which also induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.
Using that
\ev_{t}
is a coCartesian fibration, we conclude that the set
\obj\bigl(\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}))
is the classifying space of
\mathcal{J}
, which completes the proof.
∎

5.
The Tangle Hypothesis in ambient dimension 2

The main result of this section is Theorem
5.24
, that

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})

is the rigid monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category freely generated by a single object. This implies
Theorem

0.1
in the special case of
n=2
. We prove this result by induction on the number of duals of the distinguished object. The proof of the base case, in which case the object has a single right-dual, occupies the next section.

5.1.
Base case

For
0<i\leq\oo
, we will say that an object
V
of a monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}
has
i
right-duals (respectively, left-duals) if
V
has a right-dual
V^{R}
, and
V^{R}
has
i-1
right-duals. For
i=0
, the condition is vacuously satisfied.

Definition 5.1
.

For
0\leq i,j\leq\oo
,
\Dual^{[i,j]}\in\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})
corepresents the functor

\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\longrightarrow\spaces

which sends a monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{R}
to the subspace of
\Obj(\mathcal{R})
consisting of those objects
V
such that
V
admits
i
left-duals and
j
right-duals.
We denote the universal such object
D\in\Dual^{[i,j]}
.
We denote

\Dual:=\Dual^{[0,1]}~.

Recall Corollary
2.23
, which in particular identifies the monoidal space of objects

\obj\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{F}_{1}(\Omega\RP^{1})

with the free
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-space generated by the set
\mathbb{Z}\simeq\Omega\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. This allows the following definition.
For
0\leq i,j\leq\infty
, we denote the subset:

[-i,j]~:=~\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}\mid-i\leq a\leq j\right\}~\subset~\mathbb{Z}~.

Definition 5.2
.

For
0\leq i,j\leq\oo
, the monoidal full
\infty
-subcategory

\Bord^{[i,j]}~\subset~\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})

is that whose space of objects is the subspace

\coprod_{r\geq 0}\Conf_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\underset{\Sigma_{r}}{\times}[-i,j]^{r}\subset\coprod_{r\geq 0}\Conf_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\underset{\Sigma_{r}}{\times}\mathbb{Z}^{r}\simeq\mathbb{F}_{1}(\Omega\RP^{1})\simeq\obj\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\bigr)~.

A
[i,j]
-framing
of a tangle
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
is a framing of this tangle such that the resulting framed tangle is a morphism in
\Bord^{[i,j]}
.

In other words, an object in
\Bord^{[i,j]}
is a finite subset of
\mathbb{R}^{1}
labeled by integers within
[-i,j]
.

We single out two cases of this definition, which again follow from Corollary
2.23
.

Corollary 5.3
.

There are canonical equivalences

\mathbb{F}_{1}(\ast)\simeq\Dual^{[0,0]}\simeq\Bord^{[0,0]}

and

\mathbb{F}_{1}\left(\left\{0,1\right\}\right)\simeq\Obj\left(\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)

where
\mathbb{F}_{1}(\ast)
is the monoidal category freely generated by a single object
\ast
, and
\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0,1\})
is the monoidal space freely generated by the 2-element set
\{0,1\}
.

∎

Lemma 5.4
.

The object
\left(\{0\},0\right)\in\Bord^{[i,j]}
has
i
left-duals and
j
right-duals.
Equivalently, there is a unique monoidal functor

(24)

\Dual^{[i,j]}\longrightarrow\Bord^{[i,j]}~,\qquad D\longmapsto\left(\{0\},0\right)~.

Proof.

After Corollary
2.21
,

\left(\{0\},0\right)\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
has
a
left-duals and
b
right-duals for all
0\leq a,b\leq\infty
.
By construction, the the
a^{\rm th}
left-dual and
b^{\rm th}
right-dual are contained in
\Bord^{[i,j]}
if and only if
a\leq i
and
b\leq j
.
Consequently,
\left(\{0\},0\right)\in\Bord^{[i,j]}
has
i
left-duals and
j
right-duals.
∎

Consider the
walking adjunction
, which is the
(\infty,2)
-category
\Adj
corepresenting an adjunction among
(\infty,2)
-categories.
This
(\infty,2)
-category is the subject of the work
[
RV
]
which establishes the following remarkable features of
\Adj
.
To state it, we recall the following (see Appendix A of
[
AF7
]
for further details).

•

\mathsf{O}
is the category in which an object is a finite linearly ordered set and a morphism is an order preserving map between such.
Join of linearly ordered sets defines a monoidal structure on
\mathsf{O}
.
This monoidal category is the
walking monad
, which corepresents monads.
Its opposite
\mathsf{O}^{\op}
is the
walking comonad
, which corepresents comonads.

•

\mathsf{O}_{+}
(respectively
\mathsf{O}_{-}
) is the category in which an object is a non-empty finite linearly ordered set and a morphism is an order preserving map between such that preserves maxima (respectively minima).
Join of linearly ordered sets defines a left (respectively right) module structure
\mathsf{O}\lacts\mathsf{O}_{+}
(respectively
\mathsf{O}_{-}\racts\mathsf{O}
).

•

For each
I^{\tr}\in\mathsf{O}_{+}
, the category
\Fun^{+\infty/}(I^{\tr},\{0<+\infty\})
of maxima-preserving functors is a non-empty finite linearly ordered set, and the functor
\Fun^{+\infty/}(J^{\tr},\{0<+\infty\})\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}\Fun^{+\infty/}(I^{\tr},\{0<+\infty\})
associated to each morphism
I^{\tr}\xrightarrow{f}J^{\tr}
in
\mathsf{O}_{+}
preserves minima.
This organizes as a functor

\mathsf{O}_{+}^{\op}\xrightarrow{~\Fun^{+\infty/}(-,\{0<+\infty\})~}\mathsf{O}_{-}~,

which can be checked to be an equivalence (an inverse is given by
\Fun_{/-\infty}(-,\{-\infty<0\})
.

Theorem 5.5

(
[
RV
]
,
[
SS
]
)
.

The
(\infty,2)
-category
\Adj
admits the following description.

•

Its space of objects is canonically identified as

\Obj(\Adj)~\simeq~\{-,+\}~,

a 0-type with two path-components.

•

Its monoidal categories of endomorphisms of each of its two objects are canonically identified as the walking monad and the walking comonad:

\underline{\End}_{\Adj}(-)\underset{\simeq}{\xleftarrow{~(\mathsf{R}\circ\mathsf{L})^{\circ I}~\mapsfrom~I~}}\mathsf{O}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\underline{\End}_{\Adj}(+)\underset{\simeq}{\xleftarrow{~(\mathsf{L}\circ\mathsf{R})^{\circ I}~\mapsfrom~I~}}\mathsf{O}^{\op}~.

•

Its
\bigl(\underline{\End}_{\Adj}(-),\underline{\End}_{\Adj}(+)\bigr)
-bimodule category of morphisms from
-
to
+
,
and its
\bigl(\underline{\End}_{\Adj}(-),\underline{\End}_{\Adj}(+)\bigr)
-bimodule category of morphisms from
+
to
-
, are respectively

\underline{\Hom}_{\Adj}(-,+)\underset{\simeq}{\xleftarrow{~\mathsf{L}\circ(\mathsf{R}\circ\mathsf{L})^{\circ J}~\mapsfrom~J^{\tl}~}}\mathsf{O}_{-}~\simeq~\mathsf{O}_{+}^{\op}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\underline{\Hom}_{\Adj}(+,-)\underset{\simeq}{\xleftarrow{~(\mathsf{R}\circ\mathsf{L})^{\circ J}\circ\mathsf{R}~\mapsfrom~J^{\tr}~}}\mathsf{O}_{+}~\simeq~\mathsf{O}_{-}^{\op}~.

In particular, the
(\infty,2)
-category
\Adj
is gaunt, and its maximal
(\infty,1)
-category
\Adj_{\leq 1}\subset\Adj
is the free
(\infty,1)
-category on the directed graph

The last statement of Theorem
5.5
supplies an identification between
(\infty,1)
-categories

(25)

\ast\underset{\Obj(\Adj}{\coprod}\Adj_{\leq 1}\simeq\mathfrak{B}\mathbb{F}_{1}\left(\{0,1\}\right)~.

Observation 5.6
.

The universal properties of
\Dual
, and the walking adjunction
\Adj
, are such that they participate in a pushout diagram among
(\infty,2)
-categories:

(26)

\textstyle{\Obj(\Adj)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Adj\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{B}\Dual~.}

In particular,
the identification (
25
) supplies a morphism between monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories from the free monoidal category on the 2-element set
\{0,1\}
,

\mathbb{F}_{1}\left(\{0,1\}\right)\longrightarrow\Dual~,

that is essentially surjective.

Remark 5.7
.

Therefore, in spite of
\mathfrak{B}\Dual
fitting into a pushout involving gaunt
(\infty,2)
-categories, it may not be gaunt – indeed, the gauntness condition on an
(\infty,2)
-category is not preserved under the formation of pushouts.
This issue complicates direct “object-morphism”-style approaches to proving Proposition
5.11
.
Gauntness will, however, follow from the gauntness of
\Bord^{[0,1]}
, implied by Proposition
2.29
, through Proposition
5.11
.

Definition 5.8
.

Let
A
be a set.

(1)

A
word (in the alphabet
A
)
is a finite linearly ordered set
(I,\leq)
together with a map between sets
I\xrightarrow{\omega}A
.
When no confusion can arise, we often denote such a word
\left(\left(I,\leq\right),\omega\right)
simply as
(I,\omega)
, or as
\omega
.

(2)

Say a word
(I,\omega)
is the
empty word
if
I=\emptyset
.

(3)

Say a word
(I,\omega)
is
alternating
if, for each non-maximal element
i\in I\smallsetminus{\sf Max}(I)
, the two values
\omega(i)\neq\omega\left({\sf suc}(i)\right)
do not agree where
{\sf suc}(i)\in I
is the successor of
i
in
I
.

(4)

For
J
a finite linearly ordered set, and for
(I_{j},\omega_{j})_{j\in J}
a
J
-indexed sequence of words, their
(
J
-fold) concatenation
is the word

\underset{j\in J}{\bigstar}\omega_{j}~:=~\Bigl(\underset{j\in J}{\bigstar}I_{j}~,~\underset{j\in J}{\coprod}I_{j}\xrightarrow{(\omega_{j})_{j\in J}}A\Bigr)

whose underlying linearly ordered set is the
J
-fold join.
For
J=\{1<\dots<r\}
, this will often be denoted simply as

\omega_{r}\cdots\omega_{1}~.

(5)

An isomorphism between two words
(I,\omega)\cong(I^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
is an isomorphism between linearly ordered sets
I\xrightarrow{\alpha}I^{\prime}
such that
\omega^{\prime}\circ\alpha=\omega
.

(6)

The
space of words (in the alphabet
A
)
is the groupoid of words (in the alphabet
A
) and isomorphisms between such.

Observation 5.9
.

Let
A
be a set.
The space of words in the alphabet
A
is a 0-type, and concatenation of words defines a monoidal structure on this space.
Consequently, the space of words in the alphabet
A
is the free
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-algebra on the space
A
.

Observation 5.10
.

After Corollary
5.3
, the monoid of objects in
\Bord^{[0,1]}
is that of words in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
.

5.2.
Proof of
\Dual\simeq\Bord^{[0,1]}

This subsection is devoted to a proof of the following.

Proposition 5.11
.

The monoidal functor

\Dual\xrightarrow{~(\ref{eDualBord})~}\Bord^{[0,1]}

is an equivalence between monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories.

After Observation
5.6
, Corollary
5.3
yields the following.

Corollary 5.12
.

There is a canonical lift among
(\infty,2)
-categories:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{B}\Dual\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eDualBord})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{B}\Obj\left(\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm inclusion}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}.}

The next technical result supplies lifts of 1-morphisms along the monoidal functor
\Dual\xrightarrow{(\ref{eDualBord})}\Bord^{[0,1]}
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Consider a solid diagram
(\infty,1)
-categories,

(27)

\hbox to105.81pt{\vbox to50.36pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 52.90527pt\lower-25.18109pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-52.90527pt}{-20.02136pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -73.21 -27.7)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}

Via Corollary
5.3
, each of the composite morphisms

\omega_{s}\colon c_{0}\xrightarrow{~s~}c_{1}\longrightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\omega_{t}\colon c_{0}\xrightarrow{~t~}c_{1}\longrightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}

is a word in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
.
There is a canonical filler in (
27
) provided both
\omega_{s}
and
\omega_{t}
are alternating (and possibly empty), and provided the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

If neither
\omega_{s}
nor
\omega_{t}
is empty, then their extremal letters agree.

(2)

If
\omega_{s}
is empty and
\omega_{t}
is not empty, then the first letter of
\omega_{t}
is
0
and the last letter of
\omega_{t}
is
1
.

(3)

If
\omega_{t}
is empty and
\omega_{s}
is not empty, then the first letter of
\omega_{s}
is
1
and the last letter of
\omega_{s}
is
0
.

Proof.

Observation
5.6
supplies a composite functor

(28)

\Adj\longrightarrow\mathfrak{B}\Dual\xrightarrow{~(\ref{eDualBord})~}\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})~.

Inspection of the maximal
(\infty,1)
-subcategory of
\Adj
(Theorem
5.5
) supplies a unique lift of
\partial c_{2}\hookrightarrow c_{2}\to\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
along (
28
).
Inspection of the space of 2-morphisms of
\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
(Lemma
2.23
) and the space of 2-morphisms of
\Adj
(Theorem
5.5
) supplies a further unique lift of
c_{2}\to\mathfrak{B}\Bord_{1}^{\sf fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
along (
28
).
This unique lift determines the canonical lift of the statement of the lemma.

∎

The universal property of
\Dual
will imply that the composite morphism
\Dual\xrightarrow{L\circ(\ref{eDualBord})}\Dual
is the identity provided that the functor
L
is such that the upper square of this diagram is commutative.

Via (
23
), the diagram (
29
) among monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories can be codified in terms of its deloop, as a diagram among pointed
(\infty,2)
-categories.
Via the presentation of
(\infty,2)
-categories as presheaves on
\bTheta_{2}
established in
[
Re2
]
, the diagram (
29
) can be codified as a diagram among presheaves on
\bTheta_{2}
.

{\sf RFib}_{\bTheta_{2}}\simeq\PShv(\bTheta_{2})
of §2 of
[
Lu1
]
, the diagram (

) can in turn be codified as the following diagram among right fibrations over
\bTheta_{2}

5.2.1.
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on 1-categories

Consider the full
\infty
-subcategories
\bTheta_{2}\subset\Cat_{(\oo,2)}\supset\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
.
Their intersection is the simplex category:
\mathbf{\Delta}\simeq\bTheta_{2}\cap\Cat_{(\oo,1)}
.
In other words, an object
T\in\bTheta_{2}
is a 1-category if and only if
T=[p]
for some
p\geq 0
.
Consider, then, the full subcategory

\mathbf{\Delta}_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}\subset(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
consisting of those
\left(T\to\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)
such that
T
is a 1-category (i.e.,
T=[p]
for some
p\geq 0
).
We define the lift
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
over this full subcategory to be that given by Corollary
5.12
.

5.2.2.
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on 2-cells

We now explicitly construct the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on each object
\left(T\xrightarrow{\langle(W,\varphi)\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)
in which
T=c_{2}=[1]([1])
is the 2-cell.
So choose such an object
\left(c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle(W,\varphi)\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)
, which is a tangle
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
in the hemispherically stratified 2-disk equipped with a
[0,1]
-framing.
(As we proceed, we omit the framing
\varphi
from our notation.)

Let us overview our logic.
Consider the composite morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories
\partial c_{2}\to c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
.
Through Corollary
5.3
, this selects a pair of words in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
.
Optimistically, these two words are alternating, for in that case Lemma
5.13
could be applied to achieve the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on this object
\left(c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)
.
However, of course, these two words need not be alternating.
Our strategy, therefore, is to consider the minimal factorization of these two words by alternating words.
We then minimally factor the 2-cell, commensurately with this factorization of its boundary, as a 2-categorical composition of 2-cells.
We then lift each of these constituent 2-cells via Corollary
5.3
, which supplies a lift of the original 2-cell.
This logic can be organized as the existence of a flagged
(\infty,2)
-category
G(\langle W\rangle)
fitting into a diagram among flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories:

We then take the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on the object
\left(c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)\in(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
to be the composite diagonal morphism:
\left(T\to G(\langle W\rangle)\to\mathfrak{B}\Dual\right)\in(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Dual}
.

Recall that the cellular realization of the 2-cell,
\langle c_{2}\rangle=\mathbb{D}^{2}
, is the hemispherically stratified 2-disk, equipped with its solid 2-framing.

By definition of
\Bord^{[0,1]}
, the morphism
c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
is a
[0,1]
-framed tangle
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
See Figure
10
.

Figure 10
.
Depicted (in solid blue) is a
[0,1]
-framed tangle in the (solid black) hemispherically stratified 2-disk equipped with the (red) solid 2-framing. This datum is a 2-cell
c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
. Also depicted is the (dashed orange) union
A
of arcs.

As
Step 1
, we construct a finite disjoint union of arcs
A\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
with the following features.

•

The end-points of each arc in
A
are contained in the boundary of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.

•

Recall that the
[0,1]
-framing of
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
determines two words
W\cap\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
.
The set of end-points of the arcs in
A
minimally factors these words into alternating words.

Constructing
A
involves several case-by-case analyses,
ultimately exploiting that the closure of each connected component of
\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
is a 2-disk.

The
[0,1]
-framing of
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
determines an orientation on
W
with the following property and implication.

(
\dagger
)

Let
W_{\alpha}\subset W
be a connected component.
If either
W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}=\emptyset
or
W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}=\emptyset
, then
W_{\alpha}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
is oriented-isotopic relative to its boundary to a horizontal straight line oriented opposite to the first coordinate direction.

(
\dagger\dagger
)

Each connected component of
W
is a (closed) arc whose set of end-points is a subset of
(\mathbb{D}^{2})^{(1)}
, the 1-dimensional strata of the hemispherically stratified (closed) 2-disk.
In particular, each connected component of
W
separates
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Say a connected component
W_{\alpha}\subset W

crosses
if
W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}\neq\emptyset\neq W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, which is to say its end-points do not both belong to the same hemisphere of
\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}
.

Let
C\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
be a connected component of the complement of this tangle.
Its closure
\overline{C}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a compact subspace.
Consider the stratification of
\overline{C}
such that, for each
0\leq i\leq 2
, each connected component of the intersection
C\cap(\mathbb{D}^{2})^{(i)}
and each connected component of the intersection
\overline{C}\cap W\cap(\mathbb{D}^{2})^{(i)}
is a stratum of
\overline{C}
, where
(\mathbb{D}^{2})^{(i)}
is the
i
-dimensional strata of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
This stratified space
\overline{C}
is a compact 2-manifold with corners;
the closure of each 1-dimensional face of
\overline{C}
is isomorphic with
\mathbb{D}^{1}
, as a 1-manifold with boundary.
The closure of some of its 1-dimensional faces are connected components of
W
.
Also,
\partial\overline{C}\smallsetminus W=\partial C
is the boundary of
C
, as a 2-manifold with corners.
This boundary
\partial C\subset\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}
is an open subspace of the boundary of the 2-disk, as a 2-manifold with corners.
Each 1-dimensional face in
\partial C
is a connected open interval of the upper-hemisphere
\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}
or lower-hemisphere
\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
of the boundary
\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Therefore, the finite set of 1-dimensional faces of
C
admits a partition indexed by the set
\{+,-\}
– corresponding to which hemisphere a face belongs to – and each partition inherits a linear order from the
1^{st}
coordinate direction of the solid 2-framing of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Note that
\overline{C}
is isomorphic, as a manifold with corners, with a convex subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Now, say a connected component
C\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
is
coherent
if there exists an orientation of the boundary topological 1-manifold
\partial\overline{C}
that extends the orientation of
W\cap\partial\overline{C}\subset W
.
Say such a
C
is
incoherent
if it is not coherent.
Let
C\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
be an incoherent connected component.
Choose a (closed) arc
A_{C}\subset C
with the following properties.
See Figure
10
.

(A.1)

The arc separates: The complement
C\smallsetminus A_{C}
has exactly two connected components.
Necessarily, then, the set of end-points
\partial A_{C}\subset\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}
are in the boundary of the 2-disk.

(A.2)

There is containment
S^{0}\cap C\subset\partial A_{C}
.
In other words, each 0-dimensional stratum of the hemispherically stratified 2-disk
\mathbb{D}^{2}
that is contained in
C
is an end-point of the arc
A_{C}
.

(A.3)

There exists an orientation on
\partial\overline{C}\smallsetminus A_{C}
, which is the boundary of
\overline{C}
remove the set of end-points of the arc
A_{C}
, that extends the orientation of
W\cap\partial(\overline{C}\smallsetminus A_{C})\subset W
.

We now argue that such an arc
A_{C}\subset C
exists.
Note that
C
incoherent implies
W\neq\emptyset
is not empty.
Fix an embedding
\overline{C}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathbb{R}^{2}
whose image in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a convex 2-submanifold with corners.
Isotope
\varphi
as needed to ensure that the unique affine arc between two points each in distinct faces of
\overline{C}
only intersects the boundary of
\overline{C}
at its end-points.
Any such arc separates
C
.
We now construct
A_{C}
through cases.
In each case, the named
A_{C}
will satisfy the desired property in light of (
\dagger
).

•

Suppose
S^{0}\cap C=S^{0}
.
Then take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(S^{0})
.

•

Suppose
S^{0}\cap C=\{e_{1}\}
, the singleton consisting of the first standard basis vector of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Then there is exactly one connected component
W_{\alpha}\subset\overline{C}\cap W
that crosses.
Denote
\{p_{\pm}\}:=W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
.

–

Suppose
W_{\alpha}
is oriented such that the orientation is inward at
p_{-}
and outward at
p_{+}
.
In this case, select an element
x
in the 1-dimensional face of
C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
whose closure contains
p_{+}
.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,e_{1}\})
.

–

Suppose
W_{\alpha}
is oriented such that the orientation is outward at
p_{-}
and inward at
p_{+}
.
In this case, select an element
x
in the 1-dimensional face of
C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
whose closure contains
p_{-}
.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,e_{1}\})
.

•

Suppose
S^{0}\cap C=\{-e_{1}\}
, the singleton consisting of the negation of the first standard basis vector of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Then there is exactly one connected component
W_{\alpha}\subset\overline{C}\cap W
that crosses.
Denote
\{p_{\pm}\}:=W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
.

–

Suppose
W_{\alpha}
is oriented such that the orientation is inward at
p_{-}
and outward at
p_{+}
.
In this case, select an element
y
in the 1-dimensional face of
C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
whose closure contains
p_{-}
.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{-e_{1},y\})
.

–

Suppose
W_{\alpha}
is oriented such that the orientation is outward at
p_{-}
and inward at
p_{+}
.
In this case, select an element
y
in the 1-dimensional face of
C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
whose closure contains
p_{+}
.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{-e_{1},y\})
.

•

Suppose
S^{0}\cap C=\emptyset
.
In this case, there are either exactly 0 or 2 connected components of
W\cap\overline{C}
that cross.

–

Suppose no connected component of
W\cap\overline{C}
crosses.
Using the supposition that
S^{0}\cap C=\emptyset
, then every connected component of
W\cap\overline{C}
has end-points in the same hemisphere.
Assume, without loss in generality, that this hemisphere is
\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
The supposition that
C
is incoherent ensures the boundary
\partial C
has more than one connected component.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of the boundary
\partial C
, select an element
x
in the minimal connected component and an element
y
in the maximal connected component.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,y\})
.

–

Suppose exactly two connected components
W_{\alpha},W_{\beta}\subset W\cap\overline{C}
cross.
Denote
\{p_{\pm}\}:=W_{\alpha}\cap\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
and
\{q_{\pm}\}:=W_{\beta}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}
.

*

Suppose the orientation of
W_{\alpha}
is inward at
p_{-}
and the orientation of
W_{\beta}
is inward at
q_{-}
.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of
\partial C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, select an element
x
in the maximal connected component.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of
\partial C\cap\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, select an element
y
in the minimal connected component.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,y\})
.

*

Suppose the orientation of
W_{\alpha}
is inward at
p_{-}
and the orientation of
W_{\beta}
is inward at
q_{+}
.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of
\partial C\cap\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, select an element
x
in the minimal connected component and an element
y
in the maximal connected component.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,y\})
.

*

Suppose the orientation of
W_{\alpha}
is inward at
p_{+}
and the orientation of
W_{\beta}
is inward at
q_{-}
.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of
\partial C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, select an element
x
in the minimal connected component and an element
y
in the maximal connected component.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,y\})
.

*

Suppose the orientation of
W_{\alpha}
is inward at
p_{+}
and the orientation of
W_{\beta}
is inward at
q_{+}
.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of
\partial C\cap\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, select an element
x
in the minimal connected component.
In the linear order on the set of connected components of
\partial C\cap\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}
, select an element
y
in the maximal connected component.
Take
A_{C}
to be the preimage by
\varphi
of the unique affine arc whose set of end-points is
\varphi(\{x,y\})
.

Using that the map
\mathsf{O}(k)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Diff(\mathbb{D}^{k})
is an equivalence for
0\leq k\leq 2
(
[
Sm
]
), the moduli space of such arcs
A_{C}\subset C
is contractible.

The containment
A_{C}\subset C
for each incoherent connected component of
\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
implies
A_{C}
and
A_{C^{\prime}}
are disjoint if
C
and
C^{\prime}
are distinct.
Take
A
to be the disjoint union of arcs, one for each incoherent connected component of
\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
:

A~:=~\underset{C~{\rm incoherent}}{\bigsqcup}A_{C}~\subset~\mathbb{D}^{2}~,

which is a properly embedded 1-dimensional submanifold with boundary of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
This concludes Step 1.

As
Step 2
, we use the union of arcs
A\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
to construct the flagged
(\infty,2)
-category
G(\langle W\rangle)
.
As
Step 2a
, we construct an
(\infty,1)
-category the deloop of the free monoid generated by the factors in the alternating factorization of the words
W\cap\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
.
As
Step 2b
, we define
G(\langle W\rangle)
by freely adjoining finitely many 2-cells to this
(\infty,1)
-category.

The complement
\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus A\subset\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
is a finite union of open arcs.
Consider the finite sets

E_{\pm}~:=~\pi_{0}\left(\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus A\right)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad E~:=~E_{-}\amalg E_{+}

of such open arcs.
Consider the
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathfrak{B}\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)
, which is the deloop of the free monoid generated by the finite set
E
.
This concludes Step 2a.

We now implement Step 2b.
The complement
\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus A
is a finite disjoint union of contractible subspaces of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Consider the finite set of such:

F~:=~\pi_{0}\left(\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus A\right)~.

For each
D\in F
consider the subsets

s(D)~:=~\left\{e\in E_{-}\mid e\subset D\right\}~\subset~E_{-}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad t(D)~:=~\left\{e\in E_{+}\mid e\subset D\right\}~\subset~E_{+}~.

The linear orders on each of
E_{\pm}
inherited from the
1^{st}
-coordinate direction of the 2-framing on
\mathbb{D}^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
determines a linear order on each of these subsets.
With these linear orders, we regard
s(D)
as a word in the alphabet
E_{-}
and
t(D)
as a word in the alphabet
E_{+}
.
Invoking Observation
5.9
, we then have two composite maps

s\colon F\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{1}(E_{-})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)\hookleftarrow\mathbb{F}_{1}(E_{+})\longleftarrow F\colon t

from
F
to the underlying 0-type of the free associative algebra on the set
E
.
Define the flagged
(\infty,2)
-category
G\left(\left\langle W\right\rangle\right)
as the pushout:

Put differently,
G\left(\left\langle W\right\rangle\right)
is the flagged
(\infty,2)
-category in which: there is a unique object; its space of 1-endomorphisms is the 0-type of words in the alphabet
E
; for each
D\in F
, there is a (freely) generating 2-morphism from the word
s(D)
to the word
t(D)
.
See Figure
11
.
This concludes Step 2b.

Figure 11
.
Depicted is the flagged
(\infty,2)
-category
G(\langle W\rangle)
, equipped with its morphism

, associated to the 2-cell

depicted in Figure
10
. The flagged
(\infty,2)
-category
G(\langle W\rangle)
is that freely generated by the 0-, 1-, and 2-dimensional cells in this picture. The picture is achieved from that of Figure
10
by collapsing each (dashed orange) arc.

As
Step 3
, we construct a morphism

(32)

c_{2}\xrightarrow{~q~}G(\langle W\rangle)

between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories, as in the diagram (
31
).
Informally,
q
selects the maximal composite 2-cell in
G(\langle W\rangle)
(see Figure
11
).

Through Observation
5.9
, the linear orders on the finite sets
E_{-}
and
E_{+}
respectively define words
s,t\in\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)
.

We therefore have a morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories:

(s,t)\colon\partial c_{2}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{B}\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)\longrightarrow G(\langle W\rangle)~.

(\infty,2)

We do this by induction on the number of connected components of
A
, which is the number of incoherent connected components
C\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
.
Suppose
A=\emptyset
is empty.
Then each of the finite sets
E_{\pm}=\ast
is a singleton, so that
E
has cardinality 2, and
F
is a singleton, and so the canonical morphism
c_{2}=(c_{2})^{\amalg F}\to G(\langle W\rangle)
is the sought functor
q
.
Next, suppose
A
has at least one connected component.
Select a connected component
A_{C}\subset A
.
Isotope the tangle
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
and the arc
A_{C}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
to assume
A_{C}
is an affine arc that is not parallel to the
2^{nd}
coordinate direction of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Projecting the
1^{st}
coordinate direction of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
onto
A_{C}
therefore determines a framing of
A_{C}
.
Consider the union
\Gamma:=\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}\cup A_{C}
.
The framing of
A_{C}
, and the given framings of
\partial_{\pm}\mathbb{D}^{2}
also via the
1^{st}
-coordinate direction of the solid 2-framing of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
, give
\Gamma
the structure of a finite
directed
graph: Its set of vertices is the union
S^{0}\cup\partial A_{C}
, which has cardinality at least 2 and at most 4; its set of directed edges is
\{A_{C}\}\cup\pi_{0}(\partial\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus\left(S^{0}\cup\partial A_{C})\right)
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{C}(\Gamma)
the quiver generated by this finite directed graph: It is the
(\infty,1)
-category freely generated by
\Gamma
.
By design of the arc
A_{C}
, each edge of
\Gamma
determines a word in the alphabet
E
, with the edge
A_{C}
determining the empty word.
We therefore have a morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories

(34)

\mathfrak{C}(\Gamma)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{B}\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)\longrightarrow G(\langle W\rangle)~.

Now, using that the arc
A_{C}
separates
\mathbb{D}^{2}
, denote each connected component of the complement
H_{-}\sqcup H_{+}:=\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus A_{C}
.
The closure
\overline{H}_{\pm}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
is a compact 2-manifold with corners.
Each face of the boundary
\partial\overline{H}_{\pm}
has a given framing.
Further,
\overline{H}_{\pm}
inherits a solid 2-framing from that of
\mathbb{D}^{2}
, and along each face of the boundary
\partial\overline{H}_{\pm}
, the projection of the
1^{st}
coordinate of this solid 2-framing agrees with the given framing.
Choose a piecewise smooth homeomorphism
\mathbb{D}^{2}\xrightarrow{\psi_{\pm}}\overline{H}_{\pm}
that is a diffeomorphism away from the end-points of
A_{C}
, and that respects solid 2-framings and the framings of the faces.
Denote
W_{\pm}:=\psi^{-1}_{\pm}(W)\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
, which are tangles in
\mathbb{D}^{2}
, and they inherit a
[0,1]
-framing from that of
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Consequently, they are selected by morphisms
c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W_{\pm}\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
.
Furthermore,
A_{\pm}:=\psi^{-1}_{\pm}(A\smallsetminus A_{C})\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
is a disjoint union of arcs, one for each incoherent connected component
\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W_{\pm}
, each that satisfies the characteristic conditions (A.1)-(A.3) above.
By construction, there are strictly fewer connected components of each of
A_{\pm}
than there are of
A
.
By induction on the number of connected components of
A
,
we therefore have fillers

Consider the solid diagram among flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories,

(36)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\partial c_{2})^{\amalg\{\pm\}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}(\Gamma)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\partial c_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(c_{2})^{\amalg\{\pm\}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{0}_{\pm}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\pm}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in which
\mathcal{C}
defined as the pushout of the left square.
Being a pushout, the diagram (
35
) supplies a canonical morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories

(37)

\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow G(\langle W\rangle)~.

Observe that a 2-categorical composition of the two 2-cells
(c_{2})^{\amalg\{\pm\}}\xrightarrow{h_{\pm}}\mathcal{C}
in
\mathcal{C}
supplies a filler in (
36
).
Finally, define the sought filler (
33
) to be the composition

q\colon c_{2}\xrightarrow{~f~}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{~(\ref{s25})~}G(\langle W\rangle)~.

This concludes Step 3.

As
Step 4
, we construct a factorization

(38)

\hbox to188.25pt{\vbox to59.86pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 94.12279pt\lower-33.35474pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

For each
e\in E
, the intersection
W\cap e\subset e
is a finite set.
The
1^{st}
-coordinate of the solid 2-framing on
\mathbb{D}^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
determines an orientation on
e
, which thereafter induces a linear ordering on this finite set
W\cap e
.
Furthermore, the
[0,1]
-framing of
W\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
determines a map
W\cap e\to\{0,1\}
.
In this way, through Observation
5.9
, we have a map

(39)

E\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0,1\})\underset{\rm Lem~\ref{t.bord.obj}}{\hookrightarrow}\Bord^{[0,1]}~.

By design, the map (
39
) takes values in words in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
that are
alternating
.
Via the monoidal structure on the
(\infty,1)
-category
\Bord^{[0,1]}
, this map uniquely extends as a monoidal functor

(40)

\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)\longrightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}~.

By definition of the categorical deloop, this is a morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories:

(41)

\mathfrak{B}\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}~.

Next, let
D\in F
.
Note that, by design, the map

(42)

F\amalg F\xrightarrow{~\langle s,t\rangle~}\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)\xrightarrow{~(\ref{s6})~}\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0,1\})

takes values in words in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
that are
alternating
.
Consider the intersection of the closure of
D
with the tangle
W
:

W_{D}~:=~W\cap\overline{D}~.

Consider the quotient of the closure of
D
by each arc contained in it:

R_{D}~:=~\overline{D}_{/\sim}~,

where
x\sim y
if
x=y
or both
x
and
y
belong to the same connected component of
A\cap\overline{D}
.
As
\overline{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is isomorphic with a polygon, then
R_{D}
is isomorphic with a polygon collapse some (but not all) of its edges, and is therefore a compact surface with corners that refines a closed 2-disk.
Furthermore,
R_{D}
inherits a solid 2-framing from that of
\overline{D}\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
.
Using that the compact subspaces
W,A\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}
are disjoint, the quotient map
\overline{D}\to R_{D}
restricts to
W_{D}\subset\overline{D}
as an isomorphism onto its image, which we again denote as

W_{D}~\subset~R_{D}~.

This subspace is a tangle in a solidly 2-framed stratified manifold with corners refining a closed 2-disk, and it inherits a
[0,1]
-framing from that of
W_{D}\subset\overline{D}
.
By definition of
\Bord^{[0,1]}
, this tangle
W_{D}\subset R_{D}
together with this
[0,1]
-framing define a morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories:

c_{2}\xrightarrow{~\langle W_{D}\rangle~}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}~.

By design, the resulting diagram among flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\partial c_{2})^{\amalg F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(c_{2})^{\amalg F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}\mathbb{F}_{1}(E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\langle W_{D}\rangle)_{D\in F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s,t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{s8})}

commutes.
By definition of the flagged
(\infty,2)
-category
G\left(\left\langle W\right\rangle\right)
as a pushout, this diagram is a morphism between flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories:

(43)

G\left(\left\langle W\right\rangle\right)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}~.

Using that
W=\underset{D\in F}{\bigcup}W_{D}\subset\underset{D\in F}{\bigcup}\overline{D}=\mathbb{D}^{2}
, the diagram among flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories

(44)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G(\langle W\rangle)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle W\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ref{s9})}

commutes.
This concludes Step 4.
Note how, by design, this factorization of
\langle W\rangle
has the property that its restriction to each of the 1-cells
c_{1}\xrightarrow{s,t}c_{2}
is the coarsest
alternating
factorization of the words selected by the composite morphisms
c_{1}\xrightarrow{s,t}c_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
.

Step 5
, we construct a factorization

Using that the flagged
(\infty,2)
-category is, in fact, a flagged
(\infty,1)
-category, Lemma
5.12
supplies a unique filler (a).
Using that the map (
42
) takes values in alternating words,
Lemma
5.13
supplies the filler (b).
This concludes Step 5.
In doing so we have constructed the diagram among
(\infty,2)
-categories (
31
), and have constructed the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on 2-cells in
\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
.

5.2.3.
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on general objects

We now define the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on a general object
\left(T\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)\in(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
.
Write
T=[p]([q_{1}],\dots,[q_{p}])
.
If
p=0
, then
T
is a 1-category, which was considered in a previous subsection.
So suppose
p>0
.
The canonical diagram

\textstyle{\{1\}()\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{1<\dots<p\}([q_{2}],\dots,[q_{p}])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{0<1\}([q_{1}])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[p]([q_{1}],\dots,[q_{p}])}

is a pushout in
\bTheta_{2}
, and therefore also defines a pushout in
(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
.
Therefore, the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}\xrightarrow{(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}}(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Dual}
on
T
can be defined as a pushout of the values of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on the other terms in this square.
Via induction on
p
, we are reduced to the cases in which
T=[1]([q])
or
T=c_{0}
.
Suppose
T=[1]([q])
.
If
q=0
, then
T
is a 1-category, and it is an instance of a case considered in the previous subsection.
So suppose
q>0
.
The canonical diagram

\textstyle{[1](\{1\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[1](\{1<\dots<q\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[1](\{0<1\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[1]([q])}

is a pushout in
\bTheta_{2}
, and therefore also defines a pushout in
(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
.
Therefore, the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}\xrightarrow{(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}}(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Dual}
on
T
can be defined as a pushout of the values of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on the other terms in this square.
Via induction on
q
, we are reduced to the cases in which
T=c_{k}
for
0\leq k\leq 2
.
These cases were addressed in the previous subsections.
In this way, we have defined the value of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}\xrightarrow{(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}}(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Dual}
on each object
\left(T\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)
.

5.2.4.
Functoriality of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}

We now argue that the above-defined values of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
assemble as a functor over
(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
.

We first make some observations about the subspace
A\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}\smallsetminus W
of subsection §
5.2.2
.
Let
\left(T\xrightarrow{\langle W\rangle}\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\right)
be an object in

(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
.
Write
T=[p]([q_{1}],\dots,[q_{p}])
.
For each
0<i\leq p
, and each
0<j\leq q_{i}
, the inclusion
c_{2}\simeq\{i-1<i\}(\{j-1<j\})\hookrightarrow T
determines an embedding between stratified spaces
\mathbb{D}^{2}=\langle c_{2}\rangle\hookrightarrow\langle T\rangle
from the hemispherically stratified 2-disk.
Denote the image of this embedding as
\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\subset\langle T\rangle
.
Similarly, for each
0<i\leq p
, and each
0\leq j\leq q_{i}
, the inclusion
c_{1}\simeq\{i-1<i\}(\{j\})\hookrightarrow T
determines an embedding between stratified spaces
\mathbb{D}^{1}=\langle c_{1}\rangle\hookrightarrow\langle T\rangle
from the hemispherically stratified 1-disk.
Denote the image of this embedding as
\mathbb{D}^{1}_{i,j}\subset\langle T\rangle
.
For each
0<i\leq p
, and each
0<j\leq q_{i}
, recognize the upper- and lower-hemispheres:

\mathbb{D}^{1}_{i,j-1}~=~\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbb{D}^{1}_{i,j}~=~\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}~.

Denote the intersection of the
[0,1]
-framed tangle
W\subset\langle T\rangle
with each of these subspaces as

W_{i,j}~:=~W\cap\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}~\subset~\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}~,

which are, themselves,
[0,1]
-framed tangles.

Recall from the argument of subsection
5.2.2
that the factorization (
44
) restricts along each of the generating 1-cells of
c_{2}
as the coarsest alternating factorization of word in the alphabet
\{0,1\}
selected by each such 1-cell.
As such,
\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\cap A_{i,j}\subset\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}=\mathbb{D}^{1}_{i,j}
is isotopic in
\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j+1}\cap A_{i,j+1}\subset\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}=\mathbb{D}^{1}_{i,j}
.
Invoking the isotopy extension theorem, choose isotopies of each pair
(\partial A_{i,j}\subset A_{i,j})\subset\left(\partial(\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\smallsetminus W_{i,j})\subset\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\smallsetminus W\right)

to ensure the following properties.

•

For each
0<i\leq p
and each
0<j<q_{i}
, there is an equality between finite subsets of
\mathbb{D}^{1}_{i,j}
:

\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\cap A_{i,j}~=~\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j+1}\cap A_{i,j+1}~.

•

For each
0<i\leq p
, the union of arcs
\underset{0<j\leq q_{i}}{\bigcup}A_{i,j}\subset\left\langle\{i-1<i\}([q_{i}])\right\rangle
is a smooth properly embedded 1-submanifold with boundary that is transverse to each stratum of
\left\langle\{i-1<i\}([q_{i}])\right\rangle
.

In this way, we can choose a finite disjoint union of (closed) arcs
A\subset\langle T\rangle\smallsetminus W
that is transverse to the strata of
\langle T\rangle
, each of whose end-points lies in the boundary of
\langle T\rangle\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and such that, for each
0<i\leq p
and
0<j\leq q_{p}
, the intersection
\mathbb{D}^{2}_{i,j}\cap A
is the disjoint union of arcs of the subsection §
5.2.2
.

We now explain how the values of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
established in the previous subsection canonically assemble as a functor.
Let
[k]\xrightarrow{\langle T_{0}\to\cdots\to T_{k}\to\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\rangle}(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
be a functor from an object
[k]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
.
The values of
(\bTheta_{2})_{/L}
on object supplies lifts in the diagram among flagged
(\infty,2)
-categories

(46)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}\Dual}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

such that each right-angled triangle therein commutes.
We must explain how this is a commutative diagram.
Through the functor
\bTheta_{2}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\langle-\rangle}\cMfd_{2}^{\sf sfr}
, the functor
[k]\xrightarrow{\langle T_{\bullet}\to\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}\rangle}(\bTheta_{2})_{/\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
is the data of a proper constructible bundle
X\to\Delta^{k}
equipped with a fiberwise solid 2-framing, equipped with a compact subspace
W\subset X
that is a fiberwise (over the standardly stratified simplex
\Delta^{k}
) tangle equipped with a fiberwise
[0,1]
-framing together with, for each
0\leq\ell\leq k
, an identification of the fiber over
\Delta^{\{\ell\}}\subset\Delta^{k}
,

(W_{|\Delta^{\{\ell\}}}\subset X_{|\Delta^{\{\ell\}}})~\cong~(W_{\ell}\subset\langle T_{\ell}\rangle)

with the
[0,1]
-framed tangle selected by
T_{\ell}\to\mathfrak{B}\Bord^{[0,1]}
.
Commutativity of (
46
) follows upon observing that there exists a compact subspace
A\subset X\smallsetminus W
whose projection
A\to\Delta^{k}
is a constructible bundle, and that is fiberwise transverse to the strata of
X
, and such that, for each
0\leq\ell\leq k
, the intersection
A\cap\left(\langle T_{\ell}\rangle\smallsetminus W\right)
is a subspace isotopic (through properly embedded 1-submanifolds with boundary that are transverse to the strata of
\langle T_{\ell}\rangle
) with the subspace from the paragraph above.

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.11
.

5.3.
Inductive step

This section establishes that the diagrams

\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j+1]}}

and

\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto i}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[1,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto i}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i+1,j]}}

are pushouts of monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories. See Lemma
5.17
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
A
and
B
in
\Alg(\Spaces)
be monoids. Assume further that the unit maps
\ast\rightarrow A
and
\ast\rightarrow B
are monomorphisms, and let
\overline{A}=A\smallsetminus\ast
and
\overline{B}=B\smallsetminus\ast
denote the complements of the identity components.
Then the underlying space of the coproduct of monoids is
