A\star B\simeq\ast\amalg\coprod_{k\geq 0}\overline{A}\times(\overline{B}\times\overline{A})^{k}\amalg\overline{B}\times(\overline{A}\times\overline{B})^{k}\amalg(\overline{A}\times\overline{B})^{k+1}\amalg(\overline{B}\times\overline{A})^{k+1}~.

Proof.

Consider the adjunction
\Alg^{\sf nu}(\Spaces)\leftrightarrows\Alg(\Spaces)
, where the left adjoint adds a basepoint, sending a nonunital monoid
M
to
M_{\ast}=M\amalg\ast
, and the right adjoint is the forgetful functor. The condition that the unit is a monomorphism implies that the monoids
A
and
B
are in the image of the left adjoint. That is, there are equivalences
A\simeq\overline{A}_{\ast}
and
B\simeq\overline{B}_{\ast}
. Since left adjoints preserves coproducts, we have an equivalence
A\star B\simeq(\overline{A}\star\overline{B})_{\ast}
, where
\overline{A}\star\overline{B}
is the coproduct in nonunital monoids.

The coproduct of nonunital monoids is computed by the standard formula in terms of words alternating in
\overline{A}
and
\overline{B}
:

\overline{A}\star\overline{B}=\overline{A}\amalg\overline{B}\amalg(\overline{A}\times\overline{B})\amalg(\overline{B}\times\overline{A})\amalg(\overline{A}\times\overline{B}\times\overline{A})\amalg(\overline{B}\times\overline{A}\times\overline{B})\amalg\ldots

the terms of which can be regrouped as:

\overline{A}\star\overline{B}=\coprod_{k\geq 0}\overline{A}\times(\overline{B}\times\overline{A})^{k}\amalg\overline{B}\times(\overline{A}\times\overline{B})^{k}\amalg(\overline{A}\times\overline{B})^{k+1}\amalg(\overline{B}\times\overline{A})^{k+1}~.

The formula then follows by taking disjoint union with the identity element.
∎

Lemma 5.15
.

Consider the monomorphism of spaces

\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0,1\})\simeq\obj\bigl(\Bord^{[0,1]}\bigr)=\Bord^{[0,1]}[0]\longrightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]

and let
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\subset\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]
be the submonoid of tangles
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
for which no component of
W
corresponds to the identity morphism of
\{0\}
. That is,
W
has no component which is a horizontal tangle whose framing is given by the label
\{0\}
.
The natural map from the coproduct of monoids

\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\longrightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Given a tangle
W\in\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]\simeq\Tang^{[0,1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])
, we can decompose
W
as
W\cong W_{B}\amalg W_{0}
, where
W_{B}
is the maximal sub-tangle of
W
contained in
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}
, and
W_{0}
is the complement of
W_{B}
, which is the maximal sub-tangle of
W
contained in
\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]
. The assignment
W\mapsto W_{0}
defines a map of spaces
\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]\rightarrow\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]
. For each choice of a tangle
W_{0}\in\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]
, there is a fiber square

\textstyle{B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\cap\Tang^{[0,1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\smallsetminus W_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{W_{0}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]}

where
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\cap\Tang^{[0,1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\smallsetminus W_{0})
is the space of tangles
W^{\prime}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\smallsetminus W_{0})
no component of which is a horizontal tangle labeled by
\{0\}
.
There is a diffeomorphism

\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\smallsetminus W_{0}\cong\coprod_{|\pi_{0}W_{0}|+1}\mathbb{D}[p]\times\mathbb{R}^{1}

so there exists a equivalence of the fiber

B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\cap\Tang^{[0,1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\smallsetminus W_{0})\simeq\prod_{|\pi_{0}W_{0}|+1}B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}~.

Likewise, we can consider the fiber over an element
W_{0}\in\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]
of the canonical map

\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\longrightarrow\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]

defined by the monoid map
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\rightarrow\ast
.
By Definition
5.2
, the space

\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\simeq\coprod_{k\geq 0}\Conf_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\underset{\Sigma_{k}}{\times}\{0\}^{k}\simeq\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0\})

is the free monoid on a single element
\{0\}
. So we are considering, for
A=\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0\})
, the fiber of the map

\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0\})\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0\})

over
W_{0}\simeq\left(0,\dots,0\right)
, which is a length
k
word in the alphabet
\{0\}
for some
k\geq 0
.
Since the unit element of
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}
(indexing the empty tangle) is a disjoint and contractible component, the fiber is
(B^{(0,1]}_{[p]})^{k+1}
.

Consequently, the fiber of the map
\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\rightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]
over each
W_{0}\in\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]
induces an equivalence on fibers

\bigl(B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\bigr)^{k+1}\simeq\prod_{|\pi_{0}W_{0}|+1}B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}

for
k=|\pi_{0}W_{0}|
. This implies that the map
\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\rightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]
is an equivalence.
∎

Corollary 5.16
.

Recall the notation of Lemma
5.15
, where
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\subset\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]
is again the subspace of tangles
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
for which no component of
W
is in the image of
\{0\}
under the map
\mathbb{F}_{1}(\{0,1\})\simeq\obj\bigl(\Bord^{[0,1]}\bigr)\rightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]
. Then there is a pushout of monoids:

\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}}

where
\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}
is the coproduct of
B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}
and
\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]
in
\Alg(\Spaces)
.

Proof.

Using the equivalence
\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\simeq\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]
of Lemma
5.15
, we obtain

\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]\simeq\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]}{\star}\bigl(\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}\bigr)\simeq\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}~.

∎

Lemma 5.17
.

The commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j+1]}}

is a pushout of monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories.

Proof.

This proof relies on Lemmas
5.21
and
5.22
, which are stated and proved below.

Let
\Bord^{[a,b]}_{\bullet}
denote the simplicial monoidal space, given by the value of the forgetful functor

\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\rightarrow\Fun\bigl(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Alg(\Spaces)\bigr)

on the monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\Bord^{[a,b]}
. Consider the pushout

\Bord^{[i,j]}_{\bullet}\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}_{\bullet}}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}_{\bullet}

in
\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Alg(\Spaces))
, which takes values

[p]\mapsto\Bord^{[i,j]}[p]\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}[p]}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}[p]~,

the pushout in
\Alg(\Spaces)
of the monoidal spaces of
[p]
-simplices. We prove that the natural morphism

(47)

\Seg\Bigl(\Bord^{[i,j]}_{\bullet}\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}_{\bullet}}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}_{\bullet}\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Bord^{[i,j+1]}_{\bullet}

is an equivalence. By Lemma
3.32
, this will imply that
\Bord^{[i,j+1]}_{\bullet}
is the universal Segal space under the colimit of
\Bord^{[0,1]}_{\bullet}\leftarrow{\Bord^{[0,0]}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Bord^{[i,j]}_{\bullet}
. Since Segal-completion preserves colimits, being a left adjoint, this will show that
\Bord^{[i,j+1]}
is the colimit of
\Bord^{[0,1]}\leftarrow{\Bord^{[0,0]}}\rightarrow\Bord^{[i,j]}
in monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories.

Consider the functor
F
defined as the composite in the following diagram:

(48)

\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})_{/[p]^{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\spaces}
\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}[\Spaces]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}}
\textstyle{\Morita[\Spaces]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sf LIM}

To prove that the map (
47
) is an equivalence, we show that the colimit of the composite functor
F
gives the
p
-simplices
\Bord^{[i,j+1]}[p]
. That is, by construction of the functor
\Seg
, it will imply that the map (
47
) is an equivalence if the map

\colim\Bigl(\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})_{/[p]^{\circ}}\xrightarrow{F}\Spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Bord^{[i,j+1]}[p]

is an equivalence. We will compute this colimit after restricting along a final functor.
Consider a closed interval
I
, and fix an object
P\in\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]}
with
\pi_{0}([0,1]\smallsetminus P)=[p]
. From Lemma
4.6
, the colimit of the functor
F
can be computed after restriction along

\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\op}_{/P}\rightarrow\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})^{\op}_{/[p]^{\circ}}~.

We are thereby reduced to proving the map

\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{F}\Spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Bord^{[i,j+1]}[p]

is an equivalence,
where
F
is the restriction of
F
to
\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\op}_{/P}
.

First, we make use of an explicit topological space
\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}
for which there is a natural homotopy equivalence
\Bord^{[i,j+1]}[p]\simeq\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}
. See Definition
5.18
.

Second, in Definition
5.19
we construct a functor

\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\op}_{/P}\overset{\mathfrak{U}}{\longrightarrow}\Opens\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}\Bigr)

assigning to an object
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
an open subspace
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
of
\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}
. We then prove:

•

Lemma
5.21
: For any
W\in\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}
, then the subcategory

\Bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\Bigr)_{W}\subset\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}

of
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
such that there is containment
W\in\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
has contractible classifying space.
Consequently, applying the higher Seifert–van Kampen theorem of
[
Lu2
]
(specifically Theorem A.3.1), there is a natural homotopy equivalence

\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}\simeq\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}}\Opens\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\rm forget}\Spaces\Bigr)~.

•

Lemma
5.22
:
There is a natural equivalence

\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq F(P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B)

as functors from
\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{I})_{/P}
to
\Spaces
.

The above results, Lemma
5.21
and Lemma
5.22
, then imply the equivalence

\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{I})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{F}\Spaces\Bigr)\simeq\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}

which completes the proof.
∎

The following definitions and results support the above proof of Lemma
5.17
, starting with Definition
5.18
and leading to Lemma
5.23
.

Definition 5.18
.

Let
P\subset[0,1]
be an object of
\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]}
, with
\iota P\subset P
the components of
P
which map to the interior of
[0,1]
. The subspace

\Tang_{1}^{[i,j]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}\subset\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])

consists of those tangles
W
such that:

•

the critical values of the composite projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1]\rightarrow[0,1]
are contained in
\iota P
;

•

the intersection
W\cap\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\{t\}
is an object of
\Bord^{[i,j]}
for
t\in\{0,1\}
.

Definition 5.19
.

The functor

\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}}
\textstyle{\Opens\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}\Bigr)}

sends an object
\bigl(P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B\bigr)
to the open subspace
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
, defined by:

\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}:=\Bigl\{(W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])\in\Tang_{1}^{[i.j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{A}\Big|\ j\text{-splitting relative }B\Bigr\}

where
W
is a properly-embedded 1-dimensional submanifold with no closed components,
the projection
W\to[0,1]
is transverse to every point in the complement of
A
and satisfying the following
j
-splitting condition
relative to
B
:

•

for each pair of consecutive components of
[0,1]\smallsetminus B
, there exists elements
t,t^{\prime}
of these components such that the intersection

W\cap\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[t,t^{\prime}]\bigr)

has the property that there is a finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
for which:

–

W\cap\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[t,t^{\prime}]\bigr)
is disjoint from
S\times[0,1]
;

–

for any consecutive elements
s
,
s^{\prime}
in
S\cup\{-\oo,\oo\}
, the intersection

W\cap[s,s^{\prime}]\times[t,t^{\prime}]
is either
[i,j]
-framed or a component of this intersection is
\{j+1\}
-framed if its end-points map surjectively onto
\{t,t^{\prime}\}
.

Remark 5.20
.

Intuitively, the
[i,j+1]
-framed tangle
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1]
satisfies the
j
-splitting condition relative to
B
if, over each connected component of
B
in the interior of
[0,1]
, it can be split as a disjoint union of tangles each of which is either
[i,j]
-framed or
\{j,j+1\}
-framed.
See Figure
12
.

Figure 12
.
A
[i,j+1]
-framed tangle (in orange)
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1]
such that the critical values of the projection
W\to[0,1]
are contained in
A
and that satisfies the
j
-splitting condition relative to
B
.
The (purple) horizontal lines depict representatives
t\in[0,1]\smallsetminus B
of each connected component, as in Definition
5.18
.
The (red) vertical line segments depict a choice of
S\cup\{\pm\infty\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\cup\{\pm\infty\}
for each connected component of
B
.

Lemma 5.21
.

For any
W\in\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{P}
, the full subcategory

\Bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\Bigr)_{W}~\subset~\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}~,

consisting of those
(P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B)
such that
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
contains
W
, has contractible classifying space.

Proof.

First, we prove the category
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
is nonempty. For
B\in\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]}
, denote by
\iota B\subset B
the components of
B
contained in the interior of
[0,1]
. We claim that there exists
B\in\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]}
such that:

•

\iota B
contains the critical values of the composite projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1]\rightarrow[0,1]
;

•

In any consecutive components of
[0,1]\smallsetminus B
there exists elements
t
and
t^{\prime}
and a finite set
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
such that for any successive elements
s,s^{\prime}
of
S
, the intersection of
W
with
[t,t^{\prime}]\times[s,s^{\prime}]
is either empty or connected.

Before proving that such a
B
exists, we first observe that this existence implies that
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
is nonempty. Given such a
B
, define
A:=B\cap P
, and note that this defines an object of
\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]/P}
, and thus that
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
then defines an object of
\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}
.
The subset
A
contains the critical values of the projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1]\rightarrow[0,1]
, since both
P
and
B
do.
The
j
-splitting condition for
W
relative to
B
follows immediately, because:
every framed connected tangle is
\{k,k+1\}
-framed for some integer
k
, and therefore every every connected
[i,j+1]
-framed tangle satisfies the
j
-splitting condition since it is either
[i,j]
-framed or
\{j,j+1\}
-framed.

We now prove
B
exists. For each element
x\in[0,1]
, there exists a closed neighborhood
D_{x}
such that the connected components of
W\cap\mathbb{R}^{1}\times D_{x}
are split: that is, there exists a finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
such that for any successive elements
s,s^{\prime}
of
S
, the intersection of
W
with
[s,s^{\prime}]\times D_{x}
is either empty or connected. By compactness of
[0,1]
, there exists a minimal finite subset
T\subset[0,1]
for which, for each
x\in T
, the interiors
\overset{\circ}{D}_{x}
cover the interval
(0,1)
. For each pair of consecutive elements
x,x^{\prime}
of
T
, choose an element
y\in\overset{\circ}{D}_{x}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{x^{\prime}}
which is also a regular value of the composite projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1]\rightarrow[0,1]
, and set
Y
to be this finite set of elements
y
. Setting
B
to be the complement
B:=[0,1]\smallsetminus Y
, we see that
W
satisfies splitting with respect to
B
, since any consecutive element
y,y^{\prime}
of
Y=[0,1]\smallsetminus B
lie in a single open
\overset{\circ}{D}_{x}
for some
x\in T
.

Second, we now prove
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
has contractible classifying space. We use the following condition for contractibility:

•

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a connected category such that for every functor
G:\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
from a connected poset
\mathcal{K}
, there exists a factorization

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{G}}

where
\overline{\mathcal{K}}
is a category with contractible classifying space. Then
\mathcal{T}
has contractible classifying space.

We establish this factorization for
\mathcal{T}=\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
using the following construction: For any pair of objects
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
and
P\leftarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow B^{\prime}
of
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
, observe that both
P\leftarrow A\cap A^{\prime}\rightarrow B
and
P\leftarrow A\cap A^{\prime}\rightarrow B\cap B^{\prime}
form objects of

\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}
. Further,
\iota(A\cap A^{\prime})
contains the critical values of
W\rightarrow[0,1]
, and
W
satisfies the
j
-splitting condition relative to
B\cap B^{\prime}
.

We can thus form a pair of cospans in
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces A\cap A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\cap A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\cap A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B\cap B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B^{\prime}}

which shows that
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
is connected.

Now let
G:\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
be any functor from a connected poset. We can construct a factorization:

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{G}}

with

\overline{\mathcal{K}}=\ast\underset{\mathcal{K}\times\{-\}}{\coprod}\mathcal{K}\times\{-\rightarrow 0\leftarrow+\}

where
\{-\rightarrow 0\leftarrow+\}
is the 3-object category which corepresents cospans, and the functor
\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{K}}
is given by the inclusion of
\mathcal{K}\times\{+\}
. The functor
\overline{G}
is characterized by declaring, for each
k\in\mathcal{K}
, its restriction along
\{k\},\{-\rightarrow 0\leftarrow+\}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{K}}
to be the cospan

\textstyle{\underset{x\in\mathcal{K}}{\bigcap}\ev_{s}(G(x))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\underset{x\in\mathcal{K}}{\bigcap}\ev_{s}(G(x))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ev_{s}(G(k))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{x\in\mathcal{K}}{\bigcap}\ev_{t}(G(x))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ev_{t}(G(k))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ev_{t}(G(k)).}

The classifying space of
\overline{\mathcal{K}}
is contractible, so this proves that the classifying space of
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
contractible.
∎

Recall the composite functor
F
from (
48
).

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be an object of
\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})
. There is a natural equivalence

\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq F(\pi A\rightarrow\pi B)

between the open
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\subset\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{A}
and the value on
A\rightarrow B
of the composite

\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\overset{F}{\longrightarrow}\Spaces~.

Proof.

Denote the morphism
\pi A\to\pi B
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}
as
[a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{g^{\circ}}[b]^{\circ}
.
Here we reduce the proof for general morphisms
[b]\xrightarrow{g}[a]
to the case
b=1
and
g
the unique active morphism. This special case is then proved in Lemma
5.23
.

By Observation
3.26
, the value
F([a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{g^{\circ}}[b]^{\circ})
is given by the iterated fiber product:

F([0,g(0)])\underset{F(g(0))}{\times}F([g(0),g(1)])\underset{F(g(1))}{\times}\ldots\underset{F(g(b))}{\times}F([g(b),a])

where

F([k,l])=\Bord^{[i,j]}[k,l]\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}[k,l]}{\amalg}\Bord^{[0,1]}[k,l]

and

F(\{k\})=\Bord^{[i,j]}\{k\}\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}\{k\}}{\amalg}\Bord^{[0,1]}\{k\}~.

Likewise,
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
has a cover by smaller values of
\mathfrak{U}
: for
B\subset[0,1]
with
b+1
connected components, write

B=\coprod_{0\leq k\leq b}B_{k}

as the ordered disjoint union of connected components. Define

\widehat{B_{0}}=\bigl\{t\in[0,1]\ \big|\ 0\leq t\leq{\sf inf}(B_{1})\bigr\}

\widehat{B_{k}}=\bigl\{t\in[0,1]\ \big|\ {\sf sup}(B_{{\mathit{k}\text{-}1}})\leq t\leq{\sf inf}(B_{k+1})\bigr\}

\widehat{B_{b}}=\bigl\{t\in[0,1]\ \big|\ {\sf sup}(B_{b-1})\leq t\leq 1\bigr\}

for the largest connected sub-interval of
[0,1]
containing
B_{k}
which does not intersect the adjacent components
B_{{\mathit{k}\text{-}1}}
or
B_{k+1}
. Note that the
\widehat{B_{k}}
form a closed cover of
[0,1]
. We can thus decompose
\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{A}
as the fiber product

\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{0}})_{A_{0}}\underset{\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{0}}\cap\widehat{B_{1}})_{\emptyset}}{\times}\ldots\underset{\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{b-1}}\cap\widehat{B_{b}})_{\emptyset}}{\times}\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{n}})_{A_{b}}

where
\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{k}})_{A_{k}}
consists of tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{k}}
whose critical values are contained in
A_{k}:=A\cap B_{k}
, and
\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{\n 1}}\cap\widehat{B_{n}})_{\emptyset}
consists of tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{\n 1}}\cap\widehat{B_{n}}
which have no critical points. Note that
\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{\n 1}}\cap\widehat{B_{n}})_{\emptyset}
is homotopy equivalent to the space of objects of
\Bord^{[i,j+1]}
.

Restricting to the subspace
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\subset\Tang_{1}^{[i,j+1]}(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[0,1])_{A}
, it is immediate that a tangle
W
satisfies the
j
-splitting condition relative to
B
if and only if for each
k
each restriction
W_{|\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\widehat{B_{k}}}
satisfies the
j
-splitting condition relative to
B_{k}
. We can thus decompose
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
as a fiber product

\mathfrak{U}_{A_{1}\rightarrow B_{1}}\underset{\mathfrak{U}_{\emptyset\to C_{1}}}{\times}\ldots\underset{\mathfrak{U}_{\emptyset\to C_{b}}}{\times}\mathfrak{U}_{A_{b}\rightarrow B_{b}}~,

where
C_{k}
is the interior of
\widehat{B_{k\text{-}1}}\cap\widehat{B_{k}}
.
By the parameterized isotopy extension theorem, the restrictions maps in this fiber product are fibrations, so the point-set fiber product agrees with the homotopy fiber product.

To complete the proof, we can thereby reduce to the case where each
A_{i}\rightarrow B_{i}
corresponds to an active morphism
\pi A=[a]^{\circ}\rightarrow[1]^{\circ}=\pi B
. This case is proved in Lemma
5.23
.
∎

We have reduced Lemma
5.22
to the simpler subordinate case in which the object
[a]^{\circ}\rightarrow[b]^{\circ}
is the active morphism
[a]^{\circ}\rightarrow[1]^{\circ}
.

Lemma 5.23
.

For
(A\rightarrow B)\in\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})
an object with
\pi B=\{0<a\}
and
\pi A=[a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\act}\{0<a\}^{\circ}=\pi B
the active morphism, the space
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
is homotopy equivalent to the pushout in
\Alg(\Spaces)

\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}[a]}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}[a]

of the monoidal spaces of
[a]
-points.

Proof.

In the case at hand, the space
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
consists of tangles
W
with critical values contained in
A
such that there exists elements
t,t^{\prime}
(with
\iota A\subset\iota B\subset[t,t^{\prime}]
) of these components such that the intersection

W\cap\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[t,t^{\prime}]\bigr)

has the property that there is a finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
for which
W\cap\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{1}\times[t,t^{\prime}]\bigr)
is disjoint from
S\times[0,1]
, and for any successive elements
s
,
s^{\prime}
in
S
, the intersection of
W
with
[s,s^{\prime}]\times[t,t^{\prime}]
satisfies the
j
-splitting condition.

For each
r\geq 0
, consider the subspaces

\mathfrak{U}^{\{1,\ldots,r\}}_{A\rightarrow B}\subset\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}\subset\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}

where:

•

\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}\subset\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
consists of those tangles
W
such that there exists a cardinality
r
subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
with the
j
-splitting property relative to
W
(see Definition
5.19
), and any other subset
S^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
with the
j
-splitting property relative to
W
has cardinality at least
r
;

•

\mathfrak{U}^{\{1,\ldots,r\}}_{A\rightarrow B}\subset\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}
consists of those
W
which have the
j
-splitting property relative to the fixed subset
S=\{1,\ldots,r\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1}
.

Note that
\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}
is a union of components of
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
: A tangle
W\in\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
belongs to
\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}
if and only if every isotopic tangle
W^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}
belongs to
\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}
. Consequently, there is a decomposition

\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq\{\emptyset\}\amalg\coprod_{r\geq 0}\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}~.

Further, via a straight-line ambient isotopy, there is a deformation retraction of
\mathfrak{U}^{r}_{A\rightarrow B}
onto
\mathfrak{U}^{\{1,\ldots,r\}}_{A\rightarrow B}
. For
r=2k-1
or
r=2k
, respectively, the space
\mathfrak{U}^{\{1,\ldots,r\}}_{A\rightarrow B}
is homotopy equivalent to

\Bigl(\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}\times\overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}}\Bigr)^{k}\amalg\Bigl(\overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}}\times\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}\Bigr)^{k}

or, respectively,

\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}\times\Bigr(\overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}}\times\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}\Bigl)^{k}\amalg\ \overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}}\times\Bigl(\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}\times\overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[p]}}\Bigr)^{k}

where
\overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}}
and
\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}
denote the spaces
\overline{B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}}
and
\overline{\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]}
minus the unit (i.e., the 1-point component
\{\emptyset\}
containing the empty tangle).

By Corollary
5.16
, we have an equivalence between spaces:

\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]\underset{\Bord^{[0,0]}[a]}{\star}\Bord^{[0,1]}[a]\simeq\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}~.

The equivalence
\mathfrak{U}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq\Bord^{[i,j]}[a]\star B^{(0,1]}_{[a]}
now follows by Lemma
5.14
, which implies the result.
∎

We can now prove the main theorem of this section, which identifies the rigid monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category
\Dual^{[\oo,\oo]}
freely generated by a single object. For earlier work on freely generated monoidal
(1,1)
-categories, see
[
JS1
]
and
[
JS2
]
.

Theorem 5.24
.

The natural functor

\Dual^{[\oo,\oo]}\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})

is an equivalence. That is,
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is equivalent to the universal monoidal
(\oo,1)
-category containing an object which has all left-duals and all right-duals.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.11
, the natural functors

\Dual^{[0,1]}\rightarrow\Bord^{[0,1]}

\Dual^{[1,0]}\rightarrow\Bord^{[1,0]}

are equivalences.

By Lemma
5.17
, the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto j}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto 0}
\textstyle{\Bord^{[i,j+1]}}

is a pushout of monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories. For
0\leq i,j<\oo
, the diagram

\textstyle{\Dual^{[0,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle D^{\mathsf{R}^{j}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{\Dual^{[i,j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{\Dual^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle D^{\mathsf{R}^{j}}\rangle}
\textstyle{\Dual^{[i,j+1]}}

is a pushout in the
(\oo,1)
-category
\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})
of monoidal
(\oo,1)
-categories. This follows immediately from the universal property defining
\Dual^{[i,j]}
. Consequently, we obtain that the natural transformation

\Dual^{[\bullet,\bullet]}\longrightarrow\Bord^{[\bullet,\bullet]}

is an equivalence in
\Fun(\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N},\Alg(\Cat_{(\oo,1)}))
. Lastly, the natural functor

\textstyle{\underset{(i,j)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}{\colim}\Bord^{[i.j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})}

is an equivalence by inspection: Each of the functors
\Bord^{[i,j]}\rightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
is fully-faithful, and every object of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})
belongs to
\Bord^{[i,j]}
for some
i
and
j
. That the likewise colimit

\textstyle{\underset{(i,j)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}{\colim}\Dual^{[i.j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Dual^{[\oo,\oo]}}

is an equivalence again follows immediately from the universal property of
\Dual^{[i,j]}
. This implies the stated equivalence.
∎

6.
Induction from
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebras to
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebras

In this section, we construct a model for the induction functor

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}:\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)\longrightarrow\Alg_{n}(\Spaces)

defined as the left adjoint to the functor given by restriction along the standard morphism
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{n}
. We then analyze the value of operadic induction on the spaces of tangles

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[\bullet]\Bigr)

to deduce the
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-monoidal Tangle Hypothesis for
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
from the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal Tangle Hypothesis for
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
.

6.1.
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-Induction

Recall the
(\oo,1)
-category
\Disk_{k}^{\fr}
of framed
k
-dimensional Euclidean spaces: An object is a finite disjoint union of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and a morphism is a framed embedding. See Definitions 2.1, 2.7, and 2.9 from
[
AF1
]
. For each
X\in\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}
and
Y\in\Disk_{1}^{\fr}
, consider the pullback

\textstyle{\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{t}}
\textstyle{\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}\times\pi_{0}}
\textstyle{\Fin\times\Fin\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\times}
\textstyle{\Fin}

where
\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)
is the full subcategory of
\Ar(\Fin)
whose objects are the injections of finite sets.
Note that an object of the pullback consists of a triple

\bigl(U\in\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr},V\in\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr},S\subset\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\bigr)~.

Definition 6.1
.

The
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
is the
\oo
-subcategory of

\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)

consisting of:

•

those objects
(U\in\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr},V\in\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr},S\subset\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V)
such that the composite maps
S\rightarrow\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}U
and
S\rightarrow\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}V
are surjective;

•

those morphisms
f=(U\rightarrow U^{\prime},V\rightarrow V^{\prime},S\rightarrow S^{\prime})
such that for all
\alpha\in\pi_{0}U
, the restriction of
f
to

S\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\xrightarrow{f_{|}}S^{\prime}

is injective.

Lemma 6.2
.

Given embeddings
X\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
and
Y\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}
, the natural functor

\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\longrightarrow\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X^{\prime}}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y^{\prime}}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)

restricts to a functor of
\oo
-subcategories
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}}
. The values
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
thereby define a functor

\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1}^{\fr}\longrightarrow\Cat

assigning
(X,Y)\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
.

Proof.

We observe that the objects and morphisms of Definition
6.1
are preserved under the functor induced by
X\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
and
Y\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}
.

Given an object
(U\in\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr},V\in\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr},S\subset\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V)
of
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
, observe that the induced triple
(U\in\Disk_{\n 1/X^{\prime}}^{\fr},V\in\Disk_{1/Y^{\prime}}^{\fr},S\subset\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V)
retains the property that the composite maps
S\rightarrow\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}U
and
S\rightarrow\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}V
are surjective, since this property is independent of the induced embeddings of
U
and
V
into
X^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}
.

Likewise, given a morphism
(U\rightarrow U^{\prime},V\rightarrow V^{\prime},f:S\rightarrow S^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
, observe that the induced morphism in
\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X^{\prime}}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y^{\prime}}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)
again has the property that for every
\alpha\in\pi_{0}U
the restriction
f_{|}:S\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\rightarrow S^{\prime}
is injective, since again this property is independent of the induced embeddings of
U
and
V
into
X^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}
.
∎

Observe that there exists a functor

\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\longrightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}

sending a triple
(U,V,S)
to the disjoint union

\bigsqcup_{s\in S}U_{\pr_{U}(s)}

where
U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\subset U
is the component of
U
indexed by the image of
s
under the composite
S\rightarrow\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}U
.

For a functor
A:\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\rightarrow\Spaces
and for each
(X,Y)\in\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1}^{\fr}
, consider the composite functor

\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\longrightarrow\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\longrightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\xrightarrow{A}\Spaces

sending an object
(U,V,S)
to the value
A\Bigl(\underset{s\in S}{\coprod}U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\Bigr)
. Note that if
A
is monoidal (i.e.,
A
is an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra), then the value of this composite functor on
(U,V,S)
is

\underset{s\in S}{\prod}A(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})=\prod_{\alpha\in\pi_{0}U}A(U_{\alpha})^{S\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)}~.

Definition 6.3
.

For
A
an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra, the functor

\mathbb{I}(A):\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1}^{\fr}\longrightarrow\Spaces

sends
(X,Y)
to the colimit

\mathbb{I}(A)(X,Y):=\colim\Bigl(\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\longrightarrow\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\longrightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\xrightarrow{A}\Spaces\Bigr)~.

To a morphism
(X,Y)\rightarrow(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{I}(A)
assigns the map
\mathbb{I}(A)(X,Y)\rightarrow\mathbb{I}(A)(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
induced by the functor
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}}
of Lemma
6.2
.

Proposition 6.4
.

For
A
an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra, the functor
\mathbb{I}(A)
obtains the structure of an
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebra via a canonical factorization through the functor
\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1}^{\fr}\rightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{n}
. The assignment
A\mapsto\mathbb{I}(A)
of Definition
6.3
thereby defines a functor

\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}\Alg_{n}(\Spaces)~.

Proof.

First, we show that for each
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra
A
, the functor
\mathbb{I}(A):\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1}^{\fr}\rightarrow\Spaces
is monoidal separately in each variable. For a finite disjoint union of 1-disks
Y=\bigsqcup_{j\in J}Y_{j}\in\Disk^{\fr}_{1}
, we exhibit a canonical equivalence

\mathbb{I}(A)(X,Y)\simeq\prod_{j\in J}\mathbb{I}(A)(X,Y_{j})~.

To do this, note the equivalence of
(\oo,1)
-categories

\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y}\simeq\prod_{j\in J}\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y_{j}}

which restricts to a equivalence

\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\simeq\prod_{j\in J}\mathcal{P}_{X,Y_{j}}~.

This gives a canonical equivalence

\mathbb{I}(A)(X,Y)\simeq\colim\Bigl(\prod_{j\in J}\mathcal{P}_{X,Y_{j}}\xrightarrow{A}\Spaces\Bigr)\simeq\prod_{j\in J}\colim\Bigl(\mathcal{P}_{X,Y_{j}}\xrightarrow{A}\Spaces\Bigr)\simeq\prod_{j\in J}\mathbb{I}(A)(X,Y_{j})~.

This implies that the functor
\mathbb{I}(A)(X,-)
is monoidal for each
X
. The functor
\mathbb{I}(A)(-,Y)
is monoidal for each
Y
, by identical reasoning.
\mathbb{I}(A)
thus defines an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra in
\mathcal{E}_{1}
-algebras. By Dunn–Lurie additivity
[
Lu2
]
, this establishes that
\mathbb{I}(A)
is an
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebra. The assignment
A\mapsto\mathbb{I}(A)
is natural in
A
, which thus gives the stated functor
\mathbb{I}:\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)\rightarrow\Alg_{n}(\Spaces)
.

∎

Observation 6.5
.

For
A
an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra, there exists a natural transformation

\textstyle{\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-,\mathbb{R})}
\scriptstyle{A}
\textstyle{\Spaces}
\textstyle{\Downarrow}
\textstyle{\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1}^{\fr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{I}(A)}
,

given, for each
X\in\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}
, by

A(X)\longrightarrow\mathbb{I}(A)(X,\mathbb{R})~.

By the universal property of induction, we then have the following.

Corollary 6.6
.

There is a canonical natural transformation

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{I}

of functors
\Fun\bigl(\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces),\Alg_{n}(\Spaces)\bigr)
.

Lemma 6.7
.

The functor
\mathbb{I}:\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)\rightarrow\Alg_{n}(\Spaces)
preserves sifted colimits.

Proof.

The assigment
A\mapsto\underset{s\in S}{\prod}A(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})
preserves sifted colimits for each
(U,V,S)
, since the forgetful functor
\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)\rightarrow\Spaces
preserves sifted colimits and products also preserve sifted colimits. The functor
\colim_{\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}}
then commutes with all colimits, and in particular sifted colimits, from which the result follows.
∎

The goal of this section is to show that this natural transformation of Corollary
6.6
is an equivalence.
Now, every
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra admits a free resolution, thereby witnessing it as a sifted colimit of free
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebras.
So, by preservation of sifted colimits from Lemma
6.7
,
it suffices to prove
the natural transformation of Corollary
6.6
is an equivalence when
A=\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)
is a free
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra generated by a space
Z
.
For this, we will use a hypercover argument.
To implement this hypercover argument, we now replace the indexing
(\oo,1)
-category
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
with a suitable poset.
First, for
M
a framed
k
-manifold, denote the full subposet

\mathsf{D}_{k/M}~\subset~\Opens(M)

consisting of those open subsets of
M
that are diffeomorphic with a finite disjoint union of Euclidean spaces.
Proposition 2.19 of
[
AF1
]
states the evident functor

\mathsf{D}_{k/M}\longrightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{k/M}

is a localization.

Definition 6.8
.

For any
(X,Y)\in\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1}
,
P_{X,Y}
is the limit in the following diagram:

\textstyle{P_{X,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm loc}
\textstyle{\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1/X}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y}~.}

Observation 6.9
.

P_{X,Y}
can alternatively be defined as the subcategory of
\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)
satisfying the conditions of Definition
6.1
. Consequently
P_{X,Y}
is a poset.

The finality of the functor
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
will be essential to our hypercover proof of the equivalence of
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{I}
. This finality is a consequence of the following stronger localization property:

Lemma 6.10
.

The functor
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
is a localization.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{I}\subset P_{X,Y}
be the subcategory of isotopy equivalences, which is the inverse image of the isomorphisms in
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
under the functor
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
.
By Theorem 3.8 of
[
MG
]
, it suffices to prove that, for each
[p]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
, the natural functor

(49)

\Fun^{\mathcal{I}}\bigl([p],P_{X,Y}\bigr)\longrightarrow\Map\bigl([p],\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\bigr)

is a localization, where
\Fun^{\mathcal{I}}([p],P_{X,Y})\subset\Fun([p],P_{X,Y})
is the subcategory of natural transformations contained in
\mathcal{I}
as in Notation
4.4
.
Consider the subcategory
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\subset\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}
that is the preimage of the isomorphisms by the functor

\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\to\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1/X}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y}
.
By the proof of Proposition 2.19 of
[
AF1
]
, the functor

\Fun^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\bigl([p],\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\bigr)\longrightarrow\Map\bigl([p],\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1/X}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y}\bigr)

is a localization and in particular initial.
Now, consider the subcategory
\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}\subset\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)
that is the preimage of the isomorphisms by the functor

\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\to\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1/X}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)~

Because monomorphisms in
\Fin
are preserved by base change, the functor

\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\xrightarrow{\ev_{t}}\Fin
is a right fibration, and in particular a Cartesian fibration.
By Corollary 5.15 of
[
AF5
]
, the functor between pullbacks

\Fun^{\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}}\Bigl([p],\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Map\Bigl([p],\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1/X}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\Bigr)

is also initial.
Because the codomain is a space, this functor is a localization.
Observe the relations among subcategories of
\disk_{\n 1/X}\times\disk_{1/Y}\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)

\mathcal{I}~=~P_{X,Y}\cap\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}~\subset~\mathcal{I}^{\prime\prime}

in which the inclusion is full.
This implies the functor (
49
) is a localization.

∎

Notation 6.11
.

Let
Z
be a topological space.
We denote by

\Conf^{Z}(M):=\coprod_{r\geq 0}\Conf_{r}(M)\underset{\Sigma_{r}}{\times}Z^{r}~=~\left\{M\supset T\xrightarrow{\ell}Z\right\}

the topological space of
Z
-labeled configurations of points in a topological space
M
, whose underlying set consists of finite subsets of
M
equipped with a map to
Z
.

In what follows, for
(U,V,S)\in P_{X,Y}
, we denote

(U\times V)_{S}~\subset~U\times V

for the union of those connected components of
U\times V
that belong to
S\subset\pi_{0}(U)\times\pi_{0}(V)
.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
Z
be a topological space.
The assignment

(U,V,S)\longmapsto\Bigl\{X\times Y\supset T\xrightarrow{\ell}Z\Big|\ T\subset(U\times V)_{S}\ {\rm and}\ \forall\beta\in\pi_{0}V,\ T\cap(U\times V_{\beta})\xrightarrow{\pr}U\ {\rm is\ injective}\Bigr\}~,

defines a functor

P_{X,Y}\overset{F}{\longrightarrow}\Opens\Bigl(\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)\Bigr)~.

Furthermore, the resulting map between spaces

\colim\Bigl(P_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{F}\Opens\bigl(\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)\bigr)\rightarrow\Spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since
\Opens\bigl(\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)\bigr)
is a poset, to establish the existence of the asserted functor
F
it suffices to show that given a morphism
f=(U\subset U^{\prime},V\subset V^{\prime},S\rightarrow S^{\prime})
of
P_{X,Y}
,
there is containment
F(U,V,S)\subset F(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})
between subsets of
\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)
.

To show this, assume that we are given
X\times Y\supset T\xrightarrow{\ell}Z
belonging to
F(U,V,S)
, and we are given containments
U\subset U^{\prime}
,
V\subset V^{\prime}
compatibly with
f:S\rightarrow S^{\prime}
such that for each
\alpha\in\pi_{0}U
, the map
S\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\rightarrow S^{\prime}
is injective. The containment
(U\times V)_{S}\subset(U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime})_{S^{\prime}}
immediately implies the first condition, that
T
is contained in
(U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime})_{S^{\prime}}
. We now show that for each element
\beta^{\prime}\in\pi_{0}V^{\prime}
, the subset
T\cap(U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}_{\beta^{\prime}})
maps injectively to
U^{\prime}
under the projection map
\pr:U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}\rightarrow U^{\prime}
. Suppose that there exists elements
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
of
T
which map to to same element of
U
under the composite projection
T\rightarrow U\times V\rightarrow U
, and denote by
\alpha
the component of
U
that contains this element. Since
T
is assumed to belong to
F(U,V,S)
, then necessarily
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
belong to different components
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
of
V
, under the composite projection
T\rightarrow U\times V\rightarrow V
. Now, the map

S\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\rightarrow S^{\prime}

was assumed to be injective, and therefore the map
\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}V^{\prime}
is injective when restricted to the subset
\pi_{0}(V_{1}\amalg V_{2})
. Consequently the images of
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
belong to different components of
V^{\prime}
under the projection
T\rightarrow U\times V\rightarrow V
. This implies that
T
belongs to
F(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})
, and thereby establishes the existence of the functor
F
.

We now show that
F
is a hypercovering of the space
\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)
. For
(T,\ell)\in\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)
, denote by

\bigl(P_{X,Y}\bigr)_{(T,\ell)}

the subposet of
P_{X,Y}
consisting of those
(U,V,S)
for which the topological space
F(U,V,S)
contains the point
(T,\ell)
.
By the Siefert–van Kampen Theorem of
[
Lu2
]
(specifically Theorem A.3.1), to check that the colimit of
F
is
\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)
, it suffices to show the following hypercovering criterion:

•

For every
(T,\ell)\in\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)
, the classifying space of the poset
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
is contractible:

\mathsf{B}\Bigl(\bigr(P_{X,Y}\bigr)_{(T,\ell)}\Bigr)\simeq\ast~.

We prove this in two steps. First, we prove that
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
is nonempty for all
(T,\ell)\in\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)
. That is, we construct
(U,V,S)\in P_{X,Y}
such that:
T
is contained in
(U\times V)_{S}
; and for each
\beta\in\pi_{0}V
, the projection
T\cap(U\times V_{\beta})\rightarrow U
is injective. Let
\pr_{X}(T)
and
\pr_{Y}(T)
denote the images of
T
in
X
and
Y
, respectively. For each
x\in\pr_{X}(T)
and
y\in\pr_{Y}(T)
, choose connected neighborhoods
U_{x}
and
U_{y}
, respectively, such that
U_{x}
is disjoint from
U_{x^{\prime}}
for distinct element
x,x^{\prime}\in\pr_{X}(T)
and
V_{y}
is disjoint from
V_{y^{\prime}}
for distinct element
y,y^{\prime}\in\pr_{Y}(T)
. We can then set

U:=\coprod_{x\in\pr_{X}(T)}U_{x}~,\ V:=\coprod_{y\in\pr_{Y}(T)}V_{y}~,\ {\rm and}\ S:=T~.

Second, we show that for two objects
(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})
and
(U^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime\prime},S^{\prime\prime})
of
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
, there exists an object
(U,V,T)
of
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
with morphisms

(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})\leftarrow(U,V,T)\rightarrow(U^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime\prime},S^{\prime\prime})

in
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
. Since
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
is a poset, these assertions imply that
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
is cofiltered and thus that its classifying space is contractible.

Set
U=\coprod_{x\in\pr_{X}(T)}U_{x}
,
V=\coprod_{y\in\pr_{Y}(T)}V_{y}
, and
S=T
as in the first step, and additionally shrink each
U_{x}
and
V_{y}
so as to satisfy the containments
U\subset U^{\prime}\cap U^{\prime\prime}
and
V\subset V^{\prime}\cap V^{\prime\prime}
.

Note that by construction,
(U,V,T)
defines an object of
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
. Further, there are containments
U\subset U^{\prime}
and
V\subset V^{\prime}
. The containment
T\subset(U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime})_{S^{\prime}}
defines a map
T\rightarrow S^{\prime}
lying over the map
\pi_{0}U\times\pi_{0}V\rightarrow\pi_{0}U^{\prime}\times\pi_{0}V^{\prime}
. To establish the existence of a morphism from
(U,V,T)
to
(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})
, we must lastly check the condition that for each
\alpha\in\pi_{0}U
the restriction

T\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\rightarrow S^{\prime}

is injective. By construction, the intersection
T\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)
consists of elements
\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{k}\}
whose image in
X
are the same:
\pr_{X}(w_{i})=\pr_{X}(w_{j})
. Since for every
\beta^{\prime}\in\pi_{0}V^{\prime}
, the projection
T\cap(U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}_{\beta^{\prime}})\rightarrow U^{\prime}
is injective, consequently at most one of the
w_{i}\in T\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)
belong to
T\cap(U^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}_{\beta^{\prime}})
for any given connected component
V^{\prime}_{\beta^{\prime}}\subset V^{\prime}
. Therefore the map
T\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\rightarrow\pi_{0}U^{\prime}\times\pi_{0}V^{\prime}
is injective. As this map lifts to
S^{\prime}
, therefore the map
T\cap(\{\alpha\}\times\pi_{0}V)\rightarrow S^{\prime}
is injective, as required.
(U,V,T)
consequently maps to
(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})
in
(P_{X,Y})_{(T,\ell)}
and, by symmetry,
(U,V,T)
also maps to
(U^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime\prime},S^{\prime\prime})
.

∎

Lemma 6.13
.

There exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{P_{X,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\Opens\Bigl(\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)}
\textstyle{\spaces}

where the functor
F:P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Opens(\Conf^{Z}(X\times Y))
is that defined in Proposition
6.12
, and the composite functor
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Spaces
is that whose colimit defines
\mathbb{I}(\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z))(X,Y)
according to Definition
6.3
, .

Proof.

The definitions of the two composite functors of
(\oo,1)
-categories
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Spaces
both come as functors of ordinary categories
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Top
, where
\Top
is the ordinary category of topological spaces.
To construct a natural equivalence making the above diagram commute, it therefore suffices to construct a natural transformation of functors
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Top
whose value on every object of
P_{X,Y}
is a homotopy equivalences of topological spaces.
First note that the composite functor

{\rm forget}\circ F:P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Spaces

sends isotopy equivalence to homotopy equivalences, and so factors through the localization
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
.
It thereby suffices to show the natural equivalence

F(U,V,S)\simeq\underset{s\in S}{\prod}\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})

with the values of the functor
\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z):\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\rightarrow\Spaces
given by the free
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra generated by the space
Z
.

To show this, it further reduces to first showing the special case for
S=\{s\}
a singleton, of

F(U,V,\{s\})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})

and the second equivalence

F(U,V,S)\simeq\underset{s\in S}{\prod}F(U_{\pr_{U}(s)},V,\{s\})~.

To see the first equivalence, recall from the definition of
F
that

F(U,V,\{s\})=F(U_{\pr_{U}(s)},V_{\pr_{V}(s)},\{s\})

=\Bigl\{X\times Y\supset T\xrightarrow{\ell}Z\Big|\ T\subset U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\times V_{\pr_{V}(s)}\ {\rm and}\ T\xrightarrow{\pr}U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\ {\rm is\ injective}\Bigr\}~.

Since
V_{\pr_{V}(s)}\cong\mathbb{R}^{1}
is contractible, the space of
T
subsets of
U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\times V_{\pr_{V}(s)}
whose projection to
U
is injective is homotopy equivalent to the space of
T
subset of
U_{\pr_{U}(s)}
. Thus:

\Bigl\{X\times Y\supset T\xrightarrow{\ell}Z\Big|\ T\subset U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\times V_{\pr_{V}(s)}\ {\rm and}\ T\xrightarrow{\pr}U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\ {\rm is\ injective}\Bigr\}\simeq\Bigl\{U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\supset T\xrightarrow{\ell}Z\Bigr\}~.

This righthand space is homotopy equivalent to the value
\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})
, which establishes the first equivalence.

The second equivalence is immediate from the definition of
F
, that it splits as a product over the set
S
. That is, the projection
T\cap U\times V_{\beta}\rightarrow U
is injective for every
\beta\in V_{\beta}
if and only if it is true for every component of
U
, that the projection
T\cap U_{\alpha}\times V_{\beta}\rightarrow U_{\alpha}
is injective.
Since
T
is contained in the components of
U\times V
indexed by the elements of
S
,
this reformulation is true if and only if it is true for each element of
S
, that the projection
T\cap U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\times V_{\pr_{V}(s)}\rightarrow U_{\pr_{U}(s)}
is injective for each
s\in S
.
∎

We obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 6.14
.

There natural equivalences:

\mathbb{F}_{n}(Z)(X\times Y)\simeq\colim\Bigl(P_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{F}\spaces\Bigr)\simeq\colim\Bigl(\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)}\spaces\Bigr)=:\mathbb{I}(\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z))(X,Y)~.

Proof.

The equivalence
\mathbb{F}_{n}(Z)(X\times Y)\simeq\colim(P_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{F}\spaces)
follows from the hypercover condition established in Proposition
6.12
. The natural map

\colim\Bigl(P_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{F}\spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\colim\Bigl(\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)}\spaces\Bigr)

is an equivalence by the finality of
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
and the identification

F(U,V,S)\simeq\underset{s\in S}{\prod}\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})

of Lemma
6.13
.
∎

We now assemble the preceding results to establish our main technical result of this section.

Lemma 6.15
.

The natural transformation
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{I}
is an equivalence. In particular, for any
A\in\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)
and any
D\in\Disk^{\fr}_{n}
identified as
D\cong X\times Y
for
(X,Y)\in\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\times\Disk^{\fr}_{1}
, there are equivalences

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}(A)(D)\simeq\colim\Bigl(\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\longrightarrow\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\longrightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\xrightarrow{A}\Spaces\Bigr)

\simeq\colim\Bigl(P_{X,Y}\longrightarrow\bigl(\disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\longrightarrow\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\xrightarrow{A}\Spaces\Bigr)

for the value of the functor
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}(A):\Disk^{\fr}_{n}\rightarrow\Spaces
on the
n
-disk
D\cong X\times Y
.

Proof.

By Corollary
6.14
the natural map is an equivalence for a free
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra
A=\mathbb{F}_{\n 1}(Z)
. The functor
\Ind^{n}_{\n 1}
preserves sifted colimits, being a left adjoint. The functor
\mathbb{I}
preserves sifted colimits, by Lemma
6.7
. Since any
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra is a sifted colimit of free algebras, the equivalence follows for general
A
.
∎

7.
Operadic induction and projection-immersion tangles

In this section, we prove that the induction functor
\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\rightarrow\Alg_{n}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})
sends
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
to
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
:

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}\Bigr)\simeq\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}~.

We outline our argument:

(1)

Induction of spaces of
p
-simplices gives slice-wise projection-embeddings: Corollary
7.6
.

(2)

Segaling of slice-wise projection-embeddings gives projection-immersions: Lemma
7.11
.

(3)

Univalent-completion of projection-immersions gives that of the usual tangle category: Lemma
7.15
.

By universal properties, the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\scriptstyle{\Ind^{n}_{\n 1}}
\textstyle{\Alg_{n}(\Cat_{(\oo,1)})}
\textstyle{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Ind^{n}_{\n 1}}
\scriptstyle{({\rm See~Cor}~\ref{cor1})}
\textstyle{\Alg_{n}(\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Segaling}
\scriptstyle{({\rm See~Lem}~\ref{lemma.proj.emb.to.proj.imm})}
\textstyle{\Alg_{n}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\wedge}_{\sf unv}}
\scriptstyle{({\rm See~Lem}~\ref{lemma.univ.cplt.of.proj.imm})}

7.1.
Slice-wise projection-embedding tangles

Fix
X\in\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}
and
Y\in\Disk_{1}^{\fr}
with resulting product
X\times Y\in\Disk_{n}^{\fr}
. In the particular case of interest
X=\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
and
Y=\mathbb{R}^{1}
, we further fix the usual identification
\mathbb{R}^{n}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{\{n\}}
.

Definition 7.1
.

For
W
a 1-manifold with boundary,
p\geq 0
, an embedding
W\hookrightarrow X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is a
slice-wise projection-embedding
if:

•

the composite projection
W\rightarrow X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\rightarrow\mathbb{D}[p]
is transverse to
[p]\subset\mathbb{D}[p]
;

•

for each connected component
Y_{\alpha}\subset Y
,
there exists a finite subset
S\subset Y_{\alpha}
for which:

–

X\times S\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is disjoint from
W
; and

–

for each pair of successive elements
s
and
s^{\prime}
of
S\cup\{-\oo,\oo\}
, the projection

W\cap\Bigl(X\times[s,s^{\prime}]\times\mathbb{D}[p]\Bigr)\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{D}[p]

is an embedding.

The topological space

\Emb(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p])^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}~\subset~\Emb(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p])

is the topological subspace consisting of slice-wise projection-embedding.

Observe that the slice-wise projection-embedding condition implies that the composite projection
W\rightarrow X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is an immersion.
Therefore, projection onto
X\times\mathbb{D}[p]
defines a continuous map

\Emb(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p])^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\longrightarrow\Imm(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p])

to the topological space of immersions.
This allows for the following notion of a framing on a tangle that satisfies this slice-wise embedding condition.

Definition 7.2
.

A
framing
of a slice-wise projection-embedding
W\hookrightarrow X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]
consists of a nullhomotopy
\varphi
of the Gauss map
{\sf Gauss}_{(W\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{D}[p])}:W\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
associated to the composite immersion

W~\hookrightarrow~X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\xrightarrow{~\pr~}X\times\mathbb{D}[p]

and the framing of
X\times\mathbb{D}[p]
.

We topologize the set of all slice-wise projection-embeddings in the following way.

Definition 7.3
.

The topological space of framed slice-wise projection-embedding tangles is

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}^{\sf s.pr.emb}:=\coprod_{[W]}\Bigl(\Emb\bigl(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\underset{\Imm\bigl(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)}{\times}\Imm^{\fr}\bigl(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\Bigr)_{\Aut(W)}

where
\Imm^{\fr}(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p])
is the space of immersions equipped with a trivialization of the Gauss map in the sense of Definition
1.13
.

The following lemma shows how the homotopy type of the space
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}^{\sf s.pr.emb}
arises from induction.

Lemma 7.4
.

There exists a functor

P_{X,Y}\overset{F}{\longrightarrow}\Opens\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\Bigr)

whose value on an object
(U,V,S)
is the subspace of tangles
W\subset X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]
for which:

(1)

W\subset(U\times V)_{S}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
; and

(2)

for each connected component
V_{\beta}\subset V
, the projection

W\cap(U\times V_{\beta}\times\mathbb{D}[p])\rightarrow U\times\mathbb{D}[p]

is an embedding.

Furthermore, the resulting map between spaces

\colim\Bigl(P_{X,Y}\xrightarrow{F}\Opens\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\bigr)\rightarrow\Spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
6.12
, since
\Opens\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\bigr)
is a poset, to establish the existence of the asserted functor
F
it suffices to show that given a morphism
f=(U\subset U^{\prime},V\subset V^{\prime},S\rightarrow S^{\prime})
of
P_{X,Y}
, the topological space
F(U,V,S)
is contained in the topological space
F(U^{\prime},V^{\prime},S^{\prime})
, as subspaces of
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}
.
The argument here is now similar to that given in Proposition
6.12
.

We now show that
F
is a hypercovering of the space
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}
. For
(W,\varphi)\in\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}
, denote by

\bigl(P_{X,Y}\bigr)_{(W,\varphi)}

the subposet of
P_{X,Y}
consisting of those
(U,V,S)
for which the topological space
F(U,V,S)
contains the point
(W,\varphi)
.
By the Siefert–van Kampen Theorem of
[
Lu2
]
(specifically Theorem A.3.1), to check that the colimit of
F
is
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}
, it suffices to show the following hypercovering criterion:

•

For every
(W,\varphi)\in\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}
, the classifying space of the poset
(P_{X,Y})_{(W,\varphi)}
is contractible:

\mathsf{B}\Bigl(\bigr(P_{X,Y}\bigr)_{(W,\varphi)}\Bigr)\simeq\ast~.

The argument here is again similar to that given in Proposition
6.12
.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

There exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{P_{X,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\Opens\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm forget}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigl(\Disk_{\n 1/X}^{\fr}\times\Disk_{1/Y}^{\fr}\bigr)\underset{\Fin}{\times}\Ar^{\sf mono}(\Fin)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]}
\textstyle{\spaces}

where the functor
F:P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Opens(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]})
is that defined in Lemma
7.4
, and the composite functor
\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Spaces
is that whose colimit is

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]\Bigr)(X\times Y)

according to Definition
6.3
and Lemma
6.15
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
6.13
, the composite functor

{\rm forget}\circ F:P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\Spaces

sends isotopy equivalence to homotopy equivalences, and so factors through the localization
P_{X,Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X,Y}
. It thereby suffices to show the natural equivalence

F(U,V,S)\simeq\underset{s\in S}{\prod}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})[p]

with the values of the functor
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]:\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}\rightarrow\Spaces
. Recall that the value of this functor on
U\in\Disk^{\fr}_{\n 1}
is

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p](U):=\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(U)[p]\simeq\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(U\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}~.

Again as in the proof of Lemma
6.13
, we first show the equivalence for
S=\{s\}
a singleton, of

F(U,V,\{s\})\simeq\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(U_{\pr_{U}(s)})[p]

and the second equivalence

F(U,V,S)\simeq\underset{s\in S}{\prod}F(U_{\pr_{U}(s)},V,\{s\})~.

To see the first equivalence, note from the definition of
F
that

F(U,V,\{s\})=F(U_{\pr_{U}(s)},V_{\pr_{V}(s)},\{s\})~.

Given the isomorphisms
U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
and
V_{\pr_{V}(s)}\cong\mathbb{R}^{1}
, it thus suffices to show the equivalence

F(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{1},\{s\})\simeq\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}~.

The value
F(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{1},\{s\})
can be expressed in terms of the topological subspace which we will briefly denote

\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf t.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\subset\Emb(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}

of total (and
not
slice-wise) projection-embeddings: This consists of those embeddings
W\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
for which the composite
W\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is again an embedding. Direct from the definition of
F
and Definition
7.3
, the value
F(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{1},\{s\})
is equivalent to

\coprod_{[W]}\Bigl(\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf t.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\underset{\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)}{\times}\Emb^{\fr}\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\Bigr)_{\Aut(W)}~.

We can now observe the existence of the fiber square among topological spaces,

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 45.47995pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.70146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Map(W,\mathbb{R}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.47995pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.96786pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.47995pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf t.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 140.60506pt\raise-24.1079pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-45.47995pt\raise-33.6079pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl\{W\overset{f}{\hookrightarrow}\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.70497pt\raise-33.6079pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 84.70497pt\raise-33.6079pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)_{\pitchfork[p]}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces~.

Observe that the right vertical map is a fibration, and therefore this square is a homotopy pullback.
Since
\Map(W,\mathbb{R}^{1})
is contractible, the the right vertical map is a weak homotopy equivalence. From this, obtain the further equivalences

\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf t.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\underset{\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)}{\times}\Emb^{\fr}\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\simeq\Emb^{\fr}\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)_{\pitchfork[p]}

and thus

F(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{1},\{s\})\simeq\Tang^{\fr}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}

which proves the first equivalence.

The second equivalence is immediate from the definition of
F
, that it splits as a product over the set
S
. That is, the projection
W\cap U\times V_{\beta}\rightarrow U
is an embedding for every
\beta\in V_{\beta}
if and only if is true for every component
U_{\alpha}\subset U
, that the projection
W\cap U_{\alpha}\times V_{\beta}\rightarrow U_{\alpha}
is an embedding. Since
W\subset U\times V
is contained in those connected components that are indexed by elements in the subset
S
, this reformulation is true if and only if it is true for each element of
S
, that the projection
W\cap U_{\pr_{U}(s)}\times V_{\pr_{V}(s)}\rightarrow U_{\pr_{U}(s)}
is an embedding for each
s\in S
.
∎

Combined with the results of the previous section, Lemma
7.4
and Lemma
7.5
have the following consequence.

Corollary 7.6
.

For each
[p]
, there is a natural equivalence

\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]\Bigr)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Tang_{1}^{\fr,\pitchfork[p]}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])^{\sf s.pr.emb}

where
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]
is the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra in spaces given by the space of
p
-simplices of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
, and
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}
is the induction functor
\Alg_{\n 1}(\spaces)\rightarrow\Alg_{n}(\spaces)
.

∎

Definition 7.7
.

The
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebra in simplicial spaces

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet])^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}\in\Alg_{n}(\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces))

is the
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-induction
\Ind_{\n 1}^{n}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\Bigr)
.
By Corollary
7.6
, its space of
p
-simplices are the slice-wise projection-embedding tangles in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
.

7.2.
Projection-immersion tangles

Definition 7.8
.

Let
W
be a 1-manifold with boundary; let
X\in\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}
and
Y\in\Disk_{1}^{\fr}
. The topological space of
projection-immersion embeddings
into
X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is the topological subspace

\Emb\bigl(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}\subset\Emb\bigl(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)_{\pitchfork[p]}

of boundary-preserving embeddings
W\rightarrow X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]
transverse to
[p]\subset\mathbb{D}[p]
such that the composite projection

W\rightarrow X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{D}[p]

is an immersion.

Compare the following definition with Definition
7.3
.

Definition 7.9
.

Let
X\in\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}
and
Y\in\Disk_{1}^{\fr}
.
The topological space of framed projection-immersion tangles is

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}^{\sf pr.imm}:=\coprod_{[W]}\Bigl(\Emb\bigl(W,X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}\underset{\Imm\bigl(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)}{\times}\Imm^{\fr}\bigl(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)\Bigr)_{\Aut(W)}

where
\Imm^{\fr}(W,X\times\mathbb{D}[p])
is the space of immersions equipped with a trivialization of the Gauss map in the sense of Definition
1.13
.

Observation 7.10
.

Let
X\in\Disk_{\n 1}^{\fr}
and
Y\in\Disk_{1}^{\fr}
.
The spaces of framed projection-immersion tangles of Definition
7.9
organize as a simplicial space that satisfies the Segal condition:

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(X\times Y\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet])_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}^{\sf pr.imm}~\in~\fCat_{(\infty,1)}~\subset~\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces)~.

Lemma 7.11
.

The canonical map

\Seg\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}\Bigr)[p]\longrightarrow\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[p]}^{\sf pr.imm}

is an equivalence,
and moreover identifies

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}^{\sf pr.imm}

as the Segal-completion of

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}
.
Here
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}
is the
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-algebra in
\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces)
whose space of
p
-simplices is the space
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[p]}
as in Definition
7.7
;
\Seg
is as in Definition
3.25
.

Proof.

This proof relies on Lemmas
7.12
and
7.13
, which are stated and proved below.

Consider the functor
F
defined as the composite in the following diagram:

\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})_{/[p]^{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\spaces}
\textstyle{\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Morita[\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}[\Spaces]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}}
\textstyle{\Morita[\Spaces]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\sf LIM}

To prove the claimed equivalence, we show that the colimit of the composite functor
F
gives the
p
-simplices
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}
. That is, by construction of the functor
\Seg
, it will imply the claimed equivalence if the map

\colim\Bigl(\TwAro(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})_{/[p]^{\circ}}\xrightarrow{F}\Spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}

is an equivalence:
By Observation
7.10
, this result implies that
\Seg\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}\bigr)
satisfies the Segal condition, and then by Lemma
3.32
we conclude that
\Seg\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}\bigr)
is the Segal-completion of the simplicial space
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}
.
We will compute this colimit after restricting along a final functor.
Consider a closed interval
I
, and fix an object
P\in\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]}
with
\pi_{0}(\mathbb{D}[p]\smallsetminus P)=[p]
. Let

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}~\subset~\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}

denote the topological subspace of framed tangles
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
such that the critical values of the projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\rightarrow\mathbb{D}[p]
are contained in
P
.
Note the immediate consequence of isotopy extension that

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}~.

From Lemma
4.6
, the colimit of the functor
F
can be computed after restriction along

\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}\rightarrow\TwAr(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op})^{\op}_{/[p]^{\circ}}~.

We are thereby reduced to proving the map

\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{F}\Spaces\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}

is an equivalence,
where
F
is the restriction of
F
to
\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}
.

We construct a functor

\mathfrak{Z}\colon\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}\longrightarrow\Opens\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}\Bigr)

assigning to an object
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
an open subspace
\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}
of
\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}
of those
W
such that:

•
