The critical values of the composite projection
W\rightarrow\mathbb{D}[p]
are contained in
A
. That is,
W
is contained in the subspace

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{A}\subset\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}~.

•

For each pair of consecutive components of
[0,1]\smallsetminus B
, there exists elements
t,t^{\prime}
of these components
such that the intersection

W\cap\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t,t^{\prime}]\bigr)

is a slice-wise projection-embedding tangle in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t,t^{\prime}]
.

We now prove:

(1)

Lemma
7.12
: For any
W\in\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}
, the subcategory

\Bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\Bigr)_{W}\subset\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}

of
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
for which there is containment
W\in\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}
has contractible classifying space. Consequently, applying the higher Seifert–van Kampen theorem of
[
Lu2
]
(specifically Theorem A.3.1), there is a natural homotopy equivalence

\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}\simeq\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{Z}}\Opens\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\rm forget}\Spaces\Bigr)

(2)

Lemma
7.13
: There is a natural equivalence

\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq F(P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B)~.

The above results then imply the equivalence

\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{F}\Spaces\Bigr)\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}

which completes the proof.
∎

The following lemmas were used in the preceding proof, of Lemma
7.11
.

Lemma 7.12
.

The canonical map between spaces

\colim\Bigl(\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{\mathbb{D}[p]})^{\op}_{/P}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{Z}}\Opens\bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\rm forget}\Spaces\Bigr)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The statement is a direct application of the higher Seifert–van Kampen theorem of
[
Lu2
]
(specifically Theorem A.3.1).
For this theorem to apply, we must verify the following.

For any
W\in\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}_{P}
, the full subcategory

\Bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}\Bigr)_{W}\subset\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,1]})_{/P}~,

consisting of those
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
for which there is containment
W\in\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}
, has contractible classifying space.

Fix a linear identification
\mathbb{D}[p]=[0,p]
.
First, we prove that for any such
W
, the category
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
is nonempty. To do so, it suffices to show that there exists
B\in\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]}
such that:

•

B
contains the critical values of the projection
W\rightarrow[0,p]
.

•

For any two points
t
and
t^{\prime}
in consecutive components of
[0,p]\smallsetminus B
and such that
[t,t^{\prime}]
contains
B_{0}
, there exists a finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\{n\}}
for which:

–

\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times S\times[t,t^{\prime}]
is disjoint from
W
; and

–

for every pair of successive elements
s
and
s^{\prime}
of
S\cup\{-\oo,\oo\}
, the projection

W\cap\Bigl(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times[s,s^{\prime}]\times[t,t^{\prime}]\Bigr)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times[t,t^{\prime}]

is an embedding.

Since the projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\to\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]
is assumed to be an immersion,
compactness of
W
ensures,
for each element
x\in[0,p]
, there exists a closed neighborhood
D^{\prime}_{x}
such that the immersion

W\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times D^{\prime}_{x})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times D^{\prime}_{x}

is an embedding when restricted to each connected component of
W\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times D^{\prime}_{x})
. Likewise, for each element
x\in[0,p]
, there exists a closed neighborhood
D^{\prime\prime}_{x}
such that the connected components of
W\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times D^{\prime\prime}_{x})
are split in the
n
th coordinate: That is, there exists
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\{n\}}
such that
W
is disjoint from
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times S\times D^{\prime\prime}_{x}
and for each pair of successive elements
s
and
s^{\prime}
of
S
, the intersection of
W
with
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times[s,s^{\prime}]\times D^{\prime\prime}_{x}
is either empty or connected. We now set
D_{x}:=D^{\prime}_{x}\cap D^{\prime\prime}_{x}
.

By compactness of
[0,p]
, there exists a minimal finite set
T
of
x\in[0,p]
for which the interiors
D_{x}^{\circ}
cover the interval
(0,p)
. For each pair of consecutive elements
x,x^{\prime}
of
T
, choose an element
y\in D_{x}\cap D_{x^{\prime}}
which is also a regular value of the composite projection
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,p]\rightarrow[0,p]
, and set
Q
to be this finite set of elements
y
. Define
B
to be the complement,
B:=[0,p]\smallsetminus Q
. By construction
B
satisfies the bulleted conditions, which implies that
(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P})_{W}
is nonempty.

Second, we now prove
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
has contractible classifying space. We use the following essential property: For any pair of objects
P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B
and
P\leftarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow B^{\prime}
of
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
, observe that both
P\leftarrow A\cap A^{\prime}\rightarrow B
and
P\rightarrow A\cap A^{\prime}\rightarrow B\cap B^{\prime}
form objects of

\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
. The verification that
\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
has contractible classifying space is now identical to the proof in Lemma
5.21
, by checking the condition that for every functor
G:\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}
from a poset
\mathcal{K}
, there exists a factorization

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\bigl(\TwAro(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})_{/P}\bigr)_{W}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{G}}
.

∎

Lemma 7.13
.

There is a natural equivalence

\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq F(P\leftarrow A\rightarrow B)~.

Proof.

First, note that for
(A\rightarrow B)\in\TwAr(\mathsf{D}_{[0,p]})
an object with
\pi B=\{0<a\}
and
\pi A=[a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\act}\{0<a\}^{\circ}=\pi B
the active morphism, the space
\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}
is homotopy equivalent to

\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,a]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork\{0,a\}}=F([0,a])

by the condition that for any
W\in\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}
there exists
t,t^{\prime}
in
[0,p]\smallsetminus B
for which the intersection

W\cap\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[t,t^{\prime}]\bigr)

is a slice-wise projection-embedding tangle.

For general
A\rightarrow B
we decompose
\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}
as a fiber product

\mathfrak{Z}_{A_{1}\rightarrow B_{1}}\xrightarrow{~\cong~}\underset{\mathfrak{Z}_{\emptyset\to C_{1}}}{\times}\ldots\underset{\mathfrak{Z}_{\emptyset\to C_{b}}}{\times}\mathfrak{Z}_{A_{b}\rightarrow B_{b}}

as in the proof of Lemma
5.22
.
Again, the restriction maps in the fiber product are fibrations by the parametrized isotopy extension theorem.
Therefore, this iterated pullback among topological spaces is an iterated homotopy pullback.

By Observation
3.26
, the value
F([a]^{\circ}\xrightarrow{g^{\circ}}[b]^{\circ})
is given by the iterated fiber product:

F([0,g(0)])\underset{F(g(0))}{\times}F([g(0),g(1)])\underset{F(g(1))}{\times}\ldots\underset{F(g(b))}{\times}F([g(b),a])

where

F([k,l])=\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[k,l]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[k,l]}

and

F(\{k\})=\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\{k\}\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}~.

The general equivalence
\mathfrak{Z}_{A\rightarrow B}\simeq F(A\rightarrow B)
follows.
∎

Definition 7.14
.

The
\mathcal{E}_{n}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}\in\Alg_{n}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})

is defined to be
\Seg\Bigl(\Tang_{1}^{\fr}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[\bullet]\bigr)^{\sf s.pr.emb}_{\pitchfork[\bullet]}\Bigr)
, the Segal-completion of the slice-wise projection-embedding tangle spaces.
By Lemma
7.11
, its space of
p
-composable morphisms is the space

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}[p]\simeq\Tang_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}[p])^{\sf pr.imm}_{\pitchfork[p]}

from Definition
7.9
.

7.3.
Univalent-completion of projection-immersion tangles

We now prove that the projection-immersion tangle category is equivalent to the usual tangle category after univalent-completion.

Lemma 7.15
.

The functor in
\Alg_{n}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

is an equivalence after univalent-completion.

Proof.

This proof relies on Corollary
8.20
, which is stated and proved in §
8
.
By the model for univalent-completion given in Proposition 2.4 of
[
AF3
]
, it suffices to show that the map on spaces of objects is
\pi_{0}
-surjective, and all maps on hom-spaces are equivalences.

Recall that the data defining each object
W\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}
consists of: a finite set
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, a trivialization of the Gauss map
W_{i}\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n)
associated to the Gauss map of the composite immersion
W_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
and the standard solid
n
-framing of
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
.
So
\Obj\left(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}\right)\simeq\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}(\Omega\Gr_{1}(n))
.
Meanwhile, an object
W\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
consists of: a finite set
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, a trivialization of the Gauss map
W_{i}\rightarrow\Gr_{1}(n+1)
associated to the Gauss map of the composite immersion
W_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the standard solid
(n+1)
-framing of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
So
\Obj\left(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)\simeq\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}(\Omega\Gr_{1}(n+1))
.
The condition of
\pi_{0}
-surjectivity on spaces of objects then follows from surjectivity of the homomorphism
\pi_{1}(\Gr_{1}(n))\to\pi_{1}(\Gr_{1}(n+1))
for
n\geq 2
.

We now show that all maps on hom-space are equivalences.
Let
W_{0},W_{1}\in\obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}
.
Denote their images
W_{0}^{\prime},W_{1}^{\prime}\in\obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
We must show the map

\Map_{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\sf pr.imm}}(W_{0},W_{1})\rightarrow\Map_{\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}(W_{0}^{\prime},W_{1}^{\prime})

is an equivalence.

From the topological models of these tangle spaces (see Definition
7.14
and Lemma
2.22
) this equivalence will follow from showing that, for every
W
a compact 1-manifold with boundary, the horizontal maps

\textstyle{\EmbImm^{\fr}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb^{\fr}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\EmbImm^{\fr}(\partial W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb^{\fr}(\partial W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})}

induce a weak homotopy equivalence on vertical homotopy fibers. Here, we use the notation for projection-immersions of Definition
8.12
: Note that the identification
\EmbImm(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})=\Emb\bigl(W,\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\bigr)^{\sf pr.imm}
is by definition.

By the h-principle of Corollary
8.20
, we can replace the spaces of framed embedding-immersions with those of framed embedding-formal-immersions. We thus are reduced to showing that the diagram

\textstyle{\EmbfImm^{\fr}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb^{\fr}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\EmbfImm^{\fr}(\partial W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb^{\fr}(\partial W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})}

induces a weak homotopy equivalence on vertical homotopy fibers.
Let
g_{0}\in\EmbfImm^{\fr}(\partial W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{1})
.
Denote the map between vertical homotopy fibers over
g_{0}
as

(50)

{\sf Fib}_{1}\longrightarrow{\sf Fib}_{2}~.

Forgetting framing defines maps to the space of embeddings of
W
into
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
, relative to the embedding
g_{0}
of its boundary:

(51)

\textstyle{{\sf Fib}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e.fibs})}
\textstyle{{\sf Fib}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb_{g_{0}}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})}
.

The map (
50
) is an equivalence if the map between fibers of (
51
) is an equivalence.
So choose an embedding
g:W\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}
extending the embedding
g_{0}
of the boundary.
By definition of framed embeddings, the fiber of
{\sf Fib}_{2}\to\Emb_{g_{0}}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})
over
g
is the space of lifts of the Gauss map:

\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf Gauss}(g)}
\textstyle{\Gr_{1}(n+1)}
\textstyle{\partial W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

By definition of framed embedding-formal-immersions, the fiber of
{\sf Fib}_{1}\to\Emb_{g_{0}}(W,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})
over
g
is the space of pairs of lifts:

\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\sf Gauss}(g)}
\textstyle{\Gr_{1}(n+1)}
\textstyle{\Gr_{1}(n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

Since a trivialization of the Gauss map to
\Gr_{1}(n+1)
is equivalent to first lifting the Gauss map to
\Gr_{1}(n)
and then trivializing this lift, therefore these spaces of factorizations are homotopy equivalent.

∎

We now prove the main theorem of this paper.

Theorem 7.16

(Tangle Hypothesis)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a rigid
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, with
n\geq 2
. Let
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
be the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\infty,1)
-category of framed tangles of Definition
2.20
.
Evaluation at the object
\ast\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
defines an equivalence between spaces

\ev_{\ast}\colon\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Obj(\mathcal{R})~,\qquad Z\longmapsto Z(\ast)~.

between the space of objects of
\mathcal{R}
and the space of
\mathcal{R}
-valued
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal functors from
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
.

Proof.

We prove the result by induction on
n
.
First, note that the object
\ast\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is in the image of the composite functor

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n\text{-}2})\to\Ind_{{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}}^{\n 1}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}})\bigr)\to\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\sf pr.imm}\to\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})~,

in which the first is
\mathcal{E}_{n\text{-}2}
-monoidal, and the others are
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal.
The base case of
n=2
is Theorem
5.24
.
We now have the following chain of equivalence:

\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)\simeq\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})^{\sf pr.imm},\mathcal{R}\bigr)

\simeq\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\Bigl(\Ind_{{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}}^{\n 1}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}})\bigr),\mathcal{R}\Bigr)\simeq\Map_{\Alg_{{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{{\mathit{n}\text{-}2}}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)\underset{\ev_{\ast}}{\xrightarrow{\simeq}}\obj(\mathcal{R})~.

The first equivalence follows from Lemma
7.15
. The second equivalence follows from Definition
7.14
, after Lemma
7.11
and Definition
7.7
. The penultimate equivalence is the adjunction property of induction, and the last equivalence is the inductive hypothesis.
∎

8.
A relative h-principle for embedding-immersions

We now establish an h-principle for embedding-immersions, which was used in the previous section in proving Lemma
7.15
, that the univalent-completion of projection-immersion tangles gives the usual tangle category.

8.1.
Relatives sheaves and h-principles

Definition 8.1
.

Let
M
be a topological space.
For
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
a morphism of presheaves on
\Opens(M)
, then
\mathcal{F}
is a relative sheaf if for every covering sieve
\mathcal{U}\subset\Opens(M)_{/U}=\Opens(U)
the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{V\in\mathcal{U}}{\sf lim}\ \mathcal{F}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{V\in\mathcal{U}}{\sf lim}\ \mathcal{E}(V)}

is a pullback.

Remark 8.2
.

Note that if
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{F}
are both sheaves, then
\mathcal{F}
is automatically a relative sheaf since the horizontal morphisms in Definition
8.1
are both equivalences.

Definition 8.3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\Mfld_{n}^{\partial,\op}\rightarrow\Top
be a continuous functor of topologically-enriched categories. The h-sheafification of
\mathcal{F}
is

\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}:=\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{F}

the homotopy right Kan extension of the restriction of
\mathcal{F}
along
\iota:\Disk_{n}^{\partial,\leq 1,\op}\hookrightarrow\Mfld_{n}^{\partial,\op}
, the inclusion of the full topologically-enriched subcategory consisting of the two objects
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.
Then
\mathcal{F}
adheres to the
h-principle
if the map
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}
is a weak homotopy equivalence.

Remark 8.4
.

For
M
an
n
-manifold without boundary, consider the diagonal quotient (i.e., the Borel construction)

M_{\mathcal{F}}:={\sf Fr}(M)\underset{\mathsf{O}(n)}{\times}\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

where
{\sf Fr}(M)
is the frame bundle of
M
. This can alternatively be constructed as the unstraightening of the functor

M\simeq\Disk^{\leq 1,\op}_{n/M}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}\Top\rightarrow\Spaces~.

There is then a natural homotopy equivalence with the space of sections

\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}(M)\simeq\Gamma\bigl(M_{\mathcal{F}}\rightarrow M\bigr)~.

Given a choice of Riemannian metric on
M
, the natural map
\mathcal{F}(M)\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}(M)
is then equivalent to the scanning map of Segal
[
Se
]
.

Definition 8.5
.

For
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
a relative sheaf in the sense of Definition
8.1
, then
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
adheres to the
relative h-principle
if the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}\mathcal{E}}

is a homotopy pullback.

Recall the following standard notion, flexibility.

Definition 8.6
.

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}
of topological spaces on
M
is
flexible
if the restriction map
\mathcal{F}(V)\to\mathcal{F}(U)
is a Serre fibration for every pair of
U\subset V
of subsets of
M
that satisfy the following condition:

Both
U\subset M
and
V\subset M
are compact codimension-0 submanifolds of
M
with boundary.
Furthermore,
U
is contained in the point-set interior of
V
.

Example 8.7
.

Let
N
be a manifold with boundary.

•

The functor of boundary-preserving smooth maps
\Map(-,N)
, endowed with the compact-open smooth topology (also known as the weak
C^{\infty}
Whitney topology), is a flexible sheaf.

•

The functor of boundary-preserving embeddings
\Emb(-,N)
is a flexible presheaf, by the parametrized isotopy extension theorem.

•

The functor of boundary-preserving immersions
\Imm(-,N)
is a flexible sheaf on open manifolds. This is the main theorem of Smale–Hirsch immersion theory.

The essential importance of flexibility for sheaves is reflected in the following standard proposition, which effectively dates to Smale–Hirsch’s h-principle for immersions, if not before.

Proposition 8.8
.

For
\mathcal{F}
a flexible sheaf on
\Mfld_{n}^{\partial}
, then the natural map
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}
is an equivalence. That is, a flexible sheaf adheres to the h-principle.

Proof sketch.

By induction on
0\leq k\leq n
, the maps
\mathcal{F}(S^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{\nk})\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}(S^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{\nk})
and
\mathcal{F}(S^{k}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{\nk})\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}(S^{k}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{\nk})
are weak homotopy equivalences. By induction on the index of a handle presentation of
M
, the map
\mathcal{F}(M)\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}(M)
is then a weak homotopy equivalence, for all
M
.
∎

Observation 8.9
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a flexible presheaf on
n
-manifolds with boundary. If
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a map of presheaves over
\mathcal{E}
, and both
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
adhere to the relative h-principle, then
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a weak homotopy equivalence if and only if the values
\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\geq 0})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\geq 0})
are equivalences.

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
be a relative sheaf, where
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{E}
are flexible presheaves on
M
. Then
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
adheres to the relative h-principle on
M
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}
denote the functor assigning to
U\subset M
the homotopy fiber product of the diagram
\mathcal{E}(U)\rightarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{E}(U)\leftarrow\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}(U)
. Note that we can reformulate the relative h-principle as the condition that the natural transformation
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}
is an equivalence. Note that
\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}
has the following homotopy sheaf property relative to
\mathcal{E}
: For any codimension-0 submanifolds
U,V\subset M
with boundary, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}(U)\underset{\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)}

is a homotopy pullback. To show that that any map
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}
is a weak equivalence, it therefore suffices to show

(1)

\mathcal{F}(D)\rightarrow\mathsf{D}_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{F}(D)
is a weak equivalence for
D\in\Disk_{n/M}^{\partial,\leq 1}
;

(2)

For all
U,V\in\Mfld_{n/M}^{\partial}
, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U)\underset{\mathcal{F}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{F}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)}

is a homotopy pullback.

The first equivalence is immediate by construction of
\mathsf{D}
, as homotopy right Kan extension from the full subcategory
\Disk_{n/M}^{\partial,\leq 1}
. We now show the second condition. It suffices to show that for every
U
and
V
compact submanifolds of
M
with boundary, then
\mathcal{F}(U\cup V)
, which is the limit of the diagram of topological spaces

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U\cap V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)~,}

agrees with the homotopy limit of this diagram. The homotopy limit is equivalent to the ordinary limit of

(52)

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(U)^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(V)\underset{\mathcal{E}(V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U\cap V)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}

since every map in this diagram is a Serre fibration.
Using that
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{E}
is a relative sheaf, the canonical map

\mathcal{F}(U\cup V)\cong\mathcal{E}(U\cup V)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)}{\times}\Bigl(\mathcal{F}(U)\underset{\mathcal{F}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{F}(V)\Bigr)~.

is a homeomorphism.
Therefore, the ordinary limit of (
52
) is then homeomorphic to the ordinary limit of the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(U\cup V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)^{[0,1]}\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)^{[0,1]}}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)^{[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}(U)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)}
\textstyle{~.}

The map
\mathcal{E}(U)^{[0,1]}\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)^{[0,1]}}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)^{[0,1]}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}(U)\underset{\mathcal{E}(U\cap V)}{\times}\mathcal{E}(V)
is an acyclic fibration, so therefore this limit is homotopy equivalent to
\mathcal{F}(U\cup V)
.
∎

Gromov’s Theorem, that a diffeomorphism-invariant microflexible sheaf on open manifolds adheres to the h-principle
[
Gr
]
, admits a relative formulation.

Theorem 8.11
.

Let
E
be a manifold with boundary.
Consider the presheaf
\Map(-,E)\colon\Mfld_{n}^{\partial,\op}\to\Top
of smooth boundary-preserving maps to
E
.
Assume the following:

•

\mathcal{E}\subset\Map(-,E)
is an open subpresheaf which is flexible on open
n
-manifolds.

•

\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{E}
is an open relative sheaf.

Then
\mathcal{F}
is a flexible presheaf on open manifolds, and
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
adheres to the relative h-principle on open
n
-manifolds.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.10
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{F}
is a flexible presheaf on open manifolds.
Let
U\hookrightarrow V
be an inclusion of compact codimension-0 submanifolds of
M
with boundary, such that
U
is contained in the point-set interior of
V
.
Let
C:=V\smallsetminus{\sf Int}(U)
be the complement in
V
of the point-set interior of
U
.
Regard
C
as an
n
-dimensional cobordism from
\partial U
to
\partial V
.
Choose a finite handle-presentation of
C
.
Using the assumption that
M
is an open
n
-manifold,
we can assume all handles in this handle-presentation of
C
have handle-index strictly less than
n
.
Choose a linear order on the finite set of handles.
Denote by
D_{i}
the
i^{th}
handle of this handle-presentation of
C
, and by
A_{i}\subset D_{i}
its site of handle-attachment.
So, if
D_{i}
has index
k
, there are isomorphisms
D_{i}\cong\mathbb{D}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n\text{-}k}
and
A_{i}\cong(S^{k\text{-}1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\times\mathbb{D}^{n\text{-}k}
.
Consider the filtration
U=H_{0}\subset H_{1}\subset\dots\subset H_{r}=V
where
H_{i}
is the union of
U
with the first
i
handles of the handle presentation of
C
.
In other words,
H_{i}=H_{i-1}\cup D_{i}
with
H_{i-1}\cap D_{i}=A_{i}
.

By induction on
0\leq i\leq r
, we prove that the restriction map
\mathcal{F}(H_{i})\to\mathcal{F}(U)
is a Serre fibration for all
0\leq i\leq r
.
The case
i=r
establishes that the restriction map
\mathcal{F}(V)\to\mathcal{F}(U)
is a Serre fibration, as desired.
Note the factorization of the restriction map through other restriction maps:

\mathcal{F}(H_{i})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(H_{i-1})\longrightarrow\dots\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(H_{1})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(U)~.

Using that the composition of Serre fibrations is again a Serre fibration, we are reduced to proving the restriction map
\mathcal{F}(H_{i})\to\mathcal{F}(H_{i-1})
is a Serre fibration.
Recall the identities
H_{i}=H_{i-1}\cup D_{i}
with
H_{i-1}\cap D_{i}=A_{i}
.
The restriction map

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~.

The assumption that
\mathcal{E}
is a flexible presheaf ensures each of the maps in the lower square are Serre fibrations.
The assumption that
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{E}
is a relative sheaf implies this cube is a limit.
Consequently, the restriction map
\mathcal{F}(H_{i})\to\mathcal{F}(H_{i-1})
is a Serre fibration provided the restriction map
\mathcal{F}(D_{i})\to\mathcal{F}(A_{i})
is a Serre fibration.
Through the identifications
D_{i}\cong\mathbb{D}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n\text{-}k}
and
A_{i}\cong(S^{k\text{-}1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\times\mathbb{D}^{n\text{-}k}
, we are therefore reduced to showing the restriction map

\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{D}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{n\text{-}k})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}((S^{k\text{-}1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1})\times\mathbb{D}^{n\text{-}k})

is a Serre fibration for all
0\leq k<n
. This is exactly as in Proposition 3
[
Hae
]
(which is further elucidated in Figure 3.5 in the proof of Proposition 3.14
[
Ge
]
). This gives that the maps

\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{D}^{k}\times\mathbb{D}^{\nk})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(S^{{\mathit{k}\text{-}1}}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\times\mathbb{D}^{\nk})

are Serre fibrations for
k<n
.
∎

8.2.
Embedding-immersions

In this section, we fix a smooth fiber bundle
\pi:E\rightarrow N
, where
E
and
N
are manifolds with boundary and
\pi
preserves boundary.

Definition 8.12
.

For
M
a smooth manifold, an
embedding-immersion
of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is a boundary-preserving embedding
M\hookrightarrow E
with the property that the composite map
M\rightarrow E\rightarrow N
is an immersion. The topological space of embedding-immersions of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is the subspace

\EmbImm\bigl(M,E\rightarrow N\bigr)\subset\Emb(M,E)

consisting of embedding-immersions. An
immersion-immersion
of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is a boundary-preserving immersion
M\hookrightarrow E
with the property that the composite map
M\rightarrow E\rightarrow N
is an immersion. The topological space of immersion-immersions of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is the subspace

\ImmImm\bigl(M,E\rightarrow N\bigr)\subset\Imm(M,E)

consisting of immersion-immersions.

Definition 8.13
.

Let
M
be a manifold with boundary. The space of
formal immersions
of
M
into
N
is the pullback

\textstyle{\fImm(M,N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\sf Vect}^{\sf inj}(\mathsf{T}M,\mathsf{T}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\sf Vect}^{\sf inj}(\mathsf{T}\partial M,\mathsf{T}\partial N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\sf Vect}^{\sf inj}(\mathsf{T}M_{|\partial M},\mathsf{T}N_{|\partial N})~.}

Definition 8.14
.

Let
M
be a manifold with boundary. The space of formal immersions of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is the subspace

\fImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\subset\fImm(M,E)

consisting of those
\mathsf{T}M\rightarrow\mathsf{T}E
for which the both of the composites
\mathsf{T}M\rightarrow\mathsf{T}E\rightarrow\mathsf{T}N
and
\mathsf{T}\partial M\to\mathsf{T}\partial E\to\mathsf{T}\partial N
are injective.

Definition 8.15
.

Let
\pi:E\rightarrow N
be a smooth fiber bundle, where
E
and
N
are manifolds with boundary, and
\pi
is a boundary-preserving map. For
M
a smooth manifold, the space of embedding-formal-immersions of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is the homotopy pullback

\textstyle{\EmbfImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\fImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb(M,E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\fImm(M,E)~.}

The space of immersion-formal-immersions of
M
into
E\rightarrow N
is the homotopy pullback

\textstyle{\ImmfImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\fImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Imm(M,E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\fImm(M,E)~.}

Proposition 8.16
.

The natural maps
\Emb(-,-)\rightarrow\Imm(-,-)\rightarrow\fImm(-,-)
induce equivalences

\mathsf{D}\Imm(-,N)\simeq\fImm(-,N)~,

\mathsf{D}\Emb(-,E)\simeq\fImm(-,E)~,

\mathsf{D}\EmbfImm(-,E\rightarrow N)\simeq\fImm(-,E\rightarrow N)~.

Proof.

It is sufficient to observe the following criterion for h-sheafification, for
\mathcal{F}
a presheaf of topological spaces on
\Mfld_{n}^{\partial}
: A natural transformation
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
exhibits
\mathcal{G}
as the h-sheafification
\mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}
if: first,
\mathcal{G}
is a homotopy sheaf; and second, values
\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\geq 0})\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\geq 0}
are homotopy equivalences. The result then follows from the observation first that
\fImm(-,E)
is a homotopy sheaf for all
E
, and second the natural equivalences
\Emb(\mathbb{R}^{n},-)\simeq\Imm(\mathbb{R}^{n},-)\simeq\fImm(\mathbb{R}^{n},-)
and
\Emb(\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n},-)\simeq\Imm(\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n},-)\simeq\fImm(\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n},-)
.
∎

Corollary 8.17
.

Let
\pi:E\rightarrow N
a smooth fiber bundle, where
E
and
N
are manifolds with boundary, and
\pi
is a boundary-preserving map.
Then the presheaf of embedding-formal-immersions
\EmbfImm(-,E\rightarrow N)
adheres to the h-principle relative to the presheaf of embeddings
\Emb(-,E)
, in the sense of Definition
8.5
.

Proof.

We must prove that for every smooth manifold with boundary
M
, the diagram

\textstyle{\EmbfImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}\EmbfImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Emb(M,E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}\Emb(M,E)}

is a homotopy pullback. This follows immediately from the definition and Proposition
8.16
.
∎

The following is our application of the relative form of Gromov’s h-principle.

Theorem 8.18
.

Let
\pi:E\rightarrow N
be a smooth fiber bundle, where
E
and
N
are manifolds with boundary, and
\pi
is a boundary-preserving map.
Let
M
be an open manifold. The natural map

\EmbImm(M,E\rightarrow N)\longrightarrow\EmbfImm(M,E\rightarrow N)

is a weak homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

We apply Theorem
8.11
to the case
\mathcal{E}=\Emb(-,E)
and
\mathcal{F}=\EmbImm(-,E\rightarrow N)
. Note that the conditions are satisfied:
\Emb(M,E)
is open in
\Map(M,E)
and is flexible by the parametrized isotopy extension theorem. Likewise,
\EmbImm(M,E\rightarrow N)
is open in
\Emb(M,E)
, and
\EmbImm(-,E\rightarrow N)
is a sheaf relative to
\Emb(-,E)
, since an embedding
M\hookrightarrow E
being an embedding-immersion is a local condition on
M
.
∎

The Smale–Hirsch h-principle for immersions
\Imm(M,N)
holds both if the source
M
is an open manifold, and also if the dimension of the target
N
is strictly greater than that of the source. The same is true for embedding-immersions, as we show in the next proposition, and the proof is identical.

Proposition 8.19
.

Let
W
be a
k
-manifold with boundary, and let
E\xrightarrow{\pi}N
be a boundary-preserving smooth fiber bundle in which
N
is an
n
-manifold with boundary. Assume further that the inequality
k<n
holds.
The map

\EmbImm(W,E\rightarrow N)\longrightarrow\EmbfImm(W,E\rightarrow N)

is a weak homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

Observe the commutative diagram among topological spaces

(53)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{[V]}{\coprod}\EmbImm(V,E\rightarrow N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{[V]}{\coprod}\EmbfImm(V,E\rightarrow N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\EmbImm(W,E\rightarrow N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\EmbfImm(W,E\rightarrow N)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{D}_{V})_{[V]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}_{W}}

in which the coproducts are indexed by isomorphism classes of rank-
(\nk)
vector bundles over
W
, and the downward maps are restrictions along the zero-sections.
Theorem
8.18
implies the top horizontal map is a weak homotopy equivalence.
Therefore, to prove
\mathsf{D}_{W}
is a weak homotopy equivalence, it suffices to prove the following two assertions.

(1)

The map
\mathsf{D}_{W}
is surjective on path-components.

(2)

For each
(W\xrightarrow{f}E)\in\EmbImm(W,E\to N)
, and each isomorphism class
[V]
of rank-
(\nk)
vector bundles over
W
, the map
\mathsf{D}_{V}
induces a weak homotopy equivalence between vertical homotopy fibers over
f
.

To prove (1),
we first fix
(W\xrightarrow{f}E)\in\EmbfImm(W,E\to N)
.
Consider the normal bundle of the formal immersion
W\xrightarrow{\pi f}N
: It is a rank-
(\nk)
vector bundle
V
over
W
fitting into a short exact sequence of vector bundles over
W
:

0\longrightarrow\mathsf{T}W\longrightarrow(\pi f)^{\ast}\mathsf{T}N\longrightarrow V\longrightarrow 0~.

Choose a splitting of this short exact sequence, along with a splitting of the short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\Ker(\mathsf{D}\pi)\rightarrow\mathsf{T}E\rightarrow\pi^{\ast}\mathsf{T}N\rightarrow 0
.
These choices determine a linear embedding of
V
into the normal bundle of the embedding
W\xrightarrow{f}E
.
Applying the tubular neighborhood theorem to
f
, we have an embedding
V\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}E
.
By construction, the composite
V\xrightarrow{\pi\widetilde{f}}N
is an immersion extending
W\xrightarrow{\pi f}N
and witnessing the normal bundle of
\pi f
.
We conclude the right vertical map is surjective on path-components.
Theorem
8.18
implies the top horizontal map is surjective on path-components.
From the 2-of-3 property of surjections among sets, we conclude that the bottom horizontal map is surjective on path-components, as desired.

We now prove (2).
Fix
(W\xrightarrow{f}E)\in\EmbImm(W,E\to N)
.
Let
[V]
be a rank-
(\nk)
vector bundle over
W
.
If
V\to W
is not the normal bundle of the immersion
W\xrightarrow{\pi f}N
, then the homotopy fiber of the restriction map
\EmbImm(V,E\to N)\to\EmbImm(W,E\to N)
is empty.
So take
V\to W
to be the normal bundle of
\pi f
.
Denote the respective vertical fibers of (
53
) over
f
as

(54)

\EmbImm_{f}(V,E\to N)\longrightarrow\EmbfImm_{f}(V,E\to N)~.

It follows from the parametrized isotopy extension theorem that the vertical maps in (
53
) are Serre fibrations.
So to prove (2), it is sufficient to prove the map (
54
) is a weak homotopy equivalence.
Consider the topological subspaces

\ImmImm_{f}(V,E\to N)~:=~\left\{V\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}E\in\ImmImm(V,E\to N)\mid\widetilde{f}_{|W}=f\right\}~\subset~\ImmImm(V,E\to N)

and

\ImmfImm_{f}(V,E\to N)~:=~\left\{V\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}E\in\ImmfImm(V,E\to N)\mid\widetilde{f}_{|W}=f\right\}~\subset~\ImmfImm(V,E\to N)~.

Observe the inclusions among topological spaces:

(55)

\textstyle{\EmbImm_{f}(V,E\to N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{e.end2})}
\textstyle{\EmbfImm_{f}(V,E\to N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ImmImm_{f}(V,E\to N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ImmfImm_{f}(V,E\to N).}

to prove the top horizontal map is a weak homotopy equivalence, we prove the three other maps in this diagram are weak homotopy equivalences.
An application of h-principle for immersions of Smale–Hirsch gives that the bottom horizontal map in (
55
) is a weak homotopy equivalence.
Our arguments proving the vertical maps in (
55
) are weak homotopy equivalences are identical, so we only provide that for the left vertical map.

Once and for all, choose a smooth map
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times(0,1]\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with the following properties.

•

For each
t\in(0,1]
, the map
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\rho_{t}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is an open embedding.

•

The map
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\rho_{1}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is the identity map.

•

For each
0<t<t^{\prime}\leq 1
, there is containment of the closure
\overline{{\sf Image}(\rho_{t})}\subset{\sf Image}(\rho_{t^{\prime}})
.

•

For each open neighborhood
\{0\}\subset O\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, there exists
t\in(0,1]
such that
{\sf Image}(\rho_{t})\subset O
.

•

\rho
restricts as the projection map near
\{0\}\times(0,1]\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times(0,1]
.

Once and for all, choose an inner product on the vector bundle
V\to W
.
Consider the smooth map

\varphi\colon V\times(0,1]\longrightarrow V~,\qquad(v,t)\longmapsto\varphi_{t}(v):=\rho_{t}(\lVert v\rVert^{2})\cdot v~,

given by scaling by
\rho
.

Let
K
be a compact manifold with boundary.
Consider a pair of horizontal maps fitting into a solid diagram among topological spaces:

(56)

\textstyle{\partial K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{0}}
\textstyle{\EmbImm_{f}(V,E\to N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\ImmImm_{f}(V,E\to N).}

To prove the right vertical map in this diagram is a weak homotopy equivalence it is sufficient to construct a filler, up to homotopy, in this diagram.
By smooth approximation, we can homotope
F
so that it is adjoint to a smooth map
K\times V\xrightarrow{F}E
with the property that, for each
k\in K
, the restriction
V\xrightarrow{F_{|\{k\}\times V}}E
belongs to
\ImmImm_{f}(V,E\to N)
, and, for each
k\in\partial K
, the restriction
V\xrightarrow{F_{|\{k\}\times V}}E
belongs to
\EmbImm_{f}(V,E\to N)
.
Now, the inverse function theorem implies there exists an open neighborhood of
K\times W\subset K\times V
on which
F
is an embedding.
Using that
K
is compact, the tube lemma ensures the existence of
\epsilon\in(0,1]
such that the composite map
K\times V\xrightarrow{K\times\varphi_{\epsilon}}K\times V\xrightarrow{F}E
is an embedding.
Observe the following facts about this composite
F\circ(K\times\varphi_{\epsilon})
,
all of which follow from the construction of
\varphi_{t}
.

•

There is membership
F\circ(K\times\varphi_{\epsilon})\in\EmbImm_{f}(V,E\to N)
.

•

The family
F\circ(K\times\varphi_{t})
defines a path in
\ImmImm_{f}(V,E\to N)
between
F\circ(K\times\varphi_{\epsilon})
and
F
.

•

The family
F\circ(\partial K\times\varphi_{t})
defines a path in
\EmbImm_{f}(V,E\to N)
between
F\circ(\partial K\times\varphi_{\epsilon})
and
F_{0}
.

The adjoint of the map
K\times V\xrightarrow{F\circ(K\times\varphi_{\epsilon})}E
supplies the desired homotopy filler
\widetilde{F}
in (
56
).

∎

In the proof of the Tangle Hypothesis, we make use of a modification of the preceding result in which framings (in particular, trivializations of a Gauss map) are added. Let
W
be a
k
-manifold with boundary.
Let
E\xrightarrow{\pi}N
be a boundary-preserving smooth fiber bundle in which
N
is a framed
n
-manifold with boundary.
The framing of
N
determines map

\EmbImm(W,E\rightarrow N)\longrightarrow\Map(W,\Gr_{k}(n))~,\qquad(W\xrightarrow{f}E)\longmapsto\left(W\xrightarrow{~{\sf Gauss}_{\pi f}~}\Gr_{k}(n)\right)~.

Denote the homotopy fiber of this map over the constant map
W\xrightarrow{\ast}\Gr_{1}(n)
as

\EmbImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)~.

Note that a point in
\EmbImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)
is an embedding-immersion
W\xrightarrow{f}E\xrightarrow{\pi}N
together with a nullhomotopy of the Gauss map
W\xrightarrow{{\sf Gauss}_{\pi f}}\Gr_{k}(n)
.
Likewise, the framing of
N
determines map

\EmbfImm(W,E\rightarrow N)\longrightarrow\Map(W,\Gr_{k}(n))~,\qquad(W\xrightarrow{f}E)\longmapsto\left(W\xrightarrow{~{\sf Gauss}_{\pi f}~}\Gr_{k}(n)\right)~.

Denote the homotopy fiber of this map over the constant map
W\xrightarrow{\ast}\Gr_{1}(n)
as

\EmbfImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)~.

We have the following corollary of Proposition
8.19
.

Corollary 8.20
.

Let
\pi:E\rightarrow N
be a smooth fiber bundle, where
E
and
N
are manifolds with boundary,
\pi
is a boundary-preserving map, and
N
is framed
n
-manifold.
Let
W
be a compact
k
-dimensional manifold with boundary, with
k<n
. The natural map

\EmbImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)\longrightarrow\EmbfImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)

is a weak homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

Consider the map of homotopy fiber sequences with base
\Map(W,\Gr_{k}(n))
:

\textstyle{\EmbImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\EmbfImm^{\fr}(W,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\EmbImm(W,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\EmbfImm(W,E\rightarrow N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map(W,\Gr_{k}(n))~.}

Since the bottom horizontal map is a weak homotopy equivalence by Proposition
8.19
, the top horizontal map is likewise a weak homotopy equivalence.
∎

9.
The 1-dimensional Cobordism Hypothesis

Taking products with
\mathbb{R}
defines a functor

\mathbb{R}\times-\colon\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[n,n+1]}^{\sfr}~,\qquad D\mapsto\mathbb{R}\times D~.

This functor carries
\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and carries closed covers to closed covers.
This functor determines a natural transformation between representable functors:

From Definition
2.20
of the tangle
(\infty,1)
-categories, this natural transformation defines a
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal functor between flagged
(\infty,1)
-categories:

(57)

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{n})~.

Explicitly, this functor is given on spaces of objects by

\left(W\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\right)\longmapsto\left(\{0\}\times W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)

and on spaces of morphisms by

\left(W\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)\longmapsto\left(\{0\}\times W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)~.

Ignoring monoidal structures, the functors (
57
) assemble as a diagram among flagged
(\infty,1)
-categories:

(58)

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{0})\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{1})\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\longrightarrow\dots~.

The contractibility of
\Emb(W,\mathbb{R}^{\oo})
justifies the following definition.

Definition 9.1
.

The
flagged
(\oo,1)
-category of
1
-framed cobordisms
is the sequential colimit

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}:=\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})~.

Proposition 9.2
.

The flagged
(\infty,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
admits the following description.

(1)

The space of objects

\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~\simeq~\left|\left\{(S,\sigma)\right\}\right|~\simeq~\underset{r_{+},r_{-}\geq 0}{\coprod}\mathsf{B}\Sigma_{r_{+}}\times\mathsf{B}\Sigma_{r_{-}}

is the moduli space of signed finite sets (i.e., finite sets
S
equipped with a map
S\xrightarrow{\sigma}\{\pm\}
).

(2)

The space of morphisms

\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~\simeq~\Bigr|\Bigl\{W\to\mathbb{D}^{1}~,~\varphi\Bigr\}\Bigl|~,

is the moduli space of framed 1-dimensional cobordisms, which is the space of the following data:

•

a compact smooth
1
-manifold
W
with boundary;

•

a smooth map
W\to\mathbb{D}^{1}
such that the boundary of
W
is the preimage of the boundary of
\mathbb{D}^{1}
;

•

a framing
\mathsf{T}W\overset{\varphi}{\cong}\epsilon^{1}_{W}
of
W
.

(3)

The source and target maps are given by:

\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})\xrightarrow{~s~}\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~,\qquad(W\to\mathbb{D}^{1},\varphi)\longmapsto(W_{|\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{1}},\varphi_{-})~,

where, for
w\in W_{|\partial_{-}\mathbb{D}^{1}}
the sign
\varphi_{-}(w)
is
+
if and only if the inward-pointing vector at
w
aligns with the framing of
W
at
w
;

\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})\xrightarrow{~t~}\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~,\qquad(W\to\mathbb{D}^{1},\varphi)\longmapsto(W_{|\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{1}},\varphi_{+})~,

where, for
w\in W_{|\partial_{+}\mathbb{D}^{1}}
the sign
\varphi_{+}(w)
is
+
if and only if the outward-pointing vector at
w
aligns with the framing of
W
at
w
.

(4)

The composition map

\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})\underset{\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})}{\times}\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})\longrightarrow\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~,\qquad\left((W\to\mathbb{D}^{1},\varphi),(W^{\prime}\to\mathbb{D}^{1},\varphi^{\prime})\right)\longmapsto(W\cup W^{\prime},\varphi\cup\varphi^{\prime})~,

is given by concatenation of framed cobordisms.

Proof.

Sequential colimits in the
(\infty,1)
-category
\Spaces
commute with finite limits.
Consequently, the fully-faithful inclusion
\fCat_{(\infty,1)}\hookrightarrow\Fun(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Spaces)
preserves sequential colimits.
Using that colimits in functor
(\infty,1)
-categories are computed value-wise, we have that the canonical maps between spaces,

\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}))=\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[0]\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}[0]=\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})

and

\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}))=\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[1]\longrightarrow\Bord_{1}^{\fr}[1]=\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~,

are equivalences.
There are similar identifications for spaces of
p
-simplices.

Consequently, for
p\geq 0
, we have an identification:

\displaystyle\Bord_{1}^{\fr}[p]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}~\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})[p]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}~\Map_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{D}}_{[\n 1,n]}^{\sfr}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1},\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{\rm Obs~\ref{rem.T.pt.BBord}}{~\simeq~}

\displaystyle\underset{n\geq 0}{\colim}~\Bigl|\Bigl\{W\overset{\rm framed}{\underset{{\rm codim\text{-}}(\n 1)}{\hookrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}\times\mathbb{D}[p]\Bigr\}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\underset{\rm project}{\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}}

\displaystyle\Bigr|\Bigl\{W\to\mathbb{D}[p]~,~\varphi\Bigr\}\Bigl|~.

Here, the final term is a moduli space of the following data:

•

for
p>0
, a compact smooth 1-manifold
W
with boundary; for
p=0
, a compact smooth 0-manifold;

•

a smooth boundary-preserving map
W\to\mathbb{D}[p]
that is transverse to
[p]\subset\mathbb{D}[p]
;

•

an injection
\varphi\colon\mathsf{T}W\hookrightarrow\epsilon^{1}_{W}
of the tangent bundle of
W
into the trivial rank-
1
vector bundle over
W
.

The result follows by inspecting these identifications.

∎

Proposition 9.3
.

The flagged
(\infty,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
is univalent-complete.
In other words,
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
is an
(\infty,1)
-category.

Proof.

We must show the canonical map from the space of objects to the space of isomorphisms

(59)

\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})\longrightarrow\Iso(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})

is an equivalence.
It follows from Proposition
9.2
that the subspace

\Iso(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})~\subset~\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})

consists of framed 1-dimensional cobordisms that admit a both a left and a right inverse with respect to concatenation of framed cobordisms.
From the classification of compact 1-manifolds with boundary, any such 1-dimensional cobordism
W
is isomorphic with a finite disjoint union of trivial cobordisms:
W\cong(\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup S}
for some finite set
S
.
Therefore, for
W
a framed 1-dimensional cobordism that admits a left and right inverse with respect to concatenation of framed cobordisms, there is an isomorphism between framed cobordisms
W\cong(\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup(S,\sigma)}
for some signed finite set
S\xrightarrow{\sigma}\{\pm\}
.
We conclude that the map (
59
) is surjective on path-components.
The result is then proved by showing, for each point
(S,\sigma)\in\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})
, the map between spaces of based loops

(60)

\Omega_{(S,\sigma)}\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})\longrightarrow\Omega_{(\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup(S,\sigma)}}\Iso(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})

is an equivalence.

As
\Obj(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})
is the moduli space of signed finite sets, and as
\Mor(\Bord_{1}^{\fr})
is the moduli space of framed 1-dimensional cobordisms (Proposition
9.2
), the map (
60
) can be identified as the canonical map

(61)

\Sigma_{S_{+}}\times\Sigma_{S_{-}}=\Diff^{\fr}(S,\sigma)\xrightarrow{~-\times\mathbb{D}^{1}~}\Diff^{\fr}((\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup(S,\sigma)})~,

where
S_{+}:=\sigma^{-1}\{+\}
and
S_{-}:=\sigma^{-1}\{-\}
.
Every framed diffeomorphism of
(\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup(S,\sigma)}
determines a permutation of its path-components
\pi_{0}\left((\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup(S,\sigma)}\right)=S
, which preserves the partition
S=S_{+}\sqcup S_{-}
.
In this way, taking path-components defines a retraction of the map (
61
), which yields an identification between groups under
\Sigma_{S_{+}}\times\Sigma_{S_{-}}
as a wreath product:

\Diff^{\fr}((\mathbb{D}^{1})^{\sqcup(S,\sigma)})~\simeq~\left(\Sigma_{S_{+}}\times\Sigma_{S_{-}}\right)\wr\Diff^{\fr}(\mathbb{D}^{1})~.

To finish, the group of framed diffeomorphisms of
\mathbb{D}^{1}
is contractible.
Therefore, the map (
61
) is an equivalence, as desired.

∎

9.1.
Symmetric monoidal structure of
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}

We present a formalism for constructing symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories from sequences of compatible
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories.
This is much like constructing an
\infty
-loop space from a pre-spectrum.
We then apply this construction to endow the
(\infty,1)
-category
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
with a symmetric monoidal structure.

For each dimension
k
consider the symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
\Disk_{k}^{\fr}
of finite disjoint unions of framed
k
-dimensional vector spaces and framed open embeddings among them, with symmetric monoidal structure given by disjoint union.
Taking products with Euclidean spaces assembles these symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories into a functor
\Disk_{\bullet}^{\fr}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\Alg_{\sf Com}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})
.
The colimit of this functor is canonically identified as the symmetric monoidal envelope of the commutative
\infty
-operad.
Therefore, for each symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{V}
, applying
\Fun^{\otimes}(-,\mathcal{V})
defines a functor

\Alg_{-}(\mathcal{V})\colon\mathbb{N}^{\op}\to\fCat_{(\infty,1)}

whose limit is
\Alg_{\sf Com}(\mathcal{V})
.
Consider the Cartesian fibration

\Alg_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}

which is the unstraightening of this functor.
There is a fully-faithful functor to the
(\infty,1)
-category of sections of this Cartesian fibration

(62)

\Alg_{\sf Com}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\Bigr)

whose image consists of those sections that carry morphisms to Cartesian morphisms.
Explicitly, an object in the righthand
(\infty,1)
-category is the data of an
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebra
A_{k}
for each
k\geq 0
together with a map of
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-algebras
A_{k}\to(A_{k^{\prime}})_{|\mathcal{E}_{k}}
for each
k\leq k^{\prime}
, coherently compatibly; while an object in the image of (
62
) is one for which each
A_{k}\to(A_{k^{\prime}})_{|\mathcal{E}_{k}}
is an equivalence.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a Cartesian presentable
(\infty,1)
-category, which we regard as a symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category via the Cartesian product.
The functor (
62
) admits a left adjoint that implements a localization.
This left adjoint evaluates on a section
(k\mapsto A_{k})
as the Cartesian section

k~\mapsto~\underset{\ell\geq k}{\colim}(A_{\ell})_{|k}~:=~\colim\Bigl(A_{k}\to(A_{k+1})_{|\mathcal{E}_{k}}\to(A_{k+2})_{|\mathcal{E}_{k}}\to\dots\Bigr)~.

Proof.

The colimit of the functor
\mathbb{N}^{-/}\colon\mathbb{N}^{\op}\to\fCat_{(\infty,1)}
, given by
k\mapsto\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}
, is canonically identified as
\mathbb{N}
.
Therefore, to construct the alleged left adjoint it is enough to construct a downward morphism between functors
\mathbb{N}^{\op}\to\fCat_{(\infty,1)}

\textstyle{\dots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet>k}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{>k}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet\geq k}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dots}
\textstyle{\dots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Alg_{k+1}(\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dots.}

and then argue its properties.
Because, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
the object
(k=k)
is final in
(\mathbb{N}^{k/})^{\op}
, the colimit of the composite functor
\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}^{\op}\to\mathbb{N}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\Alg_{-}(\mathcal{V})}\fCat_{(\infty,1)}

is canonically identified as
\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})
.
There results a functor between
(\infty,1)
-categories

\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet\geq k}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}\Bigr)\xrightarrow{(-)_{|\mathcal{E}_{k}}}\Fun\bigl(\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}^{\op},\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})\bigr).

Postcomposing this functor with the colimit functor
\Fun\bigl(\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}^{\op},\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})\bigr)\xrightarrow{\colim}\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})
defines the functor we seek for each given
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Since the forgetful functor
\Alg_{k}(\mathcal{V})\to\mathcal{V}
preserves and creates filtered colimits, this colimit functor indeed exists.
This also implies that each square in the diagram displayed above canonically commutes.
We conclude a functor between limit
(\infty,1)
-categories

\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\Bigr)\to\Alg_{\sf Com}(\mathcal{V})
.

The functor
(-)_{|\mathcal{E}_{k}}
above carries Cartesian sections to constant functors.
Because
\mathbb{N}
has contractible classifying space, the composite functor

\Alg_{\sf Com}(\mathcal{V})\to\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\Bigr)\to\Alg_{\sf Com}(\mathcal{V})
is canonically identified as the identity functor.
Constructed by way of a colimit, there is a unit transformation from the composite functor

\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\Bigr)\to\Alg_{\sf Com}(\mathcal{V})\to\Gamma\Bigl(\Alg_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\Bigr)

to the identity functor.
Because
\mathbb{N}
is filtered, the restriction of this unit transformation to the Cartesian sections is a natural equivalence.
This completes the proof of the lemma.

∎

The diagram (
58
) defines a section:

(63)

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\Alg_{\bullet}({\fCat_{(\infty,1)}})~,\qquad k\mapsto\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{k})~.

Definition 9.5
.

The symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category of
1
-framed cobordisms

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}~:=~\underset{k\geq 0}{\colim}~\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{k})

is the value of the left adjoint functor of Lemma
9.4
on the section (
63
).

9.2.
Proof of the Cobordism Hypothesis

We now state and prove the main result of this section, the 1-dimensional Cobordism Hypothesis, first proved by Hopkins–Lurie
[
Lu3
]
.

Theorem 9.6

(1-dimensional Cobordism Hypothesis)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a rigid symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category.
Evaluation at the object
\ast\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
defines an equivalence between spaces

\ev_{\ast}\colon\Map_{\Alg_{\sf Com}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr},\mathcal{R}\bigr)\xrightarrow{~\simeq~}\Obj(\mathcal{R})~,\qquad Z\longmapsto Z(\ast)

between
\mathcal{R}
-valued symmetric monoidal functors and the space of objects of
\mathcal{R}
.

Proof.

We use the definition of
\Bord_{n}^{\fr}
as a colimit of tangle categories, and then apply the Tangle Hypothesis given by Theorem
7.16
:

\displaystyle\Map_{\Alg_{\sf Com}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr},\mathcal{R}\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{k\to\infty}{\limit}\Map_{\Alg_{k}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\Bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{k}),\mathcal{R}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{k\to\infty}{\limit}\obj(\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\obj(\mathcal{R})~.

The result follows, since we obtain a constant sequential limit with value
\obj(\mathfrak{X})
.

∎

10.
Invertible field theories and classifying spaces

We recover the expected classification of
invertible
framed topological quantum field theories.
This is phrased in terms of the groupoid completion of the framed cobordism category.

The fully-faithful inclusion
\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)\hookrightarrow\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})
has a left adjoint localization, which is the usual classifying space functor:

(64)

\mathsf{B}\colon\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})~\rightleftarrows~\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)~.

Corollary 10.1
.

There is a canonical identification as
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-spaces,

\mathsf{B}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\bigr)~\simeq~\Omega^{\n 1}S^{\n 1}~,

between the classifying space of the tangle category and the
(\n 1)
-fold loop space of the
(\n 1)
-sphere. In the
n\mapsto\oo
limit, there is a canonical identification as
\mathcal{E}_{\oo}
-spaces,

\mathsf{B}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}\bigr)~\simeq~\Omega^{\oo}\Sigma^{\oo}S^{0}~,

between the classifying space of the bordism category and the zeroth space of the sphere spectrum.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a grouplike
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-space.
Via the fully-faithful inclusion
\Spaces\to\fCat_{(\infty,1)}
, which preserves finite products,
\mathcal{G}
can be regarded as an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal flagged
(\oo,1)
-category.
Regarded as so,
\mathcal{G}
is univalent-complete and rigid, with
\Obj(\mathcal{G})=\mathcal{G}
.
We have the following sequence of canonical identifications:

\displaystyle\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)}\Bigl(\mathsf{B}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\bigr),\mathcal{G}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\oo,1)})}(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\obj(\mathcal{G})=\mathcal{G}

The first equivalence follows from the localization (
64
).
The second equivalence follows from Theorem
7.16
, the Tangle Hypothesis.
Evidently, both of these equivalences are functorial in the argument
\mathcal{G}
.
In this way, we see that
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
corepresents the forgetful functor from
\Alg_{\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}}(\Spaces)
to
\Spaces
. However, by universal properties the forgetful functor is also corepresented by the free grouplike
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-algebra generated by a point, which is
\Omega^{\n 1}S^{\n 1}
.
The desired canonical equivalence between
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-spaces then follows from the Yoneda lemma. The equivalence
\mathsf{B}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}\bigr)~\simeq~\Omega^{\oo}\Sigma^{\oo}S^{0}
follows by identical reasoning.

∎

An immediate consequence of Corollary
10.1
is a classification of invertible 1-dimensional framed topological quantum field theories.
We first introduce the following notation.
Let
0<k\leq\infty
.
For
\mathcal{R}
an
\mathcal{E}_{k}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category, denote by

{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})~\subset~\Obj(\mathcal{R})

the subspace consisting of those objects that are invertible with respect to the monoidal structure.

Corollary 10.2
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{R}
be an
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category.
There is a monomorphism between spaces,

{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})~\hookrightarrow~\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)~.

The image consists of those symmetric monoidal functors that carry each morphism in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
to an isomorphism in
\mathcal{R}
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a symmetric monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category.
There is a monomorphism between spaces,

{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})~\hookrightarrow~\Map_{\Alg_{\sf Com}(\Cat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr},\mathcal{R}\bigr)~.

The image consists of those symmetric monoidal functors that carry each morphism in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}
to an isomorphism in
\mathcal{R}
.

Proof.

We prove the first statement; a proof of the second statement is logically identical.

Consider the subspace

\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)^{\sf invbl}~\subset~\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)

consisting of those symmetric monoidal functors that carry each morphism in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
to an isomorphism in
\mathcal{R}
.
By definition of the classifying space functor as a left adjoint in (
64
), we identify this subspace:

\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)^{\sf invbl}~\simeq~\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)}\bigl(\mathsf{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\Obj(\mathcal{R})\bigr)~.

Now, let
(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\xrightarrow{Z}\mathcal{R})\in\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)^{\sf invbl}
.
Let
(S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
be an object.
Using that
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
is rigid, the value
Z(S)\in\mathcal{R}
is dualizable.
Furthermore, for
S^{\vee}\in\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
the dual of this object, the value of
Z
on the unit and counit in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})

witness
Z(S^{\vee})
as the dual in
\mathcal{R}
of
Z(S)
.
Because
Z
is assumed to carry all morphisms in
\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})
to isomorphisms in
\mathcal{R}
, the value of
Z
on this unit and counit are isomorphisms in
\mathcal{R}
.
Therefore, these dualizing data witness the value
Z(S)\in\mathcal{R}
as invertible with respect to the monoidal structure of
\mathcal{R}
.
Therefore, the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal functor
Z
factors:

\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1})\xrightarrow{~Z~}\Pic(\mathcal{R})~\subset~\Obj(\mathcal{R})~.

We conclude a further identification:

\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)^{\sf invbl}~\simeq~\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)}\bigl(\mathsf{B}\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})\bigr)~.

By construction, the
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-monoidal space
{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})
is grouplike.
So, from Corollary
10.1
we achieve a further identification:

\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\fCat_{(\infty,1)})}\bigl(\Bord_{1}^{\fr}(\mathbb{R}^{\n 1}),\mathcal{R}\bigr)^{\sf invbl}~\simeq~\Map_{\Alg_{\n 1}(\Spaces)}\bigl(\Omega^{\n 1}S^{\n 1},{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})\bigr)~\simeq~{\sf Pic}(\mathcal{R})~,

in which the final equivalence uses that the grouplike
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-space
\Omega^{\n 1}S^{\n 1}
is the free grouplike
\mathcal{E}_{\n 1}
-space generated by a point.
The result follows.

∎
